# Fighters Through Time agent context

This file applies to the entire repository. It is the durable orientation document for AI agents working on this project. Keep it synchronized with material architecture or scope changes.

`CLAUDE.md` imports this file and adds the local toolchain (Godot executable locations, verified commands), the `docs/` authority map, and Claude Code configuration. Tool-specific setup belongs there; durable project truth belongs here.

## How to use this context

1. Read this file before changing the project.
2. Read the relevant section of `design-godot.md` before implementing gameplay, content, UI, networking, save, or performance behavior. The design document is the full product and technical specification; this file is a working summary and repository map.
3. Inspect the current scripts, scenes, resources, and `project.godot` before editing. The design document describes the target, while the repository contains a partially implemented prototype.
4. Treat explicit user instructions as highest priority. Otherwise, follow the authority rule below.
5. Clearly distinguish `implemented`, `prototype/placeholder`, `planned`, and `deferred` behavior in plans and handoffs.

### The authority rule (P04, adopted 2026-09-13)

**`design-godot.md` and the adopted contracts under `docs/design-contracts/` define *intended behaviour*. Verified code, `.tres` data and scene bindings at a pinned revision describe the *current build* — they are evidence about that build, not authority to reverse an approved target.**

This inverts the pre-Package-11 rule ("if a document and a `.tres` resource disagree, the resource wins"), which quietly promoted every shipped value into the design. Reading a resource proves its stored value, not that the game loads or applies it correctly.

When the two differ: close it by changing the build, or record it in `docs/design-contracts/DESIGN_BUILD_DEVIATIONS.md` — **the live ledger** — with a stable deviation ID, the intended behaviour and its decision reference, the observed value with its pinned commit and file identifier, an owner (`Unassigned` unless claimed), and concrete acceptance criteria. A deviation does not become the new target merely because it shipped, and a record is retained after closure rather than deleted. Where the design deliberately leaves a number provisional, record that fact rather than inventing a requirement.

**The orthogonal rule survives untouched: never create a second canonical value.** Static tuning still lives in exactly one place — a `.tres`, or a single named rulebook constant — and a factory, UI script or document never holds a second copy. "The resource is not the authority" does not mean "copy the number somewhere else".

## Product identity and non-negotiable pillars

`Fighters Through Time` is a 2D game that combines side-scrolling action-adventure gameplay with a platform-fighter mode. The nine playable characters are historical figures whose exaggerated abilities are manifestations of humanity's collective memory, called Temporal Resonance.

Core design contracts:

- An ability must make sense in both modes. The same move used for Story Mode combat or a puzzle should translate naturally to Fighter Mode.
- Combat uses standard HP, not a Smash-style accumulating knockback percentage. At 0 HP a fighter is knocked out. In Fighter Mode, falling through the bottom blast zone also costs one stock; the sides and top are solid boundaries. Three of the ten stages are **Open** (Paris, Vesuvius, Nassau) — they author real pits, so the bottom blast zone is reachable through ordinary play.
- Story progression and the Temporal Resonance Grid affect Story Mode only. Fighter Mode remains competitively normalized.
- **The hero's Temporal Resonance grows across the campaign. There is no mid-campaign power loss; the charge is surrendered exactly once, by choice, at the very end.** (V7.5, Package 11 A6 — the retired power-fade arc is retired for good. Do not reintroduce a "you're losing your power" beat.)
- **The roster will grow. Never hardcode roster size, and never enumerate the cast in load-bearing ways.** `FTT.Core.CharacterRoster`, backed by `resources/Content/content_manifest.csv`, is the single runtime roster; every production consumer reads it. The one documented exception is `FTT.FighterSim.FighterCharacterID` (see the Roster section).
- **The Act III knowledge boundary:** no dialogue line, brief or UI string may state a Villain's-True-Plan fact before Level 13, and Commander Vale is named only from Level 15 on. `tests/ContentValidation/NarrativeKnowledgeBoundaryTests.cs` is the gate.
- All nine characters are available from the start in the initial build. Character unlocking is a later balance/content phase.
- A campaign save locks the selected character for the whole playthrough; there is no mid-campaign character swapping.
- The initial Fighter Mode scope is 1v1 only: local shared-screen/LAN and online rollback. Four-player free-for-all and 2v2 are post-launch work and must not be added during initial development.
- Delay-based online combat is not acceptable. The intended final online implementation is deterministic GGPO-style rollback.
- Initial builds are English-only but must be localization-ready from day one. User-visible strings use translation keys, not hardcoded English.
- Alternate costumes, cosmetic items, full-match replays, detailed post-match stat sheets, online account/profile systems, and Training Mode are outside the initial scope unless the user explicitly reprioritizes them.

## Narrative and mode context

**The V7.5 canon replaced the Unity-era one wholesale (Package 11 A6, 2026-09-13).** The Apex Archive, the Chrono-Resistance, "the cult", "cultists" and the Library-of-Alexandria overload are **retired vocabulary** — `grep -i "Apex Archive|Chrono-Resistance"` over `localization/en.csv` returns nothing, and it must stay that way.

The **Severed** are the antagonist faction (S14, Package 13 — they were "the Unbound" until 2026-09-29; only English values changed). Their mind-bound agents in each era are **the Linked** (S12). Their **First Strike** was aimed at *people, not places*: an extraction beam that takes the pivotal individual out of their moment. **Temporal Resonance is time's self-correcting response** to that theft, which is why the hero has it. The **Wardens** are the player's faction, led by Commander Sarah aboard a Time-Ship that is **cut off**, not hijacked, and that serves as the Story Mode hub; the crew is Sarah, Medic Okafor and **Chief Engineer Wren** (added by Package 13 W3). The **Meridian** is the load-bearing thread the campaign restores. The First Severed is revealed in Level 15 as **Commander Vale**, a Warden who deserted (S46/S47). The three acts are **Unmoored / Hunted / Homecoming**, though no build string shows an act title yet.

**Two different words spell "Unbound".** The faction rename never touches the *input-binding* sense — a deliberately cleared binding slot (`InputBindings.SetUnbound`, `UnboundActions`, `controls_binding_unbound`). The design master's S14 find-and-replace hit that sense by mistake; the repo mirror corrects it (`DOC-MASTER-UNBOUND-BINDING`, CLAUDE.md).

The campaign follows only the player's locked-in historical figure and the Warden crew. Other roster characters appear as **captives** of the First Strike, not as hub NPCs or companions — the `{CaptiveName1}` / `{CaptiveName2}` tokens resolve them at render time. Fighter Mode is narratively framed as sparring in the Time-Ship's Temporal Arena.

`resources/Enemies/chrono_warden.tres` keeps its ID and its "Chrono-Warden" display name deliberately: with the Wardens now the player's faction it reads as **a stolen title, worn mockingly** (the lore note is a comment in the resource). Legacy identifiers are retained everywhere — scene IDs and paths, `CampaignLevel` member names, level IDs, enemy/boss resource IDs (`archive_prime`, `apex_eraser`, `unbound_eraser`, `revolutionary_tribunal`, `iron_chancellor`), code names such as `UnboundBeam` and `UIPalette.UnboundCold`, ability IDs, `PresentationEventID` strings, atlas paths, pool config IDs, dialogue sequence IDs and **every translation key name**. Only English *values*, the non-localized debug `DisplayName` fields and prose changed.

Story Mode consists of a hub plus **sixteen route slots**, levels 0–15. (The per-hero Legacy Level 4A that sat between Levels 4 and 5 was retired by S27 and deleted by Package 13 W2 — see Scene architecture.)

| Level | Era / purpose |
|---|---|
| 0 | Intro and Chronal Integration calibration |
| 1 | Florence, 1503 — Steampunk Renaissance |
| 2 | Orléans, 1429 — Siege of Orléans |
| 3 | Chicago, 1893 — World's Fair |
| 4 | Paris, 1789 — Storming of the Bastille (boss: **the Governor's Guard**, Governor de Launay and his garrison captain) |
| 5 | Titanic, 1912 — Act I finale (the Eraser's debut; Captain Smith is taken as the Tidal Overseer falls) |
| 6 | Pompeii, 79 AD |
| 7 | Nassau, 1715 — Golden Age of Pirates |
| 8 | Alexandria, 30 BC — Cleopatra's palace |
| 9 | **Hakata Bay, 1281** — the Mongol invasion (S32; scene/IDs still `Level_09_Berlin` / `level_09_berlin`) |
| 10 | London, c. 1601 — Globe Theatre |
| 11 | Gettysburg, July 1863 |
| 12 | Lunar Landing, 1969 — Act II finale |
| 13 | Chronal Void transition |
| 14 | **The Severed Bastion** (scene/enum/IDs still `NeoEarth` / `level_14_neo_earth`) |
| 15 | **The Meridian Founding** and the final boss, **The First Severed** (scene/enum/IDs still `Alexandria` / `apex_eraser`) |

`archive_prime` displays as **The Forge Sentinel**; `apex_eraser` as **The First Severed**; `revolutionary_tribunal` as **The Governor's Guard**; `iron_chancellor` as **The Thunderbomb General**, with `infrared_border_sentry` → **Lantern-Eye Archer** and `neural_mech_walker` → **Thunderbomb Engineer** at Hakata Bay; the Level 3 Chronal Inventor is a Linked direct-current rival. The Berlin Fighter stage reads **Hakata Seawall** (hazard **Fleet Lantern**; `FighterStage_Berlin`, `berlin_wall` retained). Every resource ID is retained.

**The Mystery Thread (N03/N04).** Hero-conditional dialogue sequences use the `<baseID>@<heroID>` ID form with a matching `HeroConditionCharacterID`. Since Package 13 W4 the N03 recognitions live in per-level **absence** sequences (`level_02.absence@joan`, `level_03.absence@tesla`, `level_08.absence@cleopatra`, `level_10.absence@shakespeare`, `level_11.absence@lincoln`; the old entrance variants are retired), beside Level 1's `@leonardo` entrance/boss-intro/exit (S20/S28), the exit/post-boss variants, W3's nine `level_00.intro@<hero>` copies and `hub.act1_boundary@mozart`. The every-hero absences (L4 Desmoulins, L6 Pliny) and the `{MissingSoFar}` / `{MissingPlaces}` tokens (`FTT.Core.MissingLegendTokens`, per-hero rows with a fallback) carry the thread for heroes with no shared level. `DialogueSetData.FindSequence(baseID, activeHeroID)` prefers the hero variant and falls back to the base — **never to another hero's variant** — and selects on the *saved* hero ID, never a localized name.

The full level hazards, puzzles, bosses, dialogue, and asset requirements are in Sections 2, 3, 6–10, 16, and 17 of `design-godot.md`.

## Roster

The roster is nine today:

| ID | Character | Intended combat identity |
|---|---|---|
| `einstein` | Albert Einstein | Zoner/setup; spacetime and gravity |
| `joan` | Joan of Arc | Beginner-friendly melee rushdown; radiant hyper-armor |
| `leonardo` | Leonardo da Vinci | Gadget/hybrid; inventions and persistent turret |
| `lincoln` | Abraham Lincoln | Heavy/juggernaut; rail strikes and shockwaves |
| `cleopatra` | Cleopatra | High-skill trapper/puppeteer; sand and serpents |
| `tesla` | Nikola Tesla | Ranged setup; linked coils and electricity |
| `shakespeare` | William Shakespeare | Summoner/puppeteer; barriers and spectral actors |
| `mozart` | Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart | Ranged/tempo; sonic waves and musical platforms |
| `tubman` | Harriet Tubman | Guide / counter; North Star guidance, coded calls, foresight counters |

**Harriet Tubman replaced Pocahontas** (character review P01, Package 13 W5, 2026-09-29). Pocahontas is removed from the build — data, kit, Vine Snare construct, grid, art and every `pocahontas` translation row — and survives only as dead save data and reserved ordinals (below). Tubman's kit: **Conductor's Call** (a 5-unit ground rush of spectral scouts, grounded targets only, 18 dmg, 9 s), **Foresight** (a 20-frame counter stance that nullifies one strike or projectile and answers within 2.5 u for 20, 10 s), **North Star Leap** (a guided 8-way 4-unit leap with a 0.5-unit extended ledge snap), **The Freedom Line** (76 = 7 × 8 + 20, a straight 6-unit lantern-beam activation). Her grid is **The Crossing** (Pocahontas's shape and prices; Star Guide traversal; Safe Passage, Never Lost a Passenger, **North Star Ward**). Her presentation was D5 placeholder art generated by the in-repo tooling (`tools/asset_src/tubman/`), original rather than derived from Pocahontas; since the 2026-10-18 retro v2 sprite pass she has retro-pulp sprites, ability effects and a portrait like the rest of the roster (an agent design, ledger `PROVISIONAL-TUBMAN-RETRO-DESIGN`). Nobody has looked at either.

Canonical kits, talent grids, and tuning live in Section 5 of `design-godot.md` and in the A15 contract `docs/design-contracts/ABILITY_DATA.md`. Current baseline character stats live in `resources/Characters/*_data.tres`, which since Package 11 A6b also carry the three `Placeholder{Body,Accent,Detail}Color` presentation exports — `CharacterFactory` no longer holds a colour table.

**`FTT.Core.CharacterRoster` is the single runtime roster** (Package 11 A6b), backed by `content_manifest.csv`'s `Character` rows in authored grid order and cached for the process lifetime. `MainMenu`, `CharacterSelectScreen`, `HolodeckConsolePanel`, `AbilityVisualLibrary`, `CampaignCaptiveRoster` and the global unlock grant all read it; the menu tile binds defensively, so a roster larger than an authored grid degrades instead of throwing. **It requires the Godot runtime** — see the engine-free-payload rule under coding conventions.

**`DEFER-ROSTER-ENUM`.** `FTT.FighterSim.FighterCharacterID` is the one documented exception, and it is **append-only**: a new member takes the next ordinal, no member is reordered, renumbered or reused, and `zoneTypeID = (int)id * 10 + slot` must stay unique. The roster swap exercised it: **`Tubman = 9` is appended and `Pocahontas = 8` stays as an `[Obsolete]` reserved ordinal**; Pocahontas's persistent-object type 4 (`vine_snare`), zone type 83 and kit phases 19–20 are retired and never reused, and the protocol moved to **v4**. Its ordinals serialize into snapshots and the network protocol, so a tenth character is a protocol-affecting change that needs a rollback and protocol pass. Recorded in the deviation ledger.

**The Legacy Unlock Schedule (V7.5, Story-only).** Ability slots are restored by campaign milestone, never by dust: Movement Ability after Level 1, Special 1 after Level 2, Special 2 after Level 3, the Ultimate **cast** after Level 4 — so Level 5 is the first full-kit level now that Level 4A is gone. `FTT.Core.LegacyUnlockSchedule` owns the table and the persisted slot keys (`movement` / `special1` / `special2` / `ultimate` — a serialization contract); `PlayerController.IsAbilityUnlocked(AbilitySlot)` is the gate, consulted at the four cast sites and installed by `CharacterFactory` on the `applyStoryProgression: true` path only. Basics, Block, Rally, the meter, the death rewind, Time Freeze and Defy History are **never** gated. Locked slots are shown, never hidden (`EventBus.OnAbilitySlotLockChanged`, states `Dormant | Suppressed | Clear`), and the Story Move List badges an unrestored slot with `movelist_dormant_until`. The 975-dust grid economy is untouched.

The design document mentions `character_base_stats.md` and `ability_numeric_data.md`. Those were Unity-era numeric proposals and were removed from `docs/` on 2026-08-06 along with the rest of the pre-Godot archive; copies survive outside the repository in `D:\Projects\fighters-through-time-docs-2\`. Do not treat them as authority or reintroduce them: they assume Unity `ScriptableObject` fields and a ten-character roster including Stephen Hawking, who is not in the current nine. `design-godot.md` Section 5 and the `resources/` `.tres` files are canonical.

## Technology and runtime targets

- Engine in the current repository: Godot .NET 4.7.1 (`Godot.NET.Sdk/4.7.1`). The GDD's minimum is Godot 4.4+.
- Language/runtime: C# on .NET 10 (`net10.0`, SDK 10.0.302 pinned by `global.json`). Root namespace: `FTT`. Migrated from .NET 8 on 2026-08-06; Godot 4.7.1 loads the `net10.0` assembly and the full suite passes. `design-godot.md` Section 12 still says .NET 8 — the project file is authoritative.
- Current package/runtime dependencies: `K4os.Compression.LZ4` 1.3.8, Klotho's `Newtonsoft.Json` 13.0.4 and `LiteNetLib` 2.1.4 runtime dependencies, plus Debug-only GdUnit4 test packages.
- Current renderer setting: `gl_compatibility`, using Godot's 2D `CanvasItem` stack. The intended art pipeline uses `Light2D`, `CanvasModulate`, `LightOccluder2D`, normal maps, and custom `CanvasItem` shaders.
- Target platforms: Windows and Linux/SteamOS, with Steam Deck performance in mind. macOS was cut from launch by the 2026-09-26 design decision G03.
- Simulation and presentation target: locked 60 FPS / 60 Hz physics.
- Reference resolution: 1920×1080. `project.godot` and `ViewportEnforcer` use fixed `keep` aspect behavior with black letterbox/pillarbox clear areas.
- Performance target: 16.67 ms per frame, no gameplay-time GC spikes, at most 4 GB runtime RAM, roughly 150 draw batches, 150,000 visible vertices, 60 active `AnimatedSprite2D` nodes, and 500 active particles. Pool short-lived gameplay objects.

### Dependency status

Klotho v0.6.1 and GdUnit4 v6.2.0 are pinned under `addons/`. Klotho is experimental; the fixed-point simulation and rollback foundation are implemented, and the Package 6 rollback-readiness gate (convergence, bounded history, worst-case depth-7 resimulation) passes across all nine kits and all ten stages in the .NET test host — but that is a single Windows Debug machine with an in-memory transport, so the platform and real-transport gates are still open. Steam Networking Sockets and Steam transport are not installed. Do not describe the existing networking path as production netcode.

## Scene architecture and flow

- Main scene: `res://scenes/menus/MainMenu.tscn`.
- **Temporary developer level select (2026-08-15, debug builds only).** The main menu's root screen carries a `LevelSelectButton` (`menu_level_select`, visible when `MainMenu.ShowDeveloperLevelSelect` — `OS.IsDebugBuild()` with an `internal static bool?` test override) that runs character → a **sixteen**-tile `LevelSelectScreen` (labelled `NN  <campaign_level_*>`) → difficulty, borrowing the authored character and difficulty screens under a `_developerLevelFlow` flag, and ends in `StoryManager.StartLevelDirect(level, characterID, difficulty)`. `PrepareDirectLevel` resets campaign state, sets `CurrentLevel`, marks the tutorial complete for anything past level 0, and clears `ActiveSaveSlot` to `-1` — no save slot is created or written, so a dev launch can never overwrite a real campaign (autosave inside the level no-ops). Retire the button, the screen, the two keys, and the gate together when it is no longer wanted; `MainMenuSceneTests` pins the flow, the hidden-in-release chain, and the slotless state.
- Scene changes use single-scene replacement through `GameManager.LoadScene`, `ResourceLoader.LoadThreadedRequest`, and `SceneTree.ChangeSceneToPacked`.
- `GameManager` builds a themed `LoadingScreen` per transition (Story portal / Fighter VS / plain variant, chosen from the destination path), parents it to the autoload so it survives `ChangeSceneToPacked`, runs it at `ProcessMode.Always` so a paused tree cannot freeze it, and enforces a two-second minimum display time.
- Cross-scene selections live in `GameManager.CurrentSession` (`SessionData`): selected character, selected stage, active save slot, difficulty, `MatchSettings`, and (Package 12 W5) the never-persisted `FighterMatchOrigin` plus the one-shot hub-arrival flags.
- Default match settings are Stock mode, 3 stocks, 480 seconds, items on/**Medium** with the **Meter pickups** sub-toggle (Resonance Surge, M24/D5(b)) **off**, and stage hazards **On**. Since Package 12 W5 hazards are a plain **On/Off toggle** (`MatchSettings.StageHazardsEnabled`); `HazardTriggerFrequency` is deleted, and each stage's cadence is authored once in `FighterHazardCadence.AuthoredFrames` (2700 frames on all ten stages today, seeded from the retired Medium interval and not tuned). Overtime and Sudden Death still halve the idle gap.
- **The campaign is a sixteen-slot explicit route** (Package 11 A12; Package 13 W2 removed 4A). `StoryManager.CampaignRoute` is `[0, 1, 2 … 15]`; `AdvanceToNextLevel()` steps that list and `GetLevelScenePath(CampaignLevel)` / `GetLevelID(CampaignLevel)` resolve **by ID** (no hero parameter any more). **`CampaignLevel.LegacyNexus = 16` is an `[Obsolete]` reserved ordinal** — saves cast level indices, so it stays in the enum, off the route, resolving to nothing; never reuse it. `StoryManager` still validates a route before loading rather than changing to a broken scene; `tests/integration/CampaignRouteTests.cs` asserts every slot resolves on disk, that the manifest's `StoryLevel` rows agree with `GetLevelScenePath`, and that a sequential advance walks the route and caps at 15.
- Levels 2–15 are built on `StoryLevelControllerBase` (`scripts/Environment/`): geometry builders, `RoomTransitionTrigger` + `StoryCameraConfiner` room graphs with resume-time camera re-confinement, three checkpoints per level with **authored roles** (see Checkpoint roles below), dust tally, boss wiring, extractor placement, encounter locks (since 2026-10-12, `BuildEncounterLock`; the next bullet), a virtual post-boss/exit/completion tail, and pooled-object release on unload. **The Tutorial and Florence deliberately do not extend it** — they predate the convention and were not retrofitted, which is why their manifest rows are still `Prototype` while 2–15 are `Implemented`.
- **Encounter locks — areas the hero must clear (gameplay update 2026-10-12, user request 3; ledger `USER-ENCOUNTER-LOCKS`, rules `PROVISIONAL-ENCOUNTER-LOCK-RULES`, per level `PROVISIONAL-LOCK-LEVEL-AUTHORING`; Story only; every number provisional).** Every area that holds enemies, and every puzzle room whose puzzle blocks the way forward, is an `FTT.Environment.EncounterLock` the hero must clear: **48 locks across Levels 0–15** (L0 1, L1 4, L2–L4 3 each, L5 4, L6 3, L7 2, L8 4, L9–L15 3 each; nine require a puzzle or a breakable). Levels 2–15 build theirs in `BuildLevel` through `StoryLevelControllerBase.BuildEncounterLock(EncounterLockSpec)` (read surface `EncounterLocks`, `ActiveEncounterLock`; `ArmEncounterLocks()` runs right after `SpawnPlayer()`, after the resume baseline); Level 0 (one lock over the bay, built when the Combat Trial begins) and Florence (four) build standalone ones; every lock joins group `story_encounter_lock` and `time_freezable`. The rulebook is `EncounterLockRules` (pure; `EngageInsetPixels` 24, `StallFrames` 600, `HardCapSeconds` 300, `MinAreaWidthPixels` = the 1280 px zoomed view, `MaxAreaWidthPixels` 3200, the view size derived from `StoryCameraRules`); the spec types are `EncounterLockSpec` / `EncounterLockWave` / `EncounterLockSpawn`. **States:** `Armed` (built; the exit barrier closed; pre-placed members spawned dormant) → `Engaged` (the hero at least its half-width + 24 px inside: both barriers close, members wake into Chase on the hero and join the engagement director, waves spawn by trigger — `OnBuild`, `OnEngage`, `WhenAliveAtMost(n)`, `AfterPreviousCleared` — through a cold `EncounterSpawnTear`) → `Cleared` (latched; both barriers open). A living hero that ends a tick outside an Engaged lock (Echo Step, a rewind landing, a rift snap, a scripted move; Level 7's sea haul-outs land inside their lock) reverts it to Armed: the entry opens, the exit stays closed, members go dormant with their HP. **Barriers** (`EncounterLockBarrier`, group `story_encounter_barrier`, never `StoryGateRules.DoorGroup`) are pre-built disabled `StaticBody2D`s on Environment, 20 px wide, from `ColumnTopY` to the floor (from a gate column's foot at a gate), toggled through `SetShapeDisabledSafe`; a closing one pushes an overlapping hero to the area side; the placeholder look is an `UnboundCold` curtain with a column of hollow diamonds. **Members** (`EnemyController.Encounter.cs`) are registered at the spawn call, never inferred from a parent; summons and `DrillInvulnerable` bodies never join. A dormant member refuses every hit like the boss arena ward (a grey "0"), plus hitless statuses, Venom ticks, grabs, zone ticks and crowd bowling. Every member is x-clamped into its area after every move, and a fall net returns one whose feet drop 240 px below it. The stall watchdog relocates a member stuck for 600 ticks (off any floor, or a still melee chaser with the hero 200 px above or below) through `SafeRelocation`, to its anchor, or since FIX-ENEMY beside the hero when it is stalled on its anchor or the anchor is a post over the hero; it never kills one. **Since FIX-ENEMY (2026-10-13, `PROVISIONAL-LOCK-MEMBER-REACH`)** an engaged flyer settles to `EncounterLockRules.FlyerHoverCapPixels` (160) over the hero's feet and stays under its lock camera's top, a thrown flyer brakes at 600 px/s² (every flyer), and an engaged melee ground member steps off a ledge toward a hero more than 64 px below. An Engaged lock past 300 s with no living member and its objectives met force-clears. **Clear** = every spawned member down, every wave spawned, every required puzzle (`RequiredPuzzleIDs`) and breakable (`RequiredBreakables`) met. **A met objective latches** the first time the lock sees it, Armed or Engaged, and the lock switches the object to `PreserveCurrentState`, so a death rewind never un-solves it (FIX-OBJECTIVE, `PROVISIONAL-LOCK-OBJECTIVE-LATCH`; `RequiredObjectivesMet`, `IsRequiredPuzzleMet`, `IsRequiredBreakableMet`; a level derives any puzzle objective from `IsRequiredPuzzleMet`). **The checkpoint hold (FIX-E):** `CheckpointTrigger.IsHeldByEncounterLock` refuses a fracture's activation while a lock its F10 baseline lists is uncleared or the hero stands inside any uncleared lock (notice `checkpoint_held_by_encounter_lock`); every Middle and PreBoss anchor stands outside every area, past the longest Story basic swing from inside, and reachable before the next lock's engage line (`AnchorApproachContentTests`, `PROVISIONAL-ANCHOR-APPROACH`). **Camera:** `StoryCameraConfiner.SetLockBounds` / `ClearLockBounds` ease the limits over 0.6 s to the lock's `CameraBounds` (its area grown to the view) and swallow room `SetBounds` calls meanwhile; locks do not frame. **HUD:** `EventBus.OnEncounterLockChanged(EncounterLockPayload { LockID, State, Remaining, Total, GoDirection })` drives `StoryHUD`'s authored `SafeArea/EncounterLockPanel`: "Enemies left: N" (`hud_encounter_enemies_left`) while engaged, and a 3 s GO prompt with an arrow glyph (`hud_encounter_go`) after a clear, none for `EncounterGoDirection.None`. A lock with an `ObjectiveKey` posts it while engaged (`encounter_lock_objective_clear`), the base's `RestoreLevelObjective()` hands the level's back on the clear or a revert, and a room trigger cannot post over it meanwhile (`RoomEntryObjectiveDeferredToLock`, FIX-LEVEL). **F10:** a lock ID is an encounter ID listed by every checkpoint after it (`EncounterBaselineMap`; `ExplicitEncounterBaselineVersion` 3, so an older record falls back to the authored baseline); a resume builds it Cleared (`RestoreCleared`), and anything else re-arms in full. **F05:** every lock wave equals its manifest `RequiredWaves` row, per-level totals unchanged; the salted hunters the locks adopted became ledgered sources (`VERIFY-ERASER-DUST`). **Interplay:** wave spawn-ins and `RoomTransitionTrigger` freeze activated rooms; the three position-writing pulls move through `BaseSpecial.PullThroughCollision`; `EncounterLock.IsHolding` holds a mover at its near stop (Level 12's spire lift, Level 13's two shards); Level 10's audience stillness clock and Level 14's corridor grids pause while their lock is engaged; the Level 6 lava front and the Level 15 firestorm start on a lock's clear behind the hero (`PROVISIONAL-ESCAPE-RULES`; `EscapeSequenceController.Runner` completes a run on the first tick its runner stands past `FinishX`); the Level 5 hull flood rises on lock clears; par is scaled ×1.5 meanwhile (`TimelineIntegrityRules.ProvisionalParScale`). Boss arenas are not locks. Test seam: `EncounterLock.SuppressForTesting` builds every lock Cleared. **The authoring guide** is `docs/handoffs/GU_E.md` (its AUTHORING GUIDE, with the FIX-E rules) plus `GU_FIX_ENEMY.md` §5, `GU_FIX_OBJECTIVE.md` §8 and `GU_FIX_LEVEL.md` §8. Suites: `EncounterLockRulesTests` (11, pure), `EncounterLockTests` (22), `EncounterLockContentTests` (10, every campaign scene), `EncounterLockObjectiveLatchTests` (4), `EncounterLockObjectiveContentTests` (5), `EncounterMemberRulesTests` (6, pure), `EncounterMemberBehaviourTests` (9), `AnchorApproachContentTests` (2), `StoryHudEncounterGoPromptTests` (1), `StoryKitPullWallTests` (3), `Level05ListingRampTests` (2), and the lock cases in the per-level suites.
- **Boss arenas, enemy hit geometry, gates and the Story camera (playtest feel pass 2026-10-04, workstream ARENA and fix-round items A1–A5, G5, M1, G1, G3, G4, G6; every number provisional, ledger rows `ARENA-*` and the fix round's level-bug section).** Every campaign `BossEncounterController` (Levels 1–12, 14, 15) carries an authored `ArenaBounds` rect (each level's `BossArenaBounds`). On Levels 2, 3, 5–12, 14 and 15 its west edge is the old reveal-radius line, derived once as `BossArenaWestX = BossSpawnX − BossArenaApproachPixels` and handed to the arena overload of `BuildBossEncounter`, which takes no reveal distance — the rect is the reveal (A4); Level 4's is its courtyard entry (`Room4StartX`), Florence's its Phase 2 barricade face so the boss-room checkpoint is struck outside it. Its bodies spawn **dormant** (`BossController.IsEngaged = false`: no target, no chase, no attack) and **leashed** to the rect's x-range (chase, rest tracking and ChargeDash stop at the edge; the body is clamped after every move, knockback or teleport; historical-recovery destinations outside are refused; since 2026-10-09 a Teleport's requested x is clamped into the arena before its landing is validated, and a body whose feet fall more than four units below the arena is put back on its anchor, both at the end of this bullet), and the reveal fires on arena **entry**, not by radius. A boss built directly defaults to engaged and unleashed, and a pool cycle resets both. A dormant boss hit from outside wakes into a reveal with the intro beat skipped (bar, `BossRevealed`, music; the intro is not recorded as seen), and the intro freeze never runs over an already-engaged body. A pinned boss answers a target outside the arena only inside its engagement range (`max(MeleeRangeThreshold, RangedRangeThreshold)` × 60 px), and never with `SummonMinions` or `Teleport` (`SelectAbilityIndex(distance, targetOutsideArena)`); beyond that range it holds the edge. **The arena ward (A5, provisional, `ARENA-LEASH-EDGE-ANSWER`):** an engaged, leashed boss whose target stands more than `BossController.ArenaWardMarginPixels` (60) outside its arena **and** beyond its engagement range is warded (`IsArenaWarded`) — `OnHurtboxHit` refuses every hit (no damage, knockback, status, mark, hitstop, meter or Rally; a `UIPalette.UnboundCold` flash and a grey "0"), `ApplyStatusEffect` refuses a hitless status and Venom ticks deal nothing — so a pinned boss cannot be plinked from beyond its reach. Inside that range hits land (melee spacing against a boss holding its edge, all of Florence's antechamber once the boss has closed to its edge); a dormant or unleashed boss is never warded, so the waking hit lands; scripted `TakeDamage(int)` ignores it. The Level 13 Mirror clone is clamp-leashed to `Level13Controller.MirrorArenaBounds`; the clamp runs after the clone's own step (`MirrorParadoxController.LeashPhysicsPriority`, 100, A2), so no move, Echo Step or warp ends a frame outside, and the clone has **no** ward (`ARENA-MIRROR-NO-WARD`); since 2026-10-09 a clone that falls more than four units below the arena is put back on its spawn (the fall net, end of this bullet). Enemy MeleeStrike/ChargeDash hitboxes clip at the first Environment wall (`EnemyAttackGeometryRules`, `FTT.Combat.EnvironmentProbe`) — a strike once at activation, a charge again on each active frame its owner moved, through one reused ray query per executor (A3); AreaPulse hitboxes are not clipped (`VERIFY-AREAPULSE-WALLS`). **The player's side is the mirror (M1, provisional, `PROVISIONAL-PLAYER-MELEE-LINE-OF-SIGHT`):** the Story `MeleeHitbox` (basics and directional strikes; `Hitbox.RequiresEnvironmentLineOfSight`, set by `CharacterFactory`) lands on an enemy or boss only if an Environment ray from the hurtbox's centre back to `PlayerController.BodyCentreGlobalPosition` is clear, skipping every body that contains the target's centre (`EnvironmentProbe.IsClear(..., ignoreBodiesAtOrigin: true)`), so a wall-phasing Rift Phantom mid-wall is not sheltered by the wall it is passing through; strike surfaces, the Mirror clone, projectiles, zones, constructs and kit shape queries are not gated. Enemy shots re-acquire the telegraphed target at fire time and lead its horizontal motion (capped; `P3-HORIZONTAL-LEAD`), except that a mob's telegraph aimed at Cleopatra's sand decoy stays on the sand and a decoy raised mid-telegraph takes the shot (GAP-10c; bosses ignore the decoy). Gate columns (Levels 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 9, 11 — the breastwork and both lane roofs — and 12) rise to `StoryGateRules.ColumnTopY` (−2400), centred on and wider than the gate so no lip forms; every `BuildDoor` door joins `StoryGateRules.DoorGroup` (`story_gate_door`), and `ArenaGeometryContentTests` fails on any door or `ForcefieldBarrier` without a column no hero can clear (A1, `P5-GATE-COLUMN-SWEEP`) and on any surface a hero can get onto with less than 64 + 4 px under a solid ceiling (G5: Level 4's tower top step moved to `TowerTopStepX/Y` 4880/380, west of the walkway; Level 6's west roof one-way 1000/700 → 1000/720; Level 11's battery-ridge step 2680/730 → 2680/750; `LAYOUT-CRAWLSPACE-MOVES`). Florence's arena west wall now has a ground-level doorway (`Level01Controller.ArenaDoorwayTopY`, 700). The Story levels, the hub, Level 0 and Florence follow the hero with the `StoryCameraConfiner.EnableFollow` rig (`StoryCameraRules`: dead zone 0.1/0.15, soft zone 0.6/0.5, damping X 0.5 / Y 0.3, a 0.3 s **horizontal** look-ahead capped at 3 units (`F8-LEAD-AXIS`), one unit up). The rig drives the camera's position and never `Offset`, which `CameraShake` owns. **Since 2026-10-12 (user request 2, ledger `USER-STORY-CAMERA-ZOOM`, Story only; `PROVISIONAL-CAMERA-ZOOM-RULES`)** every Story camera is zoomed to `StoryCameraRules.ViewZoom` **1.5** from its constructor (a 1280 × 720 world-px view, `ViewWorldSize`; the dead and soft zones stay view fractions, the lead cap and snap distance world pixels); a boss or Mirror reveal frames its arena from the reveal to the defeat or teardown (`StoryCameraConfiner.RequestFraming` / `ReleaseFraming` in `StoryCameraConfiner.Framing.cs`: zoom `StoryCameraRules.FramingZoom` = clamp(1920 / (width + 2 × `FramingMarginPixels` 120), `BossFramingMinZoom` 1.0, 1.5), eased over `ZoomDampingSeconds` 0.6, never wider than the room, with a horizontal pull onto the arena leashed inside the hero's soft zone; the widest live request wins); `CameraShake.ScreenStableOffset` divides a Story camera's offset by its zoom, so the on-screen shake is unchanged; encounter locks confine through the separate lock-bounds layer and do not frame. `FighterCamera`, the Test Arena and the drills are untouched. **Level bugs the bots surfaced (fix round):** pressure plates mask Player | Environment (`PressurePlateTemplate` mask 65 — the movable weights live on Environment since M02; a plate still weighs only `WeightedObject`s and the hero), and a destroyed `DamageableEnvironmentObject` wakes the `movable_weight` bodies within 64 px, because Godot never wakes a sleeping body whose static support is disabled — Pompeii's winch was unwinnable without both (G1). Every `PathMovingPlatform` stop and `TrapdoorPlatform` leaf stands clear of static geometry or sinks flush with the top of the floor as a walk-on stop (the Level 12 spire lift now stops at 7050/1916, `Level12Controller.SpireLiftBottomY`; Level 7's SkiffA west stop moved −140 → −80), and a descending platform holds above the hero's body instead of crushing it (`IsHeldByBodyBelow`, `CrushGuardMask` = Player only) (G3, `LIFT-STOP-RULE`, `PROVISIONAL-LIFT-HOLD-OVER-HERO`). `DialogueManager.StartSequence(id)` drops a sequence requested over an active one yet returns true, so `StoryLevelControllerBase` queues its post-boss and exit beats while `IsDialogueSequenceActive` and releases them after the completion, and Levels 5, 12 and 15 start their pre-boss beat through `StartPreBossDialogue`, which plays nothing once the boss is down, the seal accepted or the level complete (G4, `PREBOSS-BEAT-AFTER-DEFEAT`). Level 9's watchtower and radar-mast Extractors moved so neither leaves a sub-hero-width strip of deck (G6). **The swinging-rope bars are non-solid** (2026-10-09, bot game bug 12, `VERIFY-MOVEMENT-FEEL-FALLOUT` (a)): `PendulumAnchorTemplate`'s 16 × 320 rope body is on collision layer 0 with mask 0 (it was Environment), and none of the seven placed ropes (Level 7 three, Level 10 four) overrides it, so it blocks no hero, enemy projectile, enemy wall or edge probe, melee line-of-sight check, kit ground-snap or Echo Step / Warp destination check, and nobody stands on it. The grab point (`LedgeGrabPoint`, Trigger layer / Player mask), the carry, the release launch, the swing, Time Freeze, rewind, the `puzzle_object` group and Level 10's audience throw never read the bar; `PendulumRopeBarTests` guards it in real physics frames. The same reruns found the Level 10 boss arena's trapdoor recess (x 8970–9670, floor at y 1260, 100 px below the boards' underside) open sideways onto a bottomless drop. Since 2026-10-09 `Level10Controller.BuildTiringHouseStage` closes both ends with 20 px walls under the boards (x 8950–8970 and 9670–9690, y 1160–1300; the trap gaps, the island and the climb back are unchanged), so the hero can no longer walk or be carried out, which `Level10ContentTests` pins with a hero-box flood from under every opening and a hard-driven hero in real physics frames. **Direct position writes meet the world since 2026-10-09** (`VERIFY-L10-ARENA-RECESS-EXIT`'s boss half, closed: a wall stops only a body physics moves, and the Tragedy King's `exit_stage_left` used to land him past the east wall, under the boards, falling forever). `FTT.Combat.RelocationRules` is the rule (it never queries the engine, so it is tested on plain rects) and `SafeRelocation` its `EnvironmentProbe` adapter, measuring each body from its first enabled collision shape: the body's own box — its full width, from its head down to 30 % of the half-height above its feet (19.2 px on the boss, 10.8 on a standard enemy, 9.6 on the hero), so the floor it stands on, a seam between floor pieces and a low step are not walls — is swept (two `CastMotion` passes against Environment, the moving body excluded) from where it stands toward the destination and stops 2 px short of the first wall it touches, so no slab however thin slips between probes (since the second review, 2026-10-09; before it, three rays alone did that work), while the three rays across its shape (centre and ±70 % of the half-height, stopping the half-width + 2 px short of a face) stay beside the sweep for a wall the box already overlaps where it starts, which a shape cast does not report, and the landing takes whichever stops first; a body that falls then needs Environment or one-way floor within 480 px below the landing, else the landing steps back toward the start every 16 px to the first supported point, and with none short of the start the write is refused and the body stays put. Flyers skip the floor half and wall-phasers the wall half (the Rift Phantom's `rift_step` lands as before); a body already over nothing — no floor under its centre or either half-width extent (`RelocationRules.HasOriginSupport`), so one standing on a ledge with its centre past the edge is supported — keeps its wall-clipped landing, while a landing needs floor under its centre; a world that cannot be asked (no tree or space, a physics flush, a shapeless owner) keeps the requested point. It covers every Teleport in `EnemyAbilityExecutor.Teleport` (ranges and telegraphs unchanged; a refused teleport moves nothing and raises no `Teleported`, so no Jackal decoy), every `SummonMinions` spawn point (`RelocationRules.ResolveSummon`: the minion's own box travels out from `EmergencePoint` — the summoner's feet, or, when the minion has no floor there, the nearest point under the summoner's body, every 16 px out to its half-width with the requested side first, that has a floor and that the minion's box reaches without a wall — so a narrow minion summoned from a ledge lands on the ledge rather than over the void; the minion is re-anchored by `ConfigureSpawn` before its first physics step, at that emergence point when refused) and Einstein's Quantum Entanglement blink (deferred to right after a hit's physics flush, where a guard break lands). Not covered: T01b's historical recovery, which still checks x only, and a teleport or summon resolved inside a flush (none is today). Behind it a **safety net** (`PROVISIONAL-ARENA-FALL-NET`): a leashed, living boss whose feet end a physics frame below its arena's **net line** — the bottom edge plus `BossController.ArenaFallNetMarginPixels` (240 px, four units), stated once as `BossController.ArenaFallNetLineY(arena)` — is put back on its `HistoricalRecoveryAnchor` (if inside the arena) or spawn point, velocity zeroed, with no HP, phase, reward or T01b change, printing `[arena-fall] boss '<id>' …` and counting `ArenaFallRecoveries`; `MirrorParadoxController` has its own copy for the clone, on the same line. The margin is Level 4's (`VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET`, closed 2026-10-09): that arena's rect ends at y 1080 but the Tribunal's walkable Phase 2 pit floor is at 1120 (`Level04Controller.TribunalPitFloorY`), and the net first shipped firing at the edge itself, returning a survivor that stood in the pit to its spawn. 240 px clears the deepest floor under any arena edge; `ArenaGeometryContentTests.NothingABossCanStandOnLiesBelowItsArenaFallNet` fails on any surface a boss could stand on, enabled or not, on or below the net line over any of the fifteen arenas, and `Level04ContentTests` stands the survivor in the open pit in real physics frames. The rule's agent-chosen numbers and its remaining limits are `PROVISIONAL-RELOCATION-CLEARANCE`. Suites: `RelocationRulesTests`, `SafeRelocationTests`, `BossArenaFallNetTests`, `Level10BossRelocationTests`, and one case each in `ArenaGeometryContentTests` and `Level04ContentTests`.
- **Level 4A is retired** (S27, story review 2026-09-28; Package 13 W2, plan D1). Deleted: the nine `Level_04A_<hero>.tscn` scenes and controllers, `LegacyLevelControllerBase`, `LegacyKitGate`, `NexusResonanceSource` and the F04 Nexus cast path in `PlayerController`, the whole GAP-14 `PuzzleOnly` fence (its only producer was the Nexus cast), `resources/Bosses/legacy/`, the nine reward manifests, the ten 4A dialogue sets, the pool configs, `level_04a_audio`, the manifest rows and 168 translation rows. Its dust row folded into Level 5, and **the Eraser's debut moved to Level 5** (an `EraserDebutTrigger` on the listing boat deck between the Middle and PreBoss anchors; `eraser_debut_bark` kept; `EraserDebutTrigger.WorldTimeSuspendedProbe` inherited the Nexus source's probe; since 2026-10-12 the debut Eraser is a member of Level 5's E-deck encounter lock, spawned by the lock's `@0` wave at (6850, 830) once one E-deck slasher is left and adopted for its bark by `EraserDebutTrigger.AdoptEraserDebut`, with `RouteTriggerSpawns = false`). `LEGACY_LEVELS.md` and `LEGACY_CHECKPOINTS.md` are retired history, and older handoffs that describe the 4A framework describe deleted code. A v7 save parked in a 4A scene re-parks at Level 5 on a fresh attempt, banking its held wallet once (the v8 step, below).
- **Levels 13–15 are the Act III gauntlet and do not return to the hub** (Package 11 A3b). They chain level → level; the hub is unreachable until campaign completion, and each Act III level banks in its own completion transaction exactly as every other level does (H02, Package 12 W2 — see the Story progression section). Level 15 still ends the campaign: post-boss beat → Temporal Core insertion at the Prime Anchor (Level 15's N01 sealing anchor; its AwaitingSeal readiness is persisted like every other level's) → `CampaignCompletionSequence` (Package 13 W3, S39/S40/S43/S48): ending dialogue (the release, the captive montage, the three S44 lesser returns, the hero's aura removed, "We've got you. Come aboard — one last time.") → **Epilogue: the Time-Ship Send-off** (`epilogue.sendoff`: Okafor, Wren, Sarah, `{HeroFarewellLine}`, the step through the Warden portal) → credits → **After-Credits: Homecoming** (`epilogue.homecoming`, once per campaign, skippable, `StorySaveData.HomecomingSeen`) → MainMenu. The completion flag is still written when the credits **begin** so quitting mid-roll cannot lose a campaign the save has already advanced past. The ending variant is chosen by **N05**: exactly **fourteen** level IDs (Levels 2–15, no hero dependence), compared **unrounded** against `CleanEndingThresholdPoints = 700f`, identical on every difficulty; a `level_04a_*` entry an old save carries is dead data and never counts. When a save `IsCompleted`, the hub loads **the last evening aboard**: a mortal hero (no aura), the crew's epilogue lines, the Holodeck and grid, and a portal offering **Go home** (`HubWorldController.GoHome` → `CampaignCompletionSequence.BeginGoHome`: the send-off and credits, never the Homecoming).
- **Calibration Drills (F18)** have two entry points (Package 11 A11). Standalone: a `CalibrationDrillsButton` on the main menu's root screen, between Fighter Mode and Settings, available on a first boot with **no Story save**, running `CalibrationDrillPicker` → `CalibrationDrillList` → `HolodeckDrill`. From the hub: the Holodeck console's second entry, which skips the picker (a campaign save locks its character) and returns to its own hub. Route state is `SessionData.CalibrationReturnScenePath` / `CalibrationDrillIndex`, **never persisted**, and `FTT.Combat.CalibrationRoute` owns the paths and both routing rules. Six scripted drills (block string, landing tech, launch DI, grab/throw, Echo Step, Rally reclaim) run on Florence's **Sealed** geometry with 60 s attempts and a 3-miss tolerance; the last drill hides "Next Drill".
- `TestArena` uses `FighterSimulationDriver`: Klotho state is authoritative and the two `PlayerController` nodes are collision-free presentation adapters. The local human/CPU match core, rules, results, rematch flow, and stage select are functional. **All ten catalog stages are authored production-contract scenes** (`scenes/fighter/FighterStage_*.tscn`, Package 6, 2026-08-08): each has its own `FighterStageGeometry` entry (fixed-point walls, ceiling, blast zone, spawn distance, two or three one-way platforms, **floor segments**, hazard and orb anchors), its own `FighterStageController` scene with era-distinct placeholder presentation and a `ParallaxBackground`, its own `FighterCamera` midpoint/zoom wiring, its own pool config, its own `StageAudioSet`, and its own preview plate. Each stage carries one of ten era-specific deterministic hazard identities with real warning/active/recovery phases (`CooldownFrames` is the recovery length), per-type movement/targeting/dwell/idle behaviour, one-shot per-fighter hit masks where specified, and the block-absorbs-a-tick rule at one shield charge. `FighterStageConformance` (shared validator) pins every scene's markers and colliders against its fixed-point geometry through `pixel = (950 + x*62.5, 700 - y*62.5)`, and every stage ships a per-stage GdUnit suite. Production art, music, and VFX remain Package 10.
- **Open and Sealed stages** (Package 11 A9). The main floor is authored as `FighterStageGeometry.FloorSegments`; **an empty array is the sentinel for an unbroken wall-to-wall floor**, which is what the seven Sealed stages and the legacy flat arena carry. **Three stages are Open** — Paris Bastille (a 5.0-wide centre courtyard pit, respawn anchor moved to (−4, 3) because stage centre is now a hole), Vesuvius Caldera (a 2.5-wide collapsed shelf against the right wall) and Nassau Flagship (a 3.0-wide stern gap). Their pits are genuinely reachable through ordinary play, which closes the 2026-08-08 audit's H-11. Pit-facing segment ends are true grabbable ledges, encoded on the existing `LedgeAnchor` int as `(Platforms.Length + segmentIndex) * 2 + side` — **no new snapshot field**, `FighterRuntimeComponent` is still exactly full at 128 bytes. Ground support, the ground snap and the drop-through gate are all per-x (`HasFloorSupport(x)`). Respawn anchors are per stage. The catalog carries `FighterStageData.IsOpenStage`, shown as a badge in stage select and the Holodeck and cross-checked against the geometry by a test.
- **Fighter menu, Versus CPU and match origins (Package 12 W5, H05/G01/M26 per D4(a)).** The main menu's Fighter button opens `scenes/menus/FighterPlayOptions.tscn` (Local Versus / Versus CPU / a Remote Play notice, `DEFER-STEAM-RPT` / Back). **Local Versus** is two human tokens only — the old CPU toggle and CPU-pick flow are gone. **Since 2026-10-16 it is press-to-join** (decision 9.1, ledger `USER-LAST-USED-DEVICE`, `PROVISIONAL-DEVICE-FOLLOW-RULES`): `InputManager.BeginLocalJoin` empties every slot (an input source keeps its own), the first device to make a join press (a face button, or a key, pad button or mouse button bound to `ui_accept`, Interact or Basic Attack) claims player 1 and the next different device player 2, `CharacterSelectScreen._Input` consumes the joining press so it does nothing else, and each panel shows its authored prompt (`P1JoinPrompt` / `select_press_to_join_player_one`, `P2JoinPrompt` / `select_press_to_join`) until its slot is claimed; a pad that disconnects frees its slot and brings its prompt back, and a slot whose pad dropped during the join takes the first free pad that connects. The join lives while the destination is the select or a Fighter stage (`InputManager.ApplyJoinLifetimeForDestination`, called by `GameManager.LoadScene`), so Rematch and Back-to-select keep it, and a load to the menu or hub ends it (`EndLocalJoin` gives an unclaimed slot 0 back to the keyboard). `ui_cancel` is routed to the device that pressed it, and only player 1's can leave the screen. The select's footer is player 1's (CM-PADUI, CM-FIXB-PADUI B17; `PROVISIONAL-PAD-MENU-RULES`): another device's directional focus moves are always swallowed, and so are its confirms that are also its token inputs (a joined keyboard player 2's Enter still presses the focused button); Up walks player 1's focus round the footer, and any other token input player 1 makes hands it back to the Remote Play notice. Versus CPU and the Holodeck never begin a join: player 1 follows the last-used device (the Autoloads section). **Versus CPU** is a P1-only select, then `HolodeckConsolePanel` in `FrontEnd` mode (no drills entry; its Back unlocks P1), then the match. `SessionData.FighterMatchOrigin` (`LocalVersus | VersusCpu | Holodeck | Drill`) is never persisted, `ReturnToHubAfterFighterMatch` is a read-only view of it, and `FTT.UI.FighterFlowRoutes` is the single statement of every exit (pause Exit, results buttons, select Back). Holodeck exits land the hero at the hub's `HolodeckConsoleSpawn` through a one-shot session flag, and **Reconfigure** reopens the console panel. Random character/stage tiles resolve through `FighterRandomPick` from `SessionData.PendingMatchSeed`, which the driver consumes as the match seed (a Rematch rolls fresh). CPU difficulty labels read `cpu_difficulty_easy/medium/hard` with the enum ordinals unchanged (G15b). The Fighter HUD shows four 20 px cooldown radials (S1 / S2 / Movement / Echo Step, the last dimmed below 30 meter; M25), and G12's P1 ▲ / P2 ● slot shapes with three local slot palettes (`PlayerSlotPalettes`, Settings → Gameplay) never reach the sim.
- Campaign scenes attach shared services through `StorySceneBootstrapper` (`scripts/Environment/`): a resource-driven `DialogueManager`, reusable `StoryHUD`, pause menu, and (for levels) the rewind manager and presentation overlay. Dialogue content lives in `DialogueSetData`/`DialogueSequenceData` resources under `resources/Dialogue/`.
- The Tutorial, Florence (gear-puzzle room gating, triggered waves — since 2026-10-12 four standalone encounter locks: streets 720–2330, piazza 2350–4430, the print shop 5800–8630, which also needs the gears, and the workshop 8900–10460, with a west wall at x 0 and its anchors at 300 / 8840 / 10660 — boss, results, autosave), and hub (every arrival on the Bridge — `BridgeSpawn`, M11 — except a Holodeck exit, which lands at `HolodeckConsoleSpawn`; the Calibration Bay anchor stays for the Training Wing; the Holodeck CPU practice loop; Commander Sarah, Medic Okafor and Chief Engineer Wren; the Repository's completion-only ledger) flows are functionally complete with placeholder presentation. **The hub since Package 13 W3:** Wren stands in the Training Wing beside the Holodeck with one tip per act; a wordless **triage readout** (`HubTriageReadout`) beside the portal flashes the next era red, burns unsealed eras cold and flares the just-sealed one gold (dark from Act III); Sarah's Act I/II sets play **one line per visit, in order**, with after-Level-N gates (`HubDialogueSelector.ArrivalSequenceFor`, order derived from the effect ledger, visit stamp `StorySaveData.LastHubLineVisit`); the Act I→II boundary (`hub.act1_boundary`, Okafor's faint-shard report) and the Act II→III departure (the Beacon handover on the Bridge, S42) play on arrival. **The portal is still never gated** (`DEFER-HUB-PORTAL-GATE`) and the hub is still one corridor. `StoryDifficultyTuning` scales Story enemy HP/damage at spawn without touching Fighter loadouts.
- **Level 0 was rebuilt again by Package 13 W3** (S01–S16). Phases run `Hold → Rift → Arrival → Calibration → …`. **The hold** (Part 1 beat 5, `FTT.Environment.Level00HoldFight`): an unnamed voice (`speaker_unknown_voice`, "???", no portrait) teaches the 3-hit string and Block in a scripted, **unlosable** fight against three era locals (`PlayerController.ScriptedHPFloor` = 1; no rewind, Rally, meter, dust or Integrity; the last local repeats a telegraphed swing until it is blocked). Beat 6 adds a ~2 s wordless Eraser **watcher** silhouette (`EraserWatcher`); a `WardenRiftGate` carries the hero to the bay. Part 2 is the rewritten arrival (`level_00.intro`, Okafor's first line, `{HeroAcceptLine}`, "Then welcome aboard"), then **Wren's offer** through `FTT.UI.TwoOptionPrompt` (the shared `ConfirmModal`, never pausing): **Full calibration** (the lessons below, with the five S08 station lines) or **Skip** (`StorySaveData.SkippedCalibration`; the skipped lessons become once-per-slot **first-use tooltips** — `FTT.Core.FirstUseTooltips`, `FirstUseTooltipPresenter` on every level, `ShownFirstUseTooltips`). Wren speaks the Part 3 opener; Sarah's S11 triage line is the hub's prologue arrival. *From Package 11 A5/A6b, still true of the Full path:* Part 1 opens on the four-beat ignition: the cold Severed extraction beam **bites**, gold **ignites on the hero**, the beam **breaks**, and a clean concentric **Warden portal** opens beside the tear — the "two kinds of door" rule the whole game's visual grammar rests on. Calibration is now `BasicHits → RallyReclaim → Block → Grab → HitstunDI → LandingTech → MeterAndDefy → UseRewind → UseTimeFreeze → Done`; the Special, Ultimate and movement-ability lessons are **deleted**, because the Legacy Unlock Schedule means the player does not have those slots yet. Part 3 is authored for base jump reach (double jump → roll), with no movement-ability corridor; its double-jump gate's upper step sits 240 px above the first (`Level00Controller.DoubleJumpGatedStepX/Y`, (1900, 540), moved 2026-10-08 because the apex hang let Lincoln's ~133 px single jump clear the old 128 / 130 px steps), above every single jump (Cleopatra's ~214 px is the best) and inside every double jump (Lincoln's ~265 px is the worst), pinned by `TutorialCalibrationContentTests`. The Grab lesson uses a guard that restores its HP on every swing until the player grabs and throws; the Hitstun Agency lesson uses a scripted free launch (no HP, no Rally, no meter, no rewind charge) with a tutorial-only hitstop for the ±15° DI read and a mandatory landing tech; Meter & Defy fills the meter (since 2026-10-11 with a direct set, `PlayerController.SetUltimateMeter(InfluenceMeterRules.MaxValue)`, not a damage-dealt gain of 100, so no Story meter-gain factor can short-fill the bar), lights the F13 seal and spends it on a scripted lethal-tagged hit. **Since 2026-10-12 (GU-L0)** the Hologram Combat Trial is one standalone encounter lock over the bay's east end (`Level00Controller.TrialLockID`, area 2580–4780, its entry barrier at 2560–2580 and the bay's east wall as its exit), built only when the trial begins: wave 1 is five drones on engage, wave 2 three drones and a `chrono_slasher` once wave 1 is down, and its clear ends the tutorial (the global kill counter is deleted); its waves name no manifest row, because Level 0 pays no dust. The roll gate moved 3100 → 2150, the trial anchor to 2300 and the font to (2450, 900), so the trial opens outside the lock; the grab lesson's drone is handed back to the pool after its throw (`GrabDroneDismissFrames` 90); the hold stays scripted and the Time Freeze drill exempt. The Eraser watcher stands at the visible view's left edge + `WatcherViewInsetPixels` (160), on the floor.
- Level *geometry, actors and hazards* are still built programmatically in the level controllers — valid prototyping state, not the intended final asset pipeline. **UI is no longer in that bucket**: as of Package 8 every production screen (main menu with its four-screen stack, Fighter character/stage/rules select, settings, both pause menus, both HUDs, dialogue box, resonance grid, level and match results, both loading variants, credits) is an authored, themed, focus-authored `.tscn`. Where a widget's *count* is data-driven (stock pips, shield charges, cooldown slots, resonance node buttons, character tiles' colour identity) it is still built in code inside the authored scene, deliberately — authoring a fixed number would cap the HUD at today's defaults.

## Autoloads and system ownership

`project.godot` currently registers these persistent autoloads:

- `EventBus`: typed C# events/payloads for non-adjacent system communication.
- `GameManager`: session data, defaults, and async scene transitions.
- `StoryManager`: campaign level, dust, rewind count, and hub/level flow.
- `PoolManager`: scene-pool registration, warm-up, spawn/release, and overflow policies.
- `InputManager`: action names, local controller assignment, and dedicated Roll capture (the deterministic Dash gesture conversion was removed with the universal dash, 2026-08-09). Since Package 12 W6 it also runs the Toggle Block latch (`BlockToggleLatch`) and per-device left-stick deadzone/down-threshold processing **before** chord recognition and quantization. That is local input only, never in a snapshot or hash. Package 11 renamed `gameplay_rewind` → **`gameplay_time_freeze`** (keyboard `R`, physical keycode 82; gamepad **L3**, `JoyButton.LeftStick` = 7, since 2026-10-11 by user direction D11, `USER-GAMEPAD-TIME-FREEZE-L3` — it inherited Back = 4 from the retired rewind until then) and added **`gameplay_grab`** and **`gameplay_echo_step`**, both **Unbound by default** on both device kinds. Time Freeze deliberately has **no `GameplayButtons` bit** — it is Story-only and can never reach the deterministic sim. **The default gamepad layout uses Godot 4 `JoyButton` numbers since 2026-10-11** (ledger `VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` (b)): Movement Ability = LeftShoulder **9**, Special 2 = RightShoulder **10**, the Ultimate chord = **9 + 10** (since 2026-10-16 the C01c recipe read from those two components, below; `gameplay_ultimate` itself carries no pad event), Time Freeze = LeftStick (L3) **7** (D11, 2026-10-11; Back **4** and Guide **5** now carry no authored action, and the Controls tab labels L3 "Pad Button 7" because there is no glyph table), Pause = Start **6**, Dialogue Log = RightStick **8**. Until then `project.godot` carried the Godot 3 numbering 4 / 5 / 4 + 5, which Godot 4 reads as Back / Guide / Back + Guide — Special 2 sat on the system Guide button and a gamepad movement press also froze time. `GamepadDefaultBindingTests` reads the defaults from `ProjectSettings` and pins both shoulders, the chord equal to its recipe's component defaults, nothing on Guide, no button shared by two actions (outside the chord until 2026-10-16; since then with no exception, and the Ultimate's direct pad slot authored empty), and (since D11) Time Freeze's pad half exactly L3 with keyboard `R` kept. Saved overrides get no schema migration: `SaveManager.ApplySavedInputBindings` runs the pure, idempotent `InputBindingSet.RepairGodot3ShoulderRows(projectDefaults)` before applying them, rewriting only rows the Controls tab could not have made on purpose (`PROVISIONAL-GAMEPAD-OVERRIDE-NO-MIGRATION` lists what it repairs and what it leaves; since D11 a stale Movement-on-Back row is repaired only while another loaded row still holds Back, and a saved Time Freeze row keeps what it carried); save schema is still v8. **Since 2026-10-16 (the combat and movement update, decisions 9.1 and 9.2; ledger `USER-LAST-USED-DEVICE`, `PROVISIONAL-DEVICE-FOLLOW-RULES`, `USER-PAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD-LENIENCY`, `PROVISIONAL-ULTIMATE-CHORD-LENIENCY`, `USER-PAD-MENUS`, `PROVISIONAL-PAD-MENU-RULES`; all local input before `PlayerInputFrame.Create`, never in a snapshot, hash or save):** *Player 1 follows the last-used device.* `PollDeviceActivity` runs once a tick, before any slot is sampled, and reads device state only: a new press of pad buttons 0–20, a bound pad axis crossing out of its action's deadzone (the triggers), the left stick leaving the device's own `StickProfile` deadzone, or a new press of a key or mouse button bound to a gameplay, direct or menu action (mouse motion never counts, and a device's first poll only records it). Activity hands slot 0 to that device; the switching press is a fresh edge, and the Toggle Block latch and the chord window drop. In a tie the current device keeps the slot, then the keyboard, then the lowest pad id; a slot an input source drives is never moved, slot 1 is never filled outside a local join, and an emptied slot 0 falls back to the keyboard. `LocalJoinMode { FollowLastUsed, Joined }`: the Local Versus select runs `BeginLocalJoin` (the Fighter-menu bullet under Scene architecture), and a lost player-1 controller outside a join resumes on a key press or that pad's face button (Story pause and `LocalFighterPause`). The poll does not run while the tree is paused. `DeviceActivityProbeForTesting` replaces every raw device read. *The pad Ultimate is the C01c recipe chord.* `gameplay_ultimate` keeps only keyboard U; `SampleGodotDevice` steps one `FTT.Core.UltimateChordWindow` per pad slot over the recipe's live component bindings, behind `ShortcutEnabled(Ultimate, Joypad)`, so the Controls tab's chord toggle is wired and remapping a component moves the chord. While the slot's Ultimate is affordable a lone LB or RB waits `UltimateChordWindow.LeniencyFrames` (3) for its partner: the partner gives only the Ultimate and masks both components until each is released; a window that runs out emits the component held, 3 frames late; a release inside it emits one held frame; not affordable, the components pass through as before, and a component a chord masked stays masked until released. Affordability is reported every tick (`InputManager.ReportUltimateAffordable`, stale after 2 ticks): Story's `PlayerController.IsUltimateAffordableForInput` (meter full, slot unlocked, not Suppressed, not time-frozen) and Fighter's `FighterSimulationDriver.PushUltimateReadiness` (meter at cap, not a busy A02 caster, not captured); the ability lockout is excluded in both, a LAN match pushes nothing (its chord passes through), and the CPU, the bots and the Mirror (`SetInputSource`) bypass it all. A pause or focus loss forgets waits and keeps masks (`ForgetChordWaits`); a device hand-over drops the whole window. `RepairGodot3ShoulderRows` now also runs `RepairStaleUltimatePadChord`, which drops a saved Ultimate pad half of {9, 10} or {4, 5} to the empty default and keeps the keyboard half, and `ApplySavedInputBindings` then runs `RestoreUnreachableUltimateChord`, which turns a pad chord switched off before the update back on when the pad Ultimate would otherwise have no route. *Menus.* `project.godot` adds pad A (button 0) to `ui_accept` and pad B (button 1) to `ui_cancel` beside their keyboard defaults (device −1, every pad); the two are menu navigation, outside `RemappableActions` and `InputShortcuts.DirectActions`, never saved, repaired or conflict-checked (`PadMenuBindingTests`). They were added because on Godot 4.7.1 the built-in actions are keyboard-only, so no pad could press a GUI button (`FIX-PAD-MENU-ACCEPT-CANCEL`). `InputManager.ConsumeHeldPresses()` (`InputManager.MenuPress.cs`) spends every device-driven slot's **press** edges on the next physics tick only: release edges pass (the sim's short-hop latch needs them) and a held chord shoulder is settled into `Held` that tick (`UltimateChordWindow.Step(settleHeld)`; CM-FIXB-PADUI B14 / B22). Every pause resume, the end of a gameplay-pausing dialogue, the Fighter select's overlay closes and its return to selection call it, and `InputManager._Input` calls it for a `ui_accept` press that a focused, visible, enabled button is about to take (`FTT.UI.PadMenuInput.FocusedButtonTakesAccept`), so one press never acts in a menu and in the game too. A pad that disconnects raises `DeviceAssigned(slot, UnassignedDevice)` from `ApplyDeviceTopology`.
- `SaveManager`: three story slots and global settings/statistics.
- `AudioManager`: AudioServer bus control and music/SFX entry points.
- `HapticFeedbackManager`: controller vibration settings and triggers.
- `CameraShake`: shared hit/impact feedback service.
- `LocalizationManager`: translation setup and lookup support.
- `ViewportEnforcer`: viewport/aspect enforcement hook.

Use the existing static `Instance` pattern for these services. Non-adjacent systems should communicate through `EventBus`; adjacent scene relationships should use exported references or explicit typed `GetNode<T>()` paths. Subscribe in `_Ready()` and unsubscribe in `_ExitTree()`. Avoid recursive `FindChild()` and repeated string-based scene-tree searches.

`NetworkManager` and `MatchmakingManager` are not current autoloads and are not wired into production flow.

|---|---|
| `TimeFreezeController` (`scripts/Environment/`) | The F03 Story escape verb. Attached by `StorySceneBootstrapper` under `includeRewind` — **levels only, never the hub**; exposed on `StorySceneServices.TimeFreeze`, group `time_freeze_controller`. |
| `CollapseTremorController` + `CollapseTremorRules` + `FallingDebris` (pool `falling_debris`) | The <20% / <10% Integrity Tremor. Attached by `StoryLevelControllerBase` on timed levels only. |

Since Package 12 W8 (M16) a 180-frame held-Interact channel with no HP and no discharge; keeps the `chronal_extractor` group, drain weight and dust share. |

| `HubTriageReadout` | Package 13 W3: the hub's wordless era triage display. |
| `ExtractionBeamPresentation`, `NearCaptureBeat` | Package 13 W4: Captain Smith's taking (Level 5) and the Hakata Bay near-capture (Level 9, `IntegrityClockPause.ScriptedBeat`). |

| `CoreMechanicCuePresenter` (child of `AudioManager`, `AudioManager.CoreCues`) | Package 12 W10: plays the H04 cue catalog from existing `EventBus` events. |

## Gameplay architecture

### Character data and runtime controller

- Static identity and baseline tuning are Godot `Resource` objects (`CharacterData`) stored as `.tres` files.
- Story runtime behavior is owned by `PlayerController : CharacterBody2D`. Fighter runtime behavior in the Test Arena is owned by `FighterSimulation`; `PlayerController` is presentation-only there.
- The canonical state set is `Idle`, `Running`, `Dashing`, `Rolling`, `Skidding`, `Crouching`, `Airborne`, `Attacking`, `UsingSpecial`, `UsingUltimate`, `Blocking`, `Stunned`, `Dazed`, `LedgeHanging`, `Dead`, `Respawning`, `UsingMovementAbility`, `Grabbing`, and **`Thrown`** (F23, appended after `Grabbing` by Package 11 A1c). `Dashing` keeps its ordinal as a permanent reservation. **`Respawning` is Fighter-only** since Package 12 W1 (R02): a Story death rewind revives through the explicit `Dead -> Idle/Airborne` edge and never enters it. The M05 knockdown and get-up are sub-phases of `Stunned`, and Echo Step's wind-up and the landing tech stay flags rather than sub-states (`DEFER-M04-SUBPHASE-FLAGS`). A thrown victim rides out its own state: no landing tech, no block escape, fixed trajectory. **The air dodge is a sub-phase of `Rolling`** (A03, below), not a new state.
- **Universal movement (user direction 2026-10-08, ledger `USER-MOVEMENT-FEEL`; the agent-chosen details are `PROVISIONAL-MOVEMENT-FEEL-RULES`; both modes, every number provisional).** The grounded run reaches full speed in **six** frames and stops in **four** (`RunAccelerationFrames` / `RunDecelerationFrames`; 14 / 12 from the 2026-08-10 gameplay-feel batch until then), counted from the stick: Story's push and release ticks take the first ramp step instead of being dead state changes, a push against the facing from a standstill turns at once with no skid (`PlayerController.PushReversesTravel`; a real reversal at speed still skids and reaches full speed the other way on frame 10, 4 + 6, as in the sim), and the sim rounds its fixed-point ramp step up by one raw unit (`FighterAirPhysics.RampStep`) so every move speed lands on the frame. **The jump arc, under normal control** (every airborne state except the launch states), takes one gravity multiplier per tick from the velocity before the step, first match wins: **fast-fall** (Down held) ×3, cancelling the Warp float; the movement-ability float window, unchanged; the **short hop** (rising from the fighter's own Jump press with Jump released, ×2.5 — Story only until 2026-10-16, both modes since, below); the **apex hang**, ×0.5 while |vy| < 2 u/s once the fighter has jumped this airtime and, since 2026-10-16, only while Jump is held; the **fall**, ×2.2; else 1.0. The fall then caps at the shared **12 u/s** terminal (Story 720 px/s; it was Story 10, sim 20), or at **15 u/s** (900 px/s) while fast-falling. **Fast-fall ramps in instead of snapping** (M01's snap to 20 is retired) and is stateless; it works any time in the air under normal control — in Story through `Airborne`, aerial swings (so the down-air still stacks into a falling strike) and the air dodge, but not aerial Specials — and a descent already above its cap (a released fast-fall, a launch that has just ended) eases down one base-gravity step per tick. The hang's discriminator is "jumped this airtime": the sim reads `RemainingJumps < MaxJumpCount` (`FighterAirPhysics.HasJumpedThisAirtime`), Story a latch, `PlayerController.JumpedThisAirtime`, set by every jump including a ledge or rope jump-off and cleared by floor contact in any state, a ledge or rope grab and the Tempest Apex refresh (Story's jump budget is untouched, and its ledge grab still refills no jump), so a walk-off, a drop-through or a ledge drop gets no hang and a ledge jump-off or the sim's climb jump does. **The launch states keep their old gravity exactly** (hitstun and tumble, knockdown, thrown, daze: Story ×1.8 with a hard 600 px/s = 10 u/s clamp, the sim flat −30 clamped at 20, `LaunchTerminalFallSpeed`), so launch arcs, DI and tech timing did not move. The shared numbers live once in `UniversalMovementRules`, as multipliers and speeds on each mode's own base gravity — and **that per-mode base gravity is intended** by the user's 2026-10-09 ruling ("Document both builds"): Story's is 18 u/s² × (0.8 + 0.4 × Weight), weight-coupled and campaign-tuned, and the sim's a flat 30 u/s² with no weight term, because Fighter Mode is competitively normalized. The GDD's Gravity & Fall Speed section states both, so the base-gravity difference is no longer part of H-7 (what H-7 still holds is in the cross-mode parity bullet under Known prototype gaps). Story's own constants (base gravity 18 with the weight coupling, the short hop, the launch band, the float, the jump-launch factors, the 4 / 8-frame air ramps) and its per-tick step (`StepVertical`, which `PlayerController.ApplyGravity` calls) live in **`FTT.Core.StoryAirPhysics`**, plain C#, which also integrates jump arcs for level code, the bots and the content tests (`SingleJumpRise`, `MultiJumpRise`, `AirtimeFrames`, `JumpDistance`) — measure a reach through it, never through a hand-copied closed form. The sim's step lives in **`FTT.FighterSim.FighterAirPhysics`** (`StepMovement`, `StepLaunch`, `RampStep`; the old `ClampToTerminal` and `FighterMovementSystem`'s own gravity constants are gone), which the CPU's arc predictions and the recovery test harnesses call too. No component, snapshot field or protocol change; every Fighter hash moved. Measured: Story Einstein's single jump 181 → 186 px, airborne 58 → 69 ticks; the sim's 2.50 → 2.54 u, 50 → 52 ticks; hang ticks 7–10 → 14–24 across both modes; a sim fast-fall from the apex lands in 15–17 ticks (the old snap 9–10, a plain fall 23–26). The higher arcs moved three pieces of level geometry: Level 10 room 3's yard wall now rises to the bridging span's underside (`Level10Controller.YardWallTopY` 628, was 700), Level 9 room 4's east boundary wall runs from y 0 to `GroundY` (it stopped at 440, and five heroes could already double-jump out of the level over it), and Level 0's double-jump gate (the Level 0 bullet). Open consequences are `VERIFY-MOVEMENT-FEEL-FALLOUT`. Its first, the Level 7 / 10 rope chains (a hanging rope release floated into the next rope's solid bar and the Level 7 Joan bot run stopped completing), was fixed on 2026-10-09 by making the rope bar non-solid (the boss-arena bullet under Scene architecture); a human pass over both chains is still owed. **The 2026-10-16 combat and movement update** (both modes unless noted; every number provisional unless the user stated it): **the short hop is in both modes** (decision 5.1, `USER-SIM-SHORT-HOP`) through one constant, `UniversalMovementRules.ShortHopGravityMultiplier` 2.5 (`StoryAirPhysics.ShortHopGravityMultiplier` forwards), and the sim's `FighterAirPhysics.StepMovement(vy, fastFalling, floating, jumpedThisAirtime, jumpHeld, riseFromOwnJump)`, whose old four-argument overload is the held-Jump step, so the CPU's arc predictions, `CpuRecoveryProfile` and the test harnesses keep planning full hops; **the band cuts only a rise from the fighter's own Jump press this airtime** (Q5, `USER-SHORT-HOP-OWN-JUMP-ONLY`: a ground jump, an air jump or a ledge climb jump — never a post-hitstun rise, a kit lift such as the Tempest's or an airborne non-launching hit; Story's `StoryAirPhysics.SelectBand` gained the same gate through `PlayerController.RiseFromOwnJump`); and **the apex hang applies only while Jump is held** (6.1, `USER-APEX-HANG-HELD`), so a released short hop drops straight through its peak while a held jump keeps the hang. The sim reads Jump through component 323's `ActionFlags` bit 1 `JumpReleasedSincePress`, written from the tick's raw edges above the hitstop gate and every input discard (it equals "Jump not held" tick for tick for real input; hand-built frames with no `Released` never set it and keep full hops), and bit 3 `RiseFromOwnJump` (`PROVISIONAL-SIM-SHORT-HOP-LATCH`). Every hero's 1-tick tap rises 41.6–41.9 % of its held apex, below the decided ~45 % and recorded rather than tuned (Q4, `PROVISIONAL-SIM-SHORT-HOP-HEIGHT`; 2-tick taps 46.2–47.0 %, 3-tick 50.4–51.7 %). The sim's aerial Special step keeps the old hang with no short hop, and a Story aerial cast reads a frozen `_jumpHeld` (`VERIFY-AERIAL-SPECIAL-HANG-PARITY`); a Story movement ability's hang reads Jump live (CM-FIXB-STORY B11). **Corner forgiveness** (6.2, `USER-CORNER-FORGIVENESS`, `PROVISIONAL-CORNER-FORGIVENESS-RULES`): a rising Story hero in `Airborne`, an airborne swing or the air dodge, never under launch gravity, slides a head clip of up to `UniversalMovementRules.HeadClipNudgeUnits` 0.2 u (12 px) past a ceiling corner and pops onto a wall top up to `LipPopUnits` (10 px) above its feet (`PlayerController.CornerForgiveness.cs`, one `PrepareMoveAndSlide` call before `MoveAndSlide`). The sim has no partial ceiling for a nudge to act on (a guarded no-op, `FighterStageGeometryTests`) and gains the one-way **lip pop** instead (Q6, `USER-SIM-LIP-POP`, `PROVISIONAL-SIM-LIP-POP`): a normal-control fighter at its apex or falling, whose feet came from below a one-way platform's top and are now at most `SimOneWayLipPopUnits` 0.2 u under it, lands on the platform, resolved before the ledge capture; floor segments never pop and the drop-through window is exempt, so a few near-edge captures became landings. **Both drop-through inputs work in both modes** (6.3, `USER-DROP-THROUGH-BOTH-INPUTS`): a double tap of Down inside `UniversalMovementRules.DropThroughDoubleTapFrames` 18 (`StoryCombatRules.DownDoubleTapFrames` forwards; the sim's window is 323 `DownTapFrames`) and Down+Jump. Neither drops through the sim's solid base floor, and a Story Down+Jump on solid ground stays a jump. In a Story swing Down+Jump drops keeping the swing in startup and active frames and cancels it into `Airborne` in recovery and the chain hold, as the sim does (CM-FIXB-STORY B10); the Story double tap is still not read in the chain hold (`VERIFY-STORY-DOUBLE-TAP-CHAIN-HOLD`). The bots' flight model follows all of it (CLAUDE.md, `PROVISIONAL-BOT-FLIGHT-MODEL`).
