Delivery re-reads the step from current self-state against the decision-time gap (`ScheduledDecision.GatesBasicAttack` / `BasicAttackDeltaRaw`) and drops a press whose string ended in the reaction delay, walking in instead — no RNG draw, no snapshot state. Beyond the band and inside close range the CPU walks in. `CloseRange` still gates the threat roll and block, the Ultimate commit, the lockout wait, the grab, the close Special ladder and the Echo Step threat. `CpuDecisionObservation` gained `SelfStringReachWidthPercent`, `SelfNextStringStep` and `TargetHurtboxHalfWidthRaw`; a hand-built observation (reach 0) keeps the legacy close-range press. Every hit band of a profile at 100 % reach or more is at least 2.0 units (Tesla's and Tubman's opener band is exactly `FromInt(2)`), so **only CPU-driven Lincoln and Joan hashes moved**. The Level 13 Mirror supplies the hero's real 40 px hurtbox, read once per bound target. Suites: `CpuMeleeBandTests` (8, pure C#), one case in `MirrorParadoxTests`.
- **M05/M07 launches and knockdown landed 2026-09-27 (Package 12 W3b, both modes).** Only a hit authored `Launches = true` launches — the basic finisher, Up-Attack, Down-Air, the three throws, and the abilities `AbilityData.Launches` marks (W3's proposal, still `VERIFY-ABILITY-LAUNCHES`). Only a launch grants DI, tumble and the landing tech. A non-launcher on a **grounded** victim is grounded knockback (a horizontal slide, `Velocity.y = 0`, still grounded, no tumble), which is what re-opens the hit-2 Block escape (M06 follow-up); on an airborne victim it keeps its vector but is not a launch. The string's row is `BasicComboRules.StringHitLaunchesFor(profile, step)` and **Lincoln's hit 2 launches** (`BasicStringProfile.Hit2Launches`, M07), so his victims get DI and tech instead of the grounded escape. **A missed tech is a knockdown**, a sub-phase of `Stunned`: `BasicComboRules.KnockdownFrames` (30) fully invulnerable and action-locked frames after the landing, then a get-up chosen from the held direction on the knockdown's last frame — neutral `NeutralGetUpFrames` (10, in place) or roll `RollGetUpFrames` (14 of travel at the roll's speed), neither invulnerable, both action-locked; a hit during the get-up ends it. Sim: Klotho component **320** `FighterKnockdownComponent` + `FighterKnockdownRules` (invulnerability rides `InvulnerabilityFrames`, the action lock discards the tick's action buttons like the countdown does). Story: `PlayerController.IsKnockedDown` / `IsInKnockdownOrGetUp` / `GetUpKind`, the knockdown playing `hitstun` and the roll get-up `roll_recovery` (H03). Non-player sources (enemies, bosses, hazards) author no launch flag and keep the legacy "knockback launches" rule on the Story player (`PlayerController.ResolveHitLaunches`); Story mobs have no knockdown yet (`VERIFY-M05-KNOCKDOWN-SCOPE`). The CPU's DI hold now fires only while airborne in hitstun (the input-side read of "launched"). Handoff: `docs/handoffs/P12_W3b.md`. **Since 2026-10-16 tumble outlives hitstun, both modes, the Story hero included** (decision 3.2, "Smash rule, both modes"; `USER-TUMBLE-AFTER-HITSTUN`, `PROVISIONAL-TUMBLE-EXIT-ACTIONS`; Story mobs stay out, `VERIFY-M05-KNOCKDOWN-SCOPE`): "Only a launch grants DI, tumble and the landing tech" still holds, but a launched fighter whose hitstun ends in the air keeps tumbling (sim 310 `Tumble` value 2 `TumbleAfterHitstun`, set by `FighterVerbRules.UpdateTumble` at the top of the next unfrozen tick; Story `PlayerController.IsTumblingAfterHitstun`), with control back: any action — a jump, the air dodge, the air strike, a Special, the Ultimate, the movement ability, Echo Step, a ledge grab (`FighterVerbRules.ExitTumbleOnAction` from the action chokepoint; in Story any state but `Airborne` and `Stunned`) — leaves it, and drift and fast-fall keep it. Doing nothing lands in the tumble chain: an owed slam bounce, then the held-Block tech, else a knockdown (the sim's `TryLandingTech`, knockdown and slam rules key on the tumble, not the hitstun; Story's `TryResolveTumbleLanding`). A non-launching hit ends a tumble, inside the launch's hitstun or after it, in both modes (CM-FIXB-SIM B1), so a juggled victim hit by a non-launcher lands plainly; a Defy proc ends it too. Sim throws still launch and tumble; Story throws never tech (F23). `systems_card_tech_body` says so. **The landing tech and the knockdown lock every action** (decision 1.1(3), `FIX-SIM-TECH-LOCK-ACTIONS`): in the sim the tech discards input from its landing tick L and refuses Specials, grabs and the Ultimate for its 12-frame lock, and its invulnerability now ends with the lock (13 ticks counting L, not about 24; CM-FIXA-SIM ruling (b)); a missed tech's knockdown and get-up lock every action from L, hitstop thaw ticks included, keeping only the get-up direction read and a later hit's DI, and a knockdown interrupts a running Special cast (CM-FIXA-SIM ruling (a); `VERIFY-SIM-KNOCKDOWN-LANDING-TICK`, closed). The grabber's 12-frame throw-animation invulnerability now counts down and ends on the throw's own tick (`FIX-SIM-THROW-GRANT-OUTLIVES-THROW`). Suites: `FighterTumbleTests`, `StoryTumbleTests`.
- **The 2026-10-04 playtest feel pass, player side (workstream FEEL and fix-round items G2, G7, R11, R12; `docs/PLAYTEST_FEEL_2026-10-04_PLAN.md` §2.1 F1–F7, §3).** **F2 — one attack clock** (`F2-SINGLE-FRAME-CLOCK`): the factory no longer builds a `CombatAnimationPlayer` and `resources/Animations/placeholder_combat_animation_library.tres` is deleted (it was a second copy of the frame tables on an idle clock hitstop never paused); every Story swing, template-timed or authored-profile, runs on `PlayerController`'s physics frame clock. **F1 — posed sheets:** `FTT.Combat.AttackPoseRules` maps a phase to the retro sheet's three key poses (wind-up through startup, strike through active, follow-through through recovery); Story holds the sprite on the chosen frame for basics, directional strikes, Specials, the Ultimate and the Mirror's activation strike (`PlayerController.IsShowingAttackPose` / `ActiveAnimationFrame` / `PlayPresentationPose`), and `FighterSimulationDriver` poses Fighter swings from `AttackPhase`, Foresight from its phase, the whole activation strike from its phase (wind-up, strike, the cinematic on the strike pose, the whiff recovery on the follow-through, with no cosmetic hold trailing it — fix round R11) and sim Specials since 2026-10-16 from their cast phases on component 323 (wind-up, strike, follow-through; until then on-press Specials took thirds of the 18-frame cosmetic hold, `SIM-SPECIAL-POSE-THIRDS`, closed, and the thirds remain for the 0 / 1 / 0 fallback and the press-tick Ultimate edge); the movement ability is never posed in either mode — it plays as a reel (`FighterSimulationDriver.PosesAbilityHold`, R11). Presentation only, read-only over sim state. **F3 — chain buffer, both modes** (the grounded string only since 2026-10-12; the air strike drops a press made during it): a BasicAttack press in startup, **active frames** or the **hitstop freeze** buffers the next hit, a second press while one is buffered queues for the hit after (Story `_queuedAttackBuffered`; sim `FighterBasicAttackRules.FlagQueuedBuffered = 64`, a spare `AttackFlags` bit — no new component), and **holding** BasicAttack at the recovery end or through the chain hold continues the chain (`BasicComboRules.ChainContinues`); Time Freeze discards, never buffers. **F4:** the chain hold plays run/idle/jump/fall, the Story player plays `hitstun` (restarted by every new hit) / `dazed` / `roll_recovery` on the tech lock. **F5 — caster freeze:** `BaseSpecial.ConfirmAbilityHit` is the one landed-ability-hit feedback point — impact VFX and `EventBus.OnHitConfirm` per direct contact (deduplicated by `ContactId`) and the caster's hitstop once per execution (`ExecutionSerial` / `TryClaimCasterHitstop`, `PlayerController.ApplyAbilityCasterHitstop`). Since fix round R12 a pooled shot and the deferred second stages (the E=mc² burst, Yorick's wave, the Requiem Chord's burst pulses) carry the firing cast's serial (`HitPayload.SourceExecutionSerial`, stamped by `BaseSpecial.SpawnPlaceholderProjectile`) and claims are monotonic, so a late hit never spends a newer cast's freeze (`F5-CASTER-FREEZE-ATTRIBUTION`). Story authors Golden Ratio, the Requiem Chord and every Ultimate as direct hits, so they freeze, while the sim delivers them as hitstop-free zone pulses (`ZONE-RESOLVED-HIT-FREEZE`, open). Kit shape-query and ground-wave hits reach it through `Credit(payload, dealt, impactPosition)`, Hitbox-delivered ones (ability hitboxes, pooled projectiles) through `PlayerController.ConfirmAbilityHitboxHit`. Ticks, constructs and hazards confirm nothing; an `ExemptFromHitstop` direct hit still confirms (impact VFX and haptics) but freezes nobody. A frozen caster's **cast** freezes with it and nothing else does: `BaseSpecial.CastClockSuspended` (synced once per tick by the owner, so an N-frame freeze pauses the cast for exactly N ticks) stops the phase clock and the caster-bound kit work each kit gates on it — lunges and other owner velocity, phase-bound multi-hit cadences, counter-stance timers — while what the cast already put into the world (ground waves, the Vortex, the Rift, spirals, trap circles, Safe Passage's trail, the Conductor's Call rush and the Tempest's windbox on their own frame counters) keeps running through the freeze, as the sim's zone and projectile systems do. **F6 — `PROVISIONAL-HITSTOP-WEIGHT`:** `BasicComboRules.HitstopFrames(damage, launches)` — floor 3 → **4**, a launching hit adds **+3** (`LaunchHitstopBonusFrames`), capped at **11** (`HitstopCeilingFrames`); both modes call it (Story attacker/victim with the payload's launch flag, the sim with the hit's `launches`), and a killing blow on a Story mob still freezes the attacker. **F7 — provisional:** `HitPayload.SourceIsStandardMob` (stamped by `EnemyController` on its attack hitbox and by `EnemyAbilityExecutor` on its pooled shots) makes `ResolveHitLaunches` refuse to launch the Story player for a Standard-tier mob's hit (grounded hitstun and a slide, no tumble, no knockdown) unless the payload authors `Launches`; elites, bosses and hazards keep the legacy "knockback launches" rule — this amends the M05/M07 bullet above. **Fix round:** `UltimateMeter.SetValue` keeps `PlayerController.CurrentUltimateMeter` in step on every write (`SyncUltimateMeterFromNode`; an Ultimate's own `Consume` used to leave the field at 100, so Defy History could still fire and saves stored a full meter — G2); Idle and Running read the Down tap before the crouch, so a Story double-tap of Down drops through a one-way from standing and running, not only from Block or an attack (G7); and a released pooled shot is parked, not freed — still in the tree and in `story_projectile`, owner slot kept — so the Level 13 Mirror's incoming-fire scan skips any shot that is not `PlaceholderProjectile.IsLive`. The player swing's wall rule (M1) is in the boss-arena bullet under Scene architecture. Suites: `FeelPassRulesTests` (pure), `FeelPassStoryTests`, `FeelPassSimTests`.
- Fighter Mode is HP plus stock, not ring-out-only combat. A Time match is decided by fewest stocks lost with no HP tiebreak (F21 above); equal totals go to Sudden Death. A true tie is logged as a match but not a win or loss.

### Ability implementation

- Shared ability metadata belongs in `[GlobalClass] AbilityData : Resource`.
- Executable abilities derive from `BaseSpecial` and follow startup → active → recovery → cleanup phases.
- Character-specific classes live in `scripts/Characters/Abilities/` and use the `FTT.Characters.Abilities` namespace.
- Persistent constructs such as Tesla coils, Leonardo's turret, serpent nests, Mozart's staffs, projectiles, VFX, enemies, pickups, and damage numbers should use `PoolManager`/`IPoolable` rather than repeated instantiate/free churn during gameplay.
- Persistent objects must have stable owner/type/state data so Story rewind and Fighter rollback can snapshot them.
- **Kit payloads are stamped, never hand-classified (Package 12 W4).** Every kit script that builds its own `HitPayload` goes through `BaseSpecial.WithAbilityContract` / `Stamp` (the ability's authored `Origin`/`Delivery`/`Launches`, a fresh `ContactId`, `SourceActorId`, and the GAP-14 `PuzzleOnly` fence) and credits through `BaseSpecial.CreditDealt` / `Credit`, which read D03g/D03h off the payload. Since D3 (2026-10-11) a kit shape-query hit credits through `Credit(Hurtbox struck, in payload, dealt, position)`, which stamps `HitPayload.ContactLanded` from `struck.DidLand(ContactId)` (the hurtbox-less overload is deleted), and `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered` calls `ConfirmAbilityHitboxHit` for every contact, after the D1a stamp, so a fully absorbed ability contact reaches `BaseSpecial.LandedDirectHitThisExecution` (its feedback stays damage-gated). A source scan (`KitAlignmentContractTests`) fails on a bare `new HitPayload {` or a literal `AddInfluenceFromDamageDealt` under `scripts/Characters/Abilities/`. **Story kit damage goes through one composition on `PlayerController` (2026-10-11, ledger `FIX-STORY-DAMAGE-BUFF-KIT-ROUTING`):** the three Story attacker factors — `StorySpecialDamageMultiplier`, the ability's scoped `AbilityDamage` lane and the item-drop damage buff `StoryTemporaryDamageMultiplier` — compose once. A hit the kit builds and delivers itself (shape queries, ground waves, bursts, the Lorentz chain, every Ultimate, construct bites, arcs and the fence, zone ticks) uses `StoryAbilityDamage(base, abilityID)` / `StoryAbilityDamageScale(abilityID)`; a value a `Hitbox` carries (pooled projectiles via `BaseSpecial.SpawnPlaceholderProjectile`, the turret bolt, Lincoln's overhead, the factory kit boxes) takes only the build-time half, `StoryAbilityHitboxDamage(base)`, and `Hitbox.CreatePayload` alone adds the contact-time half, `StoryHitboxContactDamageScale(abilityID)`. So the drop buff now reaches every Special, Ultimate, construct and zone tick exactly once (before, only `Hitbox` hits and the throw read it). Nothing outside `PlayerController` reads the buff; `KitAlignmentContractTests.KitScriptsReadStoryAbilityDamageOnlyThroughTheOneComposition` fails on a kit reading any of the three factors directly. Agent-chosen consequences (`PROVISIONAL-STORY-ABILITY-DAMAGE-ROUTING`): the buff is sampled per contact (Lincoln's Emancipator now computes damage per contact); Mozart's Minor Chord Damage now scales the Requiem Chord's 3 × 4 burst as well as its contact; and the Mirror clone's F20 outgoing multiplier, which rides `StorySpecialDamageMultiplier`, now reaches its pooled-projectile contacts and its constructs. Fighter Mode is untouched. A movement ability may authorize one cast while its cooldown runs through `BaseSpecial.TryConsumeCooldownBypass` (Second Glide, Mozart's banked Extra Note charge), and such a cast uses `TryExecute(armCooldown: false)`.
- **Multi-frame kit motion is one rulebook in two modes (Package 12 W4, extended by Package 13).** `FTT.Combat.KitMotionRules` (plus W7b's `KitReachRules`) holds the constants; the `.tres` frame counts are pinned equal to them. The sim runs each as a sim-local phase code in the **existing** universal-movement slot of component 305 (`FighterKitMotion`) — no new component — so hits cancel them and they snapshot for free: **16–18** Tesla's Lightning Blink (6/12/10 frames, 3.0 units, +0.5 Story-only Long Blink; its projectile pass-through is derived from the phase, and a ground wave is deliberately not a projectile for it), **19–20 retired** (Pocahontas's Spirit Strike — never reuse), **21–22** Tubman's North Star Leap, **24** Divine Piercing's ~3-unit lunge, **25–26** Ascendant Wings' rise and held **Wing-Dive** (Attack cancels into the air strike since 2026-10-12, the aerial string before), **27** Desert Mirage's 4-unit 8-way **sand rush** (pushbox off, no invulnerability), **28** Lincoln's **Rail Charge** (5 units / 30 frames, armored only in travel, stops on contact; every sim `MovementType.Dash` is now this charge), **29** Sonata Drift's rising **glissando**, **30** Einstein's Relativity Warp **spacetime fold** (10 hittable startup frames, then an instant relocation up to 4 units and the float window), **31** Shakespeare's Prospero's Flight **gust burst**. Universal-movement phases **5–7** are the air dodge.
- **Package 13 kit changes, in brief** (W7a/W7b, per the character review; every number provisional under A07, nothing balanced): **Einstein** — E=mc² a straight 12 u/s shot, 7 contact + 20 burst in 1.2 u; the Rift thrown up to 5 u, ground-snapped, 1.5 u, a 0.5 s slow linger; **Rift Collapse** (an E=mc² burst inside his own rift pulls and launches); the Warp fold. **Leonardo** — Golden Ratio three ticks; the turret at his feet firing straight 12 u/s bolts only at an in-range target (4 bolts), and the **glide bolt in the sim** (D14). **Tesla** — coils at his feet, 4 u arcs; the sim fence lays the Conductive mark; Lorentz Pulse 2.5 u, **Root 1.0 s**, Lorentz Attraction +0.5 s (D12); the Lorentz chain fires an 8-damage direct arc per own coil within 8 u. **Shakespeare** — Yorick thrown straight into a 1.5 u wave; **the Tempest is a windbox** (push only: no charge, hitstun, hitstop, meter or Rally); the gust burst; the Story Major displays as Midsummer Gust. **Joan** — Righteous Smite a 2.5 u grounded ground wave; Divine Piercing a Shield-Breaker lunge of 3 × 8; Ascendant Wings rise + Wing-Dive. **Mozart** — Fortissimo a slow 1.5 u-tall wall of sound; Requiem Chord 14 u/s, 4 contact + 3 × 4 burst, shaving 2 s off Fortissimo once per execution; Sonata Drift's glissando with a half-cooldown staff refund once per airtime in **both** modes (D14). **Cleopatra** — the nest at her feet, 2.0 u wide; the Vortex thrown up to 5 u, radius 1.8; the sand rush. **Lincoln** — the Emancipator a 5 u grounded ground wave; Splitting Strike a 2.2 u arc that spikes airborne targets; Rail Charge. **Since 2026-10-16 Story movement abilities fall under Story gravity** except in the phases a kit drives itself (decision 1.1(1); `FIX-STORY-MOVEMENT-ABILITY-GRAVITY`, `PROVISIONAL-MOVEMENT-ABILITY-GRAVITY-SCOPE`): `BaseSpecial.SuspendsOwnerGravityNow` holds for Joan's Wings throughout, Tesla's Blink, Einstein's Warp and Mozart's Drift in startup and active, Tubman's Leap through its first recovery frame, Shakespeare's Flight and Cleopatra's Mirage in active, and Leonardo's Daedalus dive (Lincoln's charge never), and `BaseSpecial.OwnerFallSpeedCapNow` caps the descent, so Leonardo's boost peaks at 60.9 px and his glide sinks at exactly 40 px/s (`GlideSinkSpeedPixels`) instead of rising through the whole glide. Lincoln's Story Rail Charge writes only its horizontal speed, holds X through its startup, drives from its first active tick and stops when its recovery starts, so it travels exactly 300 px from the press, as the sim does (Q7 "Stop it like the sim"; `USER-RAIL-CHARGE-STOPS-LIKE-THE-SIM`, `FIX-STORY-RAIL-CHARGE-RECOVERY-CARRY`); Cleopatra's Desert Mirage holds X through its startup too (`FIX-STORY-DESERT-MIRAGE-STARTUP-CARRY`); and every Story kit motion now moves on its first active tick (Divine Piercing 165 → 180 px, Lightning Blink 165 → 180, North Star Leap 226.67 → 240, Desert Mirage 224 → 240, Sonata Drift 170 → 180; `VERIFY-STORY-KIT-MOTION-FIRST-TICK`, closed). Handoffs `docs/handoffs/P13_W7a.md` / `P13_W7b.md` carry the exact API names.
- The current `CharacterFactory` constructs placeholder character visuals and combat child nodes in code, but consumes canonical character/ability `.tres` tuning. Long-term presentation should move toward reusable authored scenes without reintroducing duplicate numbers.
- `PlaceholderProjectile`, `PlaceholderZone`, color rectangles, and code-generated labels are scaffolding. Do not mistake them for production art or final VFX.

### Status effects

**Two slots, and stronger-wins inside a slot** (Package 11 A1 — this supersedes the retired "one active status at a time / newest completely replaces the previous" rule). `FTT.Combat.StatusSlots` is the one data contract: a `Damage` slot and a `Control` slot, routed by the single compile-time table `FTT.Combat.StatusRouting.SlotOf`. Damage: `Venom`, `RadiantBurn`. Control: `TimeDilation`, `StaticCharge`, `Root`, `Suppression`.

Within a slot: a **different type always replaces**; the **same type replaces only when `newIntensity × newDuration >= currentIntensity × currentRemaining`**, bringing its own duration; a weaker same-type application is a **complete no-op** (no replace, no refresh — the occupant keeps its own remaining time). **The other slot is never touched.** Effects still do not stack, and there is no general cleanse or post-effect immunity. All four application sites share the rule: `StatusController.ApplyStatus`, `EnemyController.ApplyStatusEffect`, `BossController.ApplyStatusEffect`, `FighterDamageRules.ApplyStatus` (the last through `ShouldReplaceRaw`, an exact `int64` product of the FP64 raw intensity and the integer frame count — no division, no float, bit-reproducible across a rollback). `StatusController.IsDamageStatus` is retained as a forwarder to `StatusRouting.SlotOf`; there is only ever one routing table.

The canonical names are `TimeDilation`, `Venom`, `StaticCharge`, `RadiantBurn`, `Root`, and **`Suppression` (= 6, appended)**. Suppression is **Story-only**: 2.0 s standard duration, Control slot, and it refuses Special 1, Special 2, the Ultimate and the Movement Ability at the four cast sites **consuming neither cooldown nor meter** (cooldowns keep ticking). Basics, block, grab, Rally, DI, landing tech, Defy History, death rewinds and **Time Freeze** are untouched by it. `FighterDamageRules.ApplyStatus` **refuses `Suppression` outright**, so an authored `.tres` carrying it cannot enter deterministic state, and `EnemyController`/`BossController` refuse it too — it is a player verb. Presentation is `GlowPresentationController.SetAuraSmothered`, a separate channel that desaturates toward `GlowPalette.SuppressionColor` and restores the arbitrated layer intact when it lifts.

`FTT.Combat.IStatusEffectTarget` (`ActiveStatuses`, `ApplyStatusEffect`, `ClearStatusEffect(slot)`, `ClearAllStatusEffects()`, `TargetNode`) is implemented by `PlayerController`, `EnemyController` and `BossController`, and all three clear on death. It deliberately does **not** inherit a damage interface. **Package 12 W3 (M08) added `FTT.Combat.IDamageable`** (`float TakeDamage(in HitPayload)`, `bool IsAlive` — it returns the HP dealt, a deliberate deviation from the GDD's `void`), implemented by the same three controllers and by `Hurtbox` (so every `EnvironmentHurtboxAdapter` strike surface too); the two interfaces coexist. The M08 hit contract — `AbilityData.HitstunFrames` / `BlockClass` / `Launches` / `Delivery` / `Origin`, `HitPayload.SourceActorId` / `ContactId` / `Launches` / `Delivery` / `Origin`, and the pure `HitClassification` mappings — is recorded in `docs/handoffs/P12_W3.md`.

Update both snapshot paths and tests when changing status semantics.

### Combo marks — the F07 Conductive mark

A **mark is not a status**: no slot, no action lock, zero stagger budget, never refused by a V7.4 armor window. `ComboMarkType { None, Conductive }` lives in `BasicComboRules` alongside `ConductiveMarkBaselineFrames` (90), `ConductiveMarkUpgradedFrames` (150) and `ConductiveMarkFenceFrames` (90); `HitPayload.ComboMark` / `.ComboMarkFrames` carry it. Tesla's finisher applies Static Charge (**a pure interrupt now**) *and* a separate caster-owned Conductive mark, and `TeslaLorentzPulse` chains on the **mark**, not on Static Charge. The Story-only `tesla_conductive_hold` node extends it to 150 frames through its `AbilityDuration` / `conductive_finisher_mark` lane (+0.667), read once in `PlayerController.FinisherConductiveMarkFrames` (90 × 1.667, rounded). Until 2026-10-11 the node did nothing: the code checked a perk key the grid never granted and nothing read the lane (ledger `FIX-TESLA-CONDUCTIVE-HOLD-UNREAD`); the dead `conductive_hold` perk key is gone from `tesla_grid.tres`, so the mark cannot be extended twice. `ConductiveMarkUpgradedFrames` stays as the design target the tests check against; gameplay no longer reads it. The cross-mode constant is never edited for a Story perk, so Fighter Mode always marks at 90. Coil-fence marks are baseline and never extended. Tesla's death sweeps **his** marks off the `Players` and `Enemies` groups (T01a) while his coils survive. Sim side: `FighterConductiveComponent` is Klotho **ID 318**.

### Collision layers

Layers 1–11 are named Player, Enemy, PlayerHitbox, EnemyHitbox, PlayerHurtbox, EnemyHurtbox, Environment, OneWayPlatform, Trigger, PersistentObject, and Projectile. `CollisionLayers` centralizes constants and masks, and tests validate the matrix. Combatant body masks are `Environment | OneWayPlatform` only — **PersistentObject is non-solid** (Package 12 W8, M02): constructs, checkpoint fractures, extractors and other strike surfaces are struck, never stood on or walked into. Anything that must physically block a combatant authors itself on `Environment` (the Level 6 `MovableWeightTemplate` boulders moved there, so they also stop enemy projectiles now). Story kit specials/ultimates that deliver through a shape query rather than a `Hitbox` mask with `FTT.Combat.StoryShapeQuery.DeliveryMask(targetHurtboxLayer)` (= the slot's Hitbox mask), so they reach PersistentObject strike surfaces like a basic swing; the exceptions are the zero-damage displacement queries (Shakespeare's Tempest windbox) and the constructs' target-acquisition queries, and a source sweep in `ShapeQueryPropDeliveryTests` fails on a new bare-mask query. `Hurtbox.TakeHit` carries the world-hold, Time Freeze and `PuzzleOnly` discards for those deliveries. Player/Enemy body masks deliberately remain physically separate; child `CombatantPushbox` Areas use those body layers for explicit soft horizontal jostling without making hurtboxes solid or allowing combatants to become floors. Ordinary enemies are roll-through, while large bosses can set `BlocksRollThrough`. Update project settings, constants, scene masks, pushboxes, and tests together.

The Stasis Echo is **gone** (Package 11 A2 deleted the class): `PressurePlate` is weighted by the player and authored weights only, and no searchlight beam has an occluder any more.

## Story progression, rewind, and saves

- Story difficulty scales enemy HP/damage/spawn pressure/drop rates and Chronal Rewind behavior; it must not alter Fighter Mode balance.
- Rewind pools: Easy 5 with 70% HP restore (V7.2, down from 100%) and checkpoint refill; Normal 3 with 50% HP restore and +1 per new checkpoint; Hard 1 with 30% HP restore and no checkpoint refill during the level. **V7.3 (2026-08-26): the pool refills to the difficulty maximum on every fresh level entry** (`StoryManager.BeginLevelRun(resumeAttempt)`), while a mid-level resume keeps the parked pool. The **death** rewind is unchanged.
- **Time Freeze (F03) replaced the manual rewind outright** (Package 11 A2, 2026-09-13). The manual rewind, the Stasis Anchor, the Stasis Echo, the scrub API and the 12 s manual cooldown are **deleted, not deprecated** — `ChronalRewindManager`'s whole manual region, `StasisEcho.cs`, `IRewindScrubbable.CancelRewindScrub` and their translation rows are gone. In their place: `TimeFreezeController`, Story-only, **5.000 s = 300 ticks**, **no charges, no meter, no dust**, a **45 s cooldown armed at thaw** (including an early end), identical on all three difficulties, legal at **zero** death-rewind charges, and **not lockable by Suppression** (its eligibility rule takes no status at all). It never writes `SceneTree.Paused`; it ticks at `ProcessMode.Inherit`, so a paused tree stops both the duration and the cooldown for free, and the cooldown also pauses during a death rewind, the collapse beat and an active dialogue sequence.
  - **The world freeze** is `IStoryTimeFreezable` — deliberately *not* `IStoryRewindSimulation`, because the rewind freeze cancels attacks and zeroes velocity, which would destroy Time Freeze's "resume preserved positions, velocities and attack phases" contract. Swept groups: `Enemies`, `persistent_construct`, `story_hazard`, `puzzle_object`, `story_loot`, `rescuable_npc`, `time_freezable`, the mirror group. **`chronal_extractor` is deliberately absent — Timeline Integrity keeps draining.** Pooled projectiles and zones freeze in place through a `ProcessMode.Disabled` fallback (nothing is cleared), and every `ProcessMode` write goes through `PhysicsSafeSetters`. A room revealed mid-freeze is frozen before its actors take a tick.
  - **Enemy invulnerability is one rule at two sites** — `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered`, and since Package 12 W8 `Hurtbox.TakeHit` too, because kit shape-query deliveries bypass `Hitbox`: a player-sourced hit on a non-player target is discarded — no damage, no stagger, no Rally echo, no meter. Because checkpoint fractures and extractors are `EnvironmentHurtboxAdapter`s at owner index −1, that same gate blocks checkpoint activation and extractor destruction during a freeze.
  - **The player mask** allows Move/Jump/Down/Roll and refuses, **without buffering**, basic attacks, grabs, Special 1/2, the Ultimate, Echo Step and the Movement Ability. Ability cooldowns, block-charge regen, status durations and `InteractionArea.TryInteract` are all suspended.
  - New freezable world objects implement `IStoryTimeFreezable` (latch a flag, mutate nothing else) and join one of `TimeFreezeController.FrozenTimeGroups`, or `FreezableGroup` if the class has no natural group.
- **Checkpoints are strike-to-activate (V7.3, uniform incl. Level 00/01):** `CheckpointTrigger`'s Area2D is only a proximity prompt; activation requires hitting the fracture's PersistentObject-layer `StrikeSurface` (an `EnvironmentHurtboxAdapter`, player attacks only — enemy hitboxes share the -1 owner index and are skipped). The entry checkpoint (`*_checkpoint_0`) self-activates via `SelfActivating`. Activation raises a **two-beat event pair**: `OnCheckpointReached`/`OnCheckpointActivated` (gameplay: state capture, rewind refresh gated on the payload's `FirstActivation`, HUD) then `OnCheckpointCommitted` (persistence: `SaveManager.SaveCheckpoint`), so the save always records the post-refresh pool. The once-per-attempt authority is `StoryManager.TryActivateCheckpoint` (persisted; gates the Mending heal + rewind refresh; respawn/save update every activation). Since 2026-10-12 (FIX-E) a fracture past an uncleared encounter lock refuses activation outright — no Mending, refresh, bank, event or save (`CheckpointTrigger.IsHeldByEncounterLock`; Scene architecture's encounter-lock bullet).
- **Checkpoints carry an authored role (F12, Package 11 A3).** `FTT.Environment.CheckpointRole` is `Entry = 0 | Middle = 1 | PreBoss = 2`, authored alongside the stable ID; `BuildCheckpoint(x, y, id, role)` **requires** it at every campaign call site. **No code may infer a role from an ID suffix** — that is exactly what would have disabled the (now retired) Level 4A PreBoss anchor, whose ID ended `_1`. Hard's middle fracture is **inert in Acts I–II only** (Act III is exempt, so all three Act III anchors are live on Hard); an inert anchor activates nothing, anchors no respawn, registers no F11 budget and reads dead grey. **PreBoss activation freezes the Integrity gauge permanently** and banks the final gate value; Entry and Middle bank the checkpoint allowance. An ID with no registered role resolves to `Entry`, which is the safe default because Entry can never lock the boss clock.
- **F11 recovery minima.** `TimelineIntegrityRules.RequiredRecovery` / `RecoveryMinimum` / `TimerRecoveryIntegrity`, computed in `double` with a 1e-6 rounding epsilon. **A budget above 100 is an authoring error and throws** — it never clamps. Only a `TimelineCollapseCause.Timer` recovery reads it, and the resolved value is written into both `LevelIntegrityPercent` and `CheckpointIntegrityPercent` before the save, so an interrupted recovery loads the already-resolved result and can never grant twice.
- **F16 lethal Story pits.** `PlayerController.KillPlayerNonHit()` is the **only** non-hit death chokepoint: HP → 0 unconditionally, bypassing the Story shield, roll/tech/post-rewind invulnerability and **Defy History** (whose used flag is untouched). No damage is applied, so there is no Rally echo, no victim meter and no hitstop. `StoryKillBoundary` (Area2D, group `story_kill_boundary`) and `StoryLevelControllerBase.BuildKillBoundary` are the authoring helpers. **Zero boundaries are authored today** — every campaign gap is explicitly documented as non-lethal — which is the open `VERIFY-STORY-PITS` ledger entry, not an omission.
- **Per-attempt state persists across a mid-level quit-and-resume (V7.3, save schema v5; the registries below are now mirrors of the F10 `AttemptState` record — see the F10 bullet):** `StoryManager` owns per-attempt registries (activated checkpoints, destroyed extractors, found secrets, Restoration Font uses, live Timeline Integrity) written into the save at every checkpoint (`WriteAttemptStateToSave`) and on collapse; `BeginLevelRun` restores them on resume (detected as "active save parked on this scene with a non-empty `LastCheckpointID`" — level completion and Restart both clear that field) and clears them on fresh entry. Scene-side, `StoryLevelControllerBase.ApplyResumedAttemptState` rebuilds destroyed extractors via `ChronalExtractor.RestoreDestroyedState()` (no dust, no +3%, no re-registration) and marks found secrets via `SecretCache.MarkAlreadyFound()` (collected through the `secret_cache` group). Collapse resume-from-anchor keeps the attempt; Restart Level and `ResetCampaignState` clear it (the latter also clears the pending-restart/collapsed fields and the boss-intro seen set).
- **Timeline Integrity is the level timer (F01, Package 11 A3).** The whole V7.1/V7.3 siphon model is **deleted**: the 600 px engagement, the 10 s grace, the 10-point siphon-share cap, `+3%` per destroyed extractor, `+2%`/`+5%` per secret, `RestoreTimelineIntegrity` and `ChronalRatingRules` are all gone. **There is no restoration path — nothing adds a point back.** The clock lives on `StoryManager`, ticks in its own `_PhysicsProcess`, and drains **globally from level load** — no engagement, no room entry, no grace — normalized against an authored per-level `ParSeconds` with difficulty multipliers **Easy 2.0 / Normal 1.5 / Hard 1.2** (and, since 2026-10-12, `TimelineIntegrityRules.ProvisionalParScale` 1.5, applied once where `StoryManager` receives par — `BeginIntegrityClock` and the F11 `SetRecoveryRouteSeconds` — until par is re-measured). The denominator is the **authored starting Extractor count, fixed at load and never recalculated from survivors**; destroying a machine lowers the *rate* (`ExtractorDrainWeight` 0.2, `MinimumRawFactor` 0.9) and leaves the gauge where it is, with a single non-numeric `integrity_drain_slowed` notice. Finding the secret applies `SecretDrainReduction` (0.1). **Collapse at zero on every difficulty.** Tier lines moved 90/70 → **50/20**; `EndingThresholdPercent` 85 → **50**. A par run ends at 50 % / 33.33 % / 16.67 % (66.7 / 55.6 / 44.4 % under the provisional scale). The clock pauses through the `IntegrityClockPause` flag set (`Dialogue | PauseMenu | DeathRewind | BossIntro | ScriptedBeat`, the last appended by Package 13 W4 for scripted beats such as the Level 9 near-capture), and the "whoever pauses hands it back, including on teardown" rule extends to it.
- **The Collapse Tremor** (`CollapseTremorController` + `CollapseTremorRules` + pooled `FallingDebris`) runs at Integrity **< 20 %** (continuous shake 0.1, a 0.3 × 0.4 s jolt every 6–8 s, debris ~every 4 s) and **< 10 %** (jolts every 3–4 s, debris ~every 2 s). Debris telegraphs for 0.75 s, deals **5 % of max HP**, is blockable (since 2026-10-16 as a Hazard-class hit through `ApplyEnvironmentalHit`, after the invulnerability gate, D04 and Aegis; until then a Basic-class hit through `ApplyEnvironmentalDamage`), never exceeds **2** airborne, never lands inside a Restoration Font radius or on an activated fracture, and **never touches enemies**. It is attached by `StoryLevelControllerBase` on timed levels only (so the Tutorial and Florence opt out by not extending it), stops whenever the clock pauses, and **retires permanently at the PreBoss lock**. All shake goes through `CameraShake`, so the accessibility screen-shake scale (0 = off) already applies — and Tremor *timing* is never reduced by it.
- **Restoration Fonts are placed (D13, 2026-10-11; `USER-RESTORATION-FONT-EVERY-LEVEL`, placement and use rules `PROVISIONAL-FONT-PLACEMENT`).** One in every campaign level 0–15, built by `RestorationFont.Create(fontID, position)` (ID `{DialoguePrefix}.restoration_font`, group `restoration_font`, a `RadialProgress` channel display): Levels 2–15 through `StoryLevelControllerBase.RestorationFontPosition`, Florence and the Tutorial through their own controllers; the Tutorial's appears only when the Combat Trial starts. The tested rule puts a font past the Middle anchor + 110 px with its channel circle outside the boss reveal, clear of checkpoints, Extractors / hold nodes and other Interact targets, and (since the 2026-10-12 fix-up, the shared test helper `FontHazardClearance`) with the hero's body anywhere in the channel circle 20 px clear of every damaging hazard, dormant phase-armed ones included (that check moved three fonts: Florence's to (9720, 900) between the spike strip and the steam geyser, Level 7's to (8700, 880) and Level 14's to (9850, 1700) between Security Core lanes 1 and 2; every position is in the ledger row); since 2026-10-12 the encounter locks moved six more — the Tutorial's to (2450, 900), Florence's to (10530, 900), Level 5's to 9200, Level 7's to 9100, Level 9's to 8640 and Level 13's to 6970 — and no font's channel circle may straddle a lock barrier (`FontHazardClearance.CheckLockBarriers`), nor its footprint stand inside a lock area; Levels 12 and 13 are low-gravity exceptions, and the Level 12, 14 and 15 fonts are no longer post-wave heals, because their wave 4 now folds into the last approach lock. A font refuses during its own running restore and after completion (`RefusesWhile`); there is no boss-fight refusal. The channel commits its use, pending heal and the wallet together (`StoryManager.CommitFontChannelCompletion`) and credits per tick; a death (the font's `EventBus.OnPlayerDied` hook and, for a reload-restored remainder no font tracks, `ChronalRewindManager.OnPlayerDied`), a Collapse or a Snap cancels the remainder without refund. Applying a pending heal on reload is `VERIFY-FONT-PENDING-HEAL-RESUME`; Chronal Feasts and Story orbs are still unplaced (`DEFER-CHRONAL-FEAST-PLACEMENT`).
- **`VERIFY-PAR-SECONDS`.** Every `ParSeconds` and every F11 `remainingRouteSeconds` fraction in the tree is **provisional and seeded, never measured** (par = authored room count × 90 s rounded up to 30 s; route fractions Entry 0.90 / Middle 0.45 / PreBoss 0.00). Do not quote them as measured numbers. Since 2026-10-12 the clock runs on the authored par × `TimelineIntegrityRules.ProvisionalParScale` (1.5, the F11 route budgets included), because the encounter locks, the speed cut and turn-taking lengthen every run; the Phase C bot batch's review of the scale is recorded in the ledger row.
- **Timeline Collapse earns a beat (V7.3):** `ChronalRewindManager` runs a 4 s fracture presentation over the frozen world (`CollapseBeatSeconds`; `RewindPresentationOverlay` shows the monochrome/crack treatment) before `BeginTimelineCollapse` extracts to the hub. The first viewing is unskippable; once `StoryManager.HasSeenCollapseBeat` (persisted on the save), Jump/Interact skips.
- **The abnormal-exit fee is retired (V7.6 ruling 2.B, Package 11 A3): crashes are free.** `SessionExitGuard` (Core) still writes `user://saves/session.marker` on StartCampaign/ResumeCampaign, refreshes it at every checkpoint save, and clears it on clean shutdown — but the marker survives only as **F10's attempt-status router**. `ApplyAbnormalExitFee` is deleted and `SaveManager._Ready` now calls `ConsumeAbnormalExitMarker()`, which reads and clears the marker and charges **nothing**; `save_notice_abnormal_exit_fee` is gone from `en.csv`. The **voluntary** 20 % pause-menu exit fee is unchanged. The completion deposit is atomic — `DepositDustToActiveSave` zeroes `save.LevelChronalDust` in the same envelope write.
- **Chronal Rating is retired (V7.6 ruling 2.A).** `ChronalRatingRules` is deleted, `results_rating` is gone, and `LevelResultsPanel.RatingLine` / `StoryManager.LastLevelChronalRating` are removed. `StorySaveData.RatingByLevel` survives as **documented dead data** through schema v7 — deleting a payload field is a breaking change neither Package 11 nor Package 12 took. Nothing may start writing it again. The tier helpers stayed on `TimelineIntegrityRules`; no `IntegrityTierRules` type exists.
- **Respec is free (V7.3):** `ResonanceProgression.RespecAll` refunds every spent point and clears the grid; the grid panel carries a "Respec (full refund)" button behind the shared ConfirmModal idiom. Since 2026-10-11 a respec (like a purchase) reaches the live campaign hero at once and never heals it (the Resonance Grid bullet below, `FIX-GRID-LIVE-APPLY`).
- **The Act III gauntlet (Package 11 A3b).** Levels 13–15 chain with no hub return; `StoryLevelControllerBase.PresentCompletion` routes an Act III results panel's Return to `LoadCurrentLevel()`. **Anchor charges** are Easy 3 / Normal 2 / Hard 1, initialized **only** on fresh entry or an explicit full Restart Level (never on a reload, a checkpoint, a hub return or a Snap), published as `EventBus.OnAnchorChargesChanged`. **Anchor Snap** replaces the Acts I–II hub extraction: same attempt, one charge spent, the same 20 % undeposited-dust fee, full HP, full difficulty rewind pool, the checkpoint Integrity allowance, the recovery event committed **before** presentation, pending healing cancelled without refund, and the level reconstructed in place. Spending the last anchor (1 → 0) still completes the Snap. With no anchors left it is **Smothered** — the game's only Game Over (`scenes/ui/GameOver.tscn`, Restart Level / Quit to Menu), persisted **before** its presentation, charged exactly once, and re-entered by every later load until an explicit Restart. `BeginTimelineCollapse` branches to `ResolveActIIIFailure` for Levels 13–15, so both designed triggers (timer-zero and a lethal event with no rewind charge) are caught at one chokepoint.
- **The Warden Beacon (F02)** is authored at every live Act III anchor by `BuildCheckpoint`. It opens the existing Repository at any **activated** Act III checkpoint and **deliberately offers no Deposit action** — `ResonanceProgression.SpendableBalance(save, characterID)` is the single statement of the rule: deposited dust only, never the active level's `LevelChronalDust`. "Data crosses the field, matter doesn't": Level 13's earnings first become spendable at Level 14's Beacon.
- **The Resonance Hold.** Act III keeps the Integrity clock, but it measures the hero's own resonance. `ResonanceHoldNode : ChronalExtractor` supplies the drain stand-ins (`SeveredConduit` L13, `CradleIntakeValve` L14, `AnchorPylon` L15), carrying the identical drain weight and dust share and keeping the `chronal_extractor` group so the freeze sweep, the F01 denominator and the drain factor are unchanged. **Since Package 12 W8 (M16) a stand-in is not smashed:** it has no HP and no discharge, and the player holds Interact for 180 frames beside it (130 px range, `RadialProgress` UI). The channel resets on damage, range, release, stun/daze/death, Time Freeze, the Post-Landing Hold or `IsWorldHeld`, while Integrity keeps draining. Completion runs the Extractor destruction path once (drain-factor drop, the F05 pickup against its ObjectID, the destroyed registry). **The candle rule:** the variants touch no player stat, ability or damage value — the hero's full kit works identically at 1 % as at 100 %.
- Rewind state records at 60 Hz into a **five-second/300-frame** buffer (2026-09-14, cut from 8 s by user direction; 8 s had itself been cut from 15 s on 2026-08-15, and 15 s raised from 5 s on 2026-08-11) and lands on the grounded frame nearest the full buffer depth — **not** the most recent grounded frame: that original rule degenerated to a zero-distance rewind for any death on solid ground, which read as the character freezing with no rewind ever happening (`ChronalRewindTests` pins the depth semantics, `DeathTriggeredRewindTests`/`LevelDeathRewindDiagnosticTests` pin the death-triggered liveness, including inside a real Level 2 boot). A last-known grounded fallback survives beyond the buffer; if none exists, use the physical checkpoint. **Pacing (2026-08-15 rework):** the whole mechanic lasts **half the rewound duration** (`ChronalRewindManager.MechanicDurationFraction`; a full 5 s history is a 2.5 s mechanic, a 3 s history 1.5 s) and opens with a **0.75 s / 45-frame hold** (`PreRewindHoldFrames`: world frozen, player suspended in the death pose, `Started` presentation live, no frame played back yet) before `Playback` begins; playback then walks the **full-resolution** history (stride 1, `PathIndexForTick` linear pacing, under 3 frames per tick for a full buffer, floored at 30 ticks for a young buffer) so motion is continuous rather than the old 12-frame teleport scrub that finished 15 s in ~1.25 s and read as glitchy. **The landing hands control straight back (2026-09-14, user direction):** there is no post-landing input lock. **R02 (Package 12 W1):** `CompleteStoryRewind(landing, hp, grounded)` revives through the explicit Story edge `Dead -> Idle/Airborne` (chosen by the landing sample's floor contact) and never enters or exits `Respawning`, which is Fighter-only; `PlayerController.StateTransitioned` makes both edges observable. **R03 Post-Landing Hold (Package 12 W1):** every Story recovery — the death rewind, the Level 0 scripted rewind, the in-session Collapse resume-from-anchor and the Act III Anchor Snap — ends in a `ChronalRewindManager.PostLandingHoldFrames` (60) hold: the hero is live, the world is frozen. The frozen set is the union of the Time Freeze and rewind sweeps (`PostLandingHoldGroups`, plan D3(a) — Extractors, the Mirror and the hero's constructs included; a room revealed mid-hold freezes through `TimeFreezeController.FreezeActivatedRoom`). The shared query `ChronalRewindManager.IsWorldHeld(tree)` gates `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered` **and** `Hurtbox.TakeHit` (which the kit's shape-query deliveries use), so frozen actors are invulnerable and give no damage, meter, Rally, checkpoint or extractor credit. Time Freeze (`CanActivate(..., worldHeld)`), `InteractionArea` (via `PlayerController.IsRecoveryWorldHeld`) and pickups wait for the thaw. Every clock runs: Integrity resumes at the landing, and the Time Freeze cooldown and the hero's timers tick. The 2 s protection is armed **at the thaw** (`PlayerController.EndRecoveryHold`), never at the landing. `EventBus.OnRecoveryLanded` / `OnRecoveryWorldThawed` (`RecoveryHoldPayload`, published by `PlayerController`) bracket the hold; the reused Time Freeze thaw warning fires `PostLandingThawWarningFrames` (30, provisional) before the thaw through `AudioManager.PlayCriticalCue` and survives Reduced Temporal Effects; the recovery music duck is held by the `Landed` payload and released by the new `RewindPresentationPhase.Thawed`. A boss whose T01b beat was running when the hero died resumes its world suspension after the thaw. The hold is never persisted: `StoryManager` arms the Collapse/Snap placement hold in memory only, stamped with its scene, and a save load, a fresh entry or a restart clears it. The death rewind keeps the attack-cancelling rewind freeze for its own groups at initiation (its unfreeze is a pure latch, so the hold has no catch-up); the Snap/Collapse holds and every other group use the Time Freeze suspend path. A death inside the hold, a collapse beat and `_ExitTree` all end it (the manager releases everything it froze on teardown). `StoryLevelControllerBase` still re-confines the camera to the room containing the landing (a 5 s rewind can cross forward-only room transitions).
- **Chronal Dust per-source awards come from an authored manifest (F05, Package 11 A10).** `resources/Content/reward_manifests/` holds 16 `LevelRewardManifest` resources (levels 0–15; the nine per-hero 4A manifests are deleted), compiled to a per-difficulty ledger by `LevelRewardDirectory` through the pure `RewardAllocator` (floor + largest remainder, ties by stable source ID, fixed shares, even split, tier bonus). **An unauthored source pays nothing** — it never falls back to a flat value, which retires "every defeated enemy drops dust": a boss summon, an endlessly repeatable reinforcement, or a restored enemy whose source is already issued draws zero and spawns no pickup. **A difficulty changes the distribution, never the level total.** `EnemyData.ChronalDustDrop` and `ChronalExtractor.DustReward` are demoted in place to **advisory fallbacks** for unledgered contexts (Test Arena, unit harnesses), not deleted. **All fifteen bosses pay 25**, including `BossData`'s default, and the Mirror Paradox has no wallet-direct path left. Only the boss still forces a Large milestone plate; an ordinary Extractor shows the icon its actual award earns. `LevelRewardDirectory.ResolveBossAward` is the single boss rule — do not re-inline it at either call site. A run visits Levels 1–15: 15 boss awards = 375, required 345, optional 280 → **720 base / 1,000 thorough**, unchanged by S27 because Level 4A's row folded into **Level 5 = 55 required / 25 boss / 30 optional**, with a `level_05.eraser_debut@0:unbound_eraser` source (`DustEconomyTests` pins the arithmetic; `docs/DUST_ECONOMY.md` mirrors it). **Mob dust never expires (D12, 2026-10-11, `USER-DUST-NEVER-EXPIRES` / `PROVISIONAL-DUST-SETTLE-RULES`):** a kill drop that reaches its 10 s timer (`ChronalDustPickup.AutoCollectSeconds`, paused under every freeze) collects itself — claim, wallet and attribution, through the idempotent `Collect()`. `LevelManager.CompleteLevel` banks every live dust pickup under the level — mob drops and (the addendum's default) boss, Extractor and Secret awards left on the ground — through `ChronalDustPickup.SettleOutstandingDust`, then sets the scene-local `LevelManager.IsComplete` (group `level_manager`); from then on no pickup pays and `StoryDropSystem` issues no kill dust (`LevelManager.IsLevelCompleteFor`), because completion has deposited the wallet and re-keyed the reward directory. A Collapse, Snap, exit or crash still drops outstanding mob pickups unpaid and re-issuable (`VERIFY-PENDING-MOB-PICKUP-RECOVERY`; the ≤ 10 s exposure is provisionally accepted). **The results panel adds up to the bank** (`RESULTS-DUST-LINES-AFTER-RESET`, fixed; `PROVISIONAL-RESULTS-CARRIED-DUST`): its tier-bonus, losses and new carried lines read `StoryManager.LastLevelTierBonusDust`, `LastLevelDustLost` and `LastLevelDustCarriedIn`, frozen before the completion's attempt reset (`LevelRewardDirectory.LastTierBonusDust` is deleted). The loss tally is keyed to the paying attempt's GUID (`RecordAttemptDustLoss`, which the voluntary exit fee now reaches too) and carried across that attempt's resume reset; `LevelDustCarriedIn` is the wallet at the load plus the losses already taken (`results_dust_carried`, hidden at 0), so categories + carried + bonus − losses equals the deposit. The tally is not persisted: after a process restart earlier fees fold into the carried line.
- **Dust banks only at level completion (H02, 2026-09-26; Package 12 W2).** `StoryManager.CommitCompletionTransaction` deposits the retained wallet plus tier bonus for **every** level, exactly once; the hub's old arrival auto-deposit and the Repository-interact deposit are deleted, and Acts I–II no longer bank at the hub. A hub return after a Collapse or a voluntary exit deposits nothing: the post-fee dust stays in the open attempt's wallet (`save.LevelChronalDust`, `StoryManager.AttemptHeldDust`) until that level is completed, the exit/Collapse 20% rule applies to the whole wallet, and a full Restart Level (pause menu, hub restart-from-beginning, Game Over) clears it. The hub's dust line now shows the deposited spendable balance, and a Repository ledger label shows the last completion's bank (`hub_repository_last_banked`) and any held attempt dust (`hub_repository_attempt_held`); `hub_dust_deposited_toast` is an arrival notice for a deposit that already happened. The v6→v7 reconciliation for a completion saved before the hub loaded is `StoryAttemptState.ReconcileUndepositedCompletion`, wired into the single v6→v7 step by Package 12 Phase C (it runs before the attempt record is minted).
- Chronal Dust funds the selected character's Story-only Resonance Grid. Progress and unlocked node IDs belong to the story save slot. **All nine grids were re-authored wholesale for V7.6** (Package 11 A4): nine **unique topologies** (Einstein mesh, Joan spine, Leonardo interlocking gear rings, Tesla two-rail circuit, Shakespeare Three Acts, Mozart ascending scale, Cleopatra converging delta, Lincoln split-rail fence, and Tubman's **The Crossing** — two roads that cross, on Pocahontas's retired shape and prices), **9 nodes each at 3/3/3 per tier, 50/75/200 = 975 unchanged**. Nodes carry an explicit `Tier`, a `PrerequisiteMode` (`All` | `Any`), an `AbilityScope`, a `GatedAbilityID` and a normalized `LayoutPosition`; `ResonanceNodeType` gained `Traversal = 2` and `ResonanceUnlockResult` gained `AbilityLocked`. Stat keys live in `ResonanceStatKeys` and ability-scoped lanes are read through `PlayerController.StoryScoped(key, abilityScope)` — the scoped bucket is copied in one assignment by `CharacterFactory`, never as a second per-ability field. Every retired key (`Armor`, `BlockDurability`, `BlockRecovery`, `ComboSpeed`, `JumpForce`, `ProjectileSpeed`, …) is gone, every root node is ungated and modifies a Level 0 system, and **all nine traversal flags are wired across all nine kits**. The panel is a real topology renderer: chips placed at their authored positions over a `ResonanceEdgeLayer` drawing **solid = All-of, dotted = Any-of, glowing = satisfied**, with a gated node shown as a dim unnamed **dormant star in its true position**. Eight Majors were reworked. `CharacterFactory` populates `PlayerController.StoryAbilityPerks` from the active save (Story-only; abilities gate bespoke behavior via `HasStoryPerk`).
  - **Grid repairs (2026-10-11, ledger section "the Resonance grid repairs").** A scoped `CooldownReduction` now resolves to the cooldown *multiplier* `max(0.05, 1 − sum)` (`ResonanceProgression.MinimumCooldownMultiplier` is the one floor, also read by the character-wide lane); it used to resolve to `1 + sum`, so Joan's Piercing, Mozart's Wave and Lincoln's Splitting cooldown minors made their Special 2 cooldown 10 % **longer** (`FIX-GRID-SCOPED-COOLDOWN-INVERTED`). Every other scoped lane still resolves to `1 + sum`. Tesla's Conductive Hold now reaches 150 frames (the F07 section, `FIX-TESLA-CONDUCTIVE-HOLD-UNREAD`) and his Pulse Radius widens the Lorentz Pulse to 172.5 px through `TeslaLorentzPulse.PulseRadius` (`FIX-TESLA-PULSE-RADIUS-UNREAD`). **The dormant-node gate is live** (`FIX-GRID-DORMANT-GATE-UNINSTALLED`): with no test override, `ResonanceProgression.IsAbilityUnlocked(save, characterID, slot)` reads the evaluated save's Legacy Unlock state through the engine-free `LegacyUnlockSchedule.UnlockedSlotsFor` / `IsUnlockedFor`, which `CharacterFactory.ResolveStoryUnlockedSlots` now also calls, so the hero's ability gate and the grid's dormant stars cannot disagree; a gated node returns `AbilityLocked` and draws as the unnamed star. `AbilityUnlockProbe` survives as a test override only; already-bought gated nodes stay bought (`PROVISIONAL-GRID-DORMANT-GATE-SOURCE`). One leak the live gate exposed is open: a visible node's missing-prerequisite tooltip still names a dormant prerequisite (`VERIFY-GRID-DORMANT-NAME-IN-PREREQ-TEXT`). Mozart's Minor Resonance is wired (Combat, the meter bullet). `ResonanceReaderCoverageTests` fails on an authored stat key, ability scope or perk key with no production reader, on a scoped cooldown minor that does not resolve into [0.05, 1), and on anything under `scripts/FighterSim/` naming a Resonance or Story-perk type.
  - **Purchases and respecs apply to the live hero at once (2026-10-11, ledger `FIX-GRID-LIVE-APPLY`, agent-chosen rules `PROVISIONAL-GRID-LIVE-APPLY`).** `ResonanceProgression.TryUnlock` and `RespecAll` raise `EventBus.OnResonanceProgressionChanged(characterID)` (`TryUnlock` still raises `OnTalentNodeUnlocked` for the autosave). Only the campaign hero listens (`PlayerController.ReceivesLiveStoryProgression`, set on the full Story path `applyStoryProgression && applyLegacyUnlockLocks`), so the Mirror clone, Fighter Mode, the Holodeck, the drills and the Test Arena never re-resolve. It re-resolves through `CharacterFactory.ReapplyStoryProgression`, which runs **`CharacterFactory.ApplyActiveStoryProgression`, the same method the build uses** (every stat lane, the scoped bucket and the perk set), refreshes the idle factory kit hitboxes, then `PlayerController.SettleLiveStoryProgression`: HP and block charges are never raised, and are clamped when a cap falls (`BlockSystem.SetMaxCharges`); the HUD gets a zero-damage `OnPlayerHPChanged`. A request that arrives mid-cast or mid-swing (`HoldsLiveStoryProgression`: any ability slot executing, or `CharacterState.Attacking` including the chain hold) waits in `LiveStoryProgressionPending` and lands on the first free tick. The Legacy locks, Defy, the meter and running cooldown timers are untouched; things a cast already put in the world keep their spawn-time values. Suite: `ResonanceLiveApplyTests` (6, real frames at a Warden Beacon).
  - **The AttackRange minors move the far edge by their full percentage** (Leonardo Brush Reach +8 %, Shakespeare Quill Reach +10 %, Lincoln Rail Reach +10 %; ledger `FIX-REACH-MINOR-FORWARD`): `StoryAttackRangeMultiplier` scales a chain box's width and forward X offset together, so Lincoln's hit-1 far edge goes 81.6 → 89.76 px (the old rule gave 84.66, +3.75 %). It still scales box height, and applies to Story's air-strike box too (the aerial chain boxes it used to scale were retired on 2026-10-12); the centred Up-Attack and Down-Air keep growing about their centre (`PROVISIONAL-REACH-MINOR-SHAPE`).
- There are three story slots plus global settings/statistics. Autosave occurs at checkpoints, level completion, and unlock events. The main menu supports localized slot summaries plus confirmed deletion of the exact primary/backup/temp/corrupt candidates for a slot.
- **F10 attempt persistence (Package 11 A3b).** An attempt is `(slot, levelID, attemptID)`; `StoryAttemptState.CreateFresh` mints an ID from **exactly two** callers — fresh entry into a level and an explicit full Restart Level. A scene reload, a hub return, a checkpoint, a death and an Anchor Snap all keep the same ID. `Revision` is monotonic and every committed change moves it once. **Load routing is driven by `attemptState.status`** (`RouteResumeByAttemptStatus`): Smothered → Game Over; Act III Active/RecoveryPending parked in a level → that level; everything else → the hub. **Ordinary loading restores the latest durable HP/meter/Integrity, not checkpoint values** — this supersedes the V7.3 "`RestoreSavedCheckpoint` restores checkpoint-time HP and meter" wording — folding the uncredited Rally meter in once (capped at 100) and clearing hero statuses. Saves go through an **ordered per-slot async writer** with a revision guard (an older write never replaces a newer one), flushed before every synchronous commit and at `_ExitTree`, with a per-second durable snapshot and a `save_notice_write_failed` path. A pre-v6 payload parked mid-level cannot have its anchors, Defy or claims reconstructed, so it is **preserved untouched and marked `LegacyRecoveryRequired`** with a notice explaining that Restart Level is needed — never silently restarted, never granted resources.
  - **The F10 level-start reset (2026-10-11, ledger section "the F10 level-start resource reset").** Fresh entry and every full Restart (pause menu, the hub's restart-from-beginning, Game Over) now start the hero at 100 % HP, zero Ultimate meter, full block charges and cooldowns ready, as F10 says. `StoryManager.MintFreshAttempt` calls `ResetDurableResourcesForFreshAttempt` (root `CurrentHP` = max, `CurrentUltimateMeter` = 0) and marks the attempt's hero as not yet built (`IsFreshAttemptHeroPending`, an in-memory latch keyed to the attempt ID, never persisted); `StoryLevelControllerBase.RestoreDurableResources` (Florence uses it too) consumes it (`TryConsumeFreshAttemptHero`) and keeps the factory-fresh hero instead of restoring durable values. Before, completing a level never reset the save's HP or meter, so both carried into the next level, and the three restart writers reset HP but not the meter (`FIX-F10-FRESH-ENTRY-RESOURCES`). Ordinary loads, mid-level resumes, death rewinds, Collapse resumes and Anchor Snaps keep their rules. **The durable snapshot and `CapturePlayerResources` skip the Story hero while a scene loads** (`TryGetAttemptHero`; `GameManager.IsLoadingScene`, test seam `StoryManager.SceneTransitionInProgressOverrideForTesting`): the outgoing scene stays in the tree under the ≥ 2 s loading screen, and its hero used to be saved into the new attempt. That guard is safe only because every exit stops the run before it loads — **Quit to Main Menu now leaves through `StoryManager.CommitOrderlyExit(retainedWallet)`** (the post-fee wallet in memory and on the save; inside a live run it also flushes the hero's HP, meter, resource timers, the rewind pool and the attempt record, commits synchronously and calls `StopLevelRun`; outside one, at the hub, it writes only the wallet) and then `StoryManager.ExitToMainMenu()` (`FIX-F10-ORDERLY-EXIT-FLUSH`: before, the per-second snapshot refunded the 20 % exit fee and the quit captured stale HP/meter). **Loading a Smothered save restores `CollapsedLevel` / `CollapsedCheckpointID`** in `RouteResumeByAttemptStatus`, so Restart Level from a reloaded Game Over restarts the parked level, not the Tutorial (`FIX-SMOTHERED-RELOAD-RESTART`). Open: a Level 0 mid-level resume still rebuilds a factory-fresh hero (`PROVISIONAL-F10-FRESH-HERO-PENDING`), and a quit from the hub's pause menu charges the 20 % fee again on an attempt's held wallet (`VERIFY-HUB-QUIT-EXIT-FEE`). Suite: `LevelStartResourceResetTests` (10).
- **Save schema is v8** (Package 13's single bump, Phase C, 2026-09-29). v8 composes exactly the declared derivations, in order: **W2 `StoryAttemptState.RetireLegacyLevelV8`** (D2 — a story payload parked on a deleted `Level_04A_*` scene banks its held wallet once into the selected hero's deposited dust, with no fee or tier bonus, and re-parks at Level 5 on a fresh attempt; 4A history in `CompletedLevels`/`IntegrityByLevel` stays as dead data), then **W5 `RosterSwapV8.MigrateRosterSwapV8`** (D4 — a `pocahontas` save is locked to `tubman`, every Pocahontas grid node is refunded from an embedded price table into Tubman's deposited dust, her deposited balance moves across, her Legacy unlocks carry over; running after the retirement means dust banked under `pocahontas` follows her), and on the global payload **W5 `RosterSwapV8.EnsureReplacementUnlocked`** (adds `tubman` once to a non-empty `UnlockedCharacters`). Both are pure and engine-free, so a v7 payload migrates without the Godot runtime. W3's four story fields — `SkippedCalibration`, `ShownFirstUseTooltips`, `LastHubLineVisit`, `HomecomingSeen` — are additive with legacy-meaning initializers and need no derivation. `StoryManager.ResumeCampaign` also applies the retirement (idempotent). `pocahontas` wins/losses, seen dialogue and grid keys stay as dead data. `SaveSchemaV8MigrationTests` pins the step. **The history below is v7** (Package 12's single bump, Phase C, 2026-09-27). v7 composes exactly three declared derivations and nothing else: **W2 `StoryAttemptState.ReconcileUndepositedCompletion`** (H02 — a payload saved after a completion but before the hub loaded, i.e. an unminted attempt with no checkpoint and a positive wallet, or a `Completed` record, banks its wallet once; it runs *before* the v6 step's `MigrateFromLegacyRoot` could mint the record, and an open attempt's held dust is never banked), **W6 `CampaignDifficultyRules.SeedLowestDifficultyUsed`** (G14 — `LowestDifficultyUsed` seeded from `Difficulty`, never raised) and **W5 `SavedMatchSettings.DeriveStageHazardsEnabled`** (the retired `HazardRate`: Off → false, anything else → true, on the global `LastMatchSettings`; an explicit toggle is never overwritten). Every other Package 12 field is additive with a field initializer equal to its legacy meaning: story `LowestDifficultyUsed`; global `PhotosensitivityNoticeSeen`, `FirstRunSetupCompleted` (initializer true, set false only on a fresh install), `CrashReports`, `DialogueTextSpeed`, `MuteWhenUnfocused`, the four category mutes, `BlockInputMode`, `StickProfiles`, `PlayerSlotPalette`, and `SavedMatchSettings.StageHazardsEnabled` / `MeterPickupsEnabled` (`HazardRate` survives as a read-only legacy field). W8 became the first writer of v6's existing `attemptState.sealReadiness` with no new field. `SaveSchemaV7MigrationTests` pins the step. **The history below is v6** (Package 11's single bump, 2026-09-13). The story payload gained `StoryDefyHistoryUsed` (F10: Defy is once per **attempt**), `TimeFreezeCooldownSeconds`, `UnlockedLegacyAbilities`, `CheckpointIntegrityPercent`, `ClaimedRewardSourceIDs`, `AttemptState` and `AnchorCharges`; the global payload gained `ReducedTemporalEffects`, `SeenDialogueIDs`, and `InputBindingSet`'s `UnboundActions` / `ShortcutEnabled`. All of them are additive with field initializers, so **the v5→v6 step only composes derivations**: the attempt record from the six loose V7.3 fields, `CheckpointIntegrityPercent` seeded from the live gauge (never invented), the Legacy Unlock backfill from `CompletedLevels`, A4's retired-node grid refund, the `gameplay_rewind` → `gameplay_time_freeze` binding rename, the Hybrid → timed-Stock match-settings normalization, and the global `SeenDialogueIDs` union from every slot's `ViewedDialogueIDs` (which runs from `SaveManager` after the slot loop, because the global payload loads first). **`CurrentVersion` is shared by both payloads** — a global-only bump also bumps story payloads; splitting the two counters is recorded as a future-package recommendation. The envelope uses AES-256-CBC, encrypt-then-HMAC-SHA-256, distinct derived encryption/authentication keys, and a random per-install key stored at `user://saves/.savekey`. Writes are atomic and verified, backups can recover a bad primary, corrupt candidates are preserved, legacy Base64/plain JSON is migrated, completed puzzle IDs persist per slot, and newer schemas are rejected without rewriting them. Never replace the per-install strategy with a hardcoded source secret.

## Fighter networking and determinism

`scripts/FighterSim/` implements a Klotho fixed-point (`FP64`/`FPVector2`) 1v1 foundation with quantized inputs, stable state IDs, full snapshots, deterministic hashes, a 120-tick history, prediction, corrected-input rollback, and resimulation. `FighterLoadoutFactory` accepts normalized base resources only, preserving Story/Fighter isolation. The Test Arena renders synchronized results without feeding Godot state back into gameplay.

The deterministic systems cover accelerated ground/air movement, universal roll phases (dash removed 2026-08-09), fixed-point fighter jostling, jump counts, solid side/top bounds, bottom-zone stocks, generic basics/specials/ultimates, block, meter, status, Stock/Time resolution (Hybrid is retired), projectiles, movement abilities (including input-directional Warp teleports with a reduced-gravity float window), deploy-limited persistent constructs, Area-execution zones (`FighterZoneComponent`/`FighterZoneSystem`: impulse-free periodic damage/status pulses that bypass shields and never zero velocity, plus owner-overlap speed bonuses like Einstein's Relativity Rift), stage hazards (On/Off with a per-stage authored cadence), Chronal Orbs (Package 12 W5/M23: each spawn draws its gap inside the frequency window, floored at 600 frames, and a free anchor, from `match.RandomState`; Resonance Surge is orb type 4 behind the Meter pickups sub-toggle), authored launches and the M05 knockdown (component 320), the Package 12/13 sim-local kit phases of component 305 (see Ability implementation), the air dodge, the Down-Air slam bounce, stock-loss despawn, the Ultimate activation strike (321), Tubman's Foresight counter (322), the burst-on-terrain projectile and the ground wave, and since 2026-10-16 (component 323) every Special's authored startup / active / recovery with its cast lock and the 0 / 1 / 0 fallback, D3, the perfect-block clock and damage-scaled shieldstun, the post-hitstun tumble, respawn protection, the short hop and the held-Jump hang, the one-way lip pop, both drop-through inputs and the ledge get-ups. `FighterStageGeometry` supplies per-stage fixed-point bounds, one-way platforms (with drop-through and edge-walk-off rules), spawn distance, and authored hazard/orb anchors; stages without authored geometry fall back to the legacy flat arena. Seven stages have an unbroken base floor (empty `FloorSegments`); three are Open. Character-specific production behavior and rollback performance/platform gates remain incomplete. Klotho v0.6.1 is experimental and this is not production online netcode.

### Klotho component inventory

**Component IDs are assigned once and never reused.** `FighterRuntimeComponent` (**305**) is exactly full at 128 bytes, and as of Package 11 A1c `FighterVerbComponent` (**310**) is too — the old "4 bytes of headroom" note is stale. New sim state needs a new component ID, and an agent who believes it needs one records the need rather than picking a number. Package 13 grew existing components rather than allocating where it could: **303** `FighterPersistentObjectComponent` gained `GlideBoltSpent` (W7a), **309** `FighterZoneComponent` gained `CollapseFramesRemaining` / `CollapseTargetMask` (W7a) and `ExecutionFlags` (W7b), and **320** grew (below). The 2026-10-11 combat-flow pass allocated nothing either: it packed the D1 hit-confirm word into 305's unread `AegisHits` int and renamed 310's Momentum counters in place. **The 2026-10-16 combat and movement update allocated exactly one ID, 323** (its plan's §1.5, before any workstream started; nothing grew 312 or 320), and reserved four zero-byte values beside it: 305 `AttackFlags` 256 and 512, 305's universal-movement values 8–10 (the air dodge owns 5–7 and the kit phases 16–31; 11–15 are still free) and 310 `Tumble` value 2. **The highest component ID is now 323.** Retired sim identifiers, never reused: kit phases **19–20**, persistent-object type **4** (`vine_snare`), zone type **83** (Tidewater), `FighterCharacterID` ordinal **8**.

| ID | Component | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| 305 | `FighterRuntimeComponent` | Core per-fighter runtime state, including the `AttackFlags` bit 128 `FighterBasicAttackRules.FlagAirAttack` (the single air strike, 2026-10-12; no new field), and the ability lockout's `AbilityLockoutFrames` (the unread `BasicCooldownFrames` slot, reused 2026-10-08) and the D1 hit-confirm word (2026-10-11), packed into the reused, previously unread `AegisHits` int: `SpecialLockoutFrames` (bits 0–15), `SwingLanded` (16), `FreezeBufferArmed` (17) and `FreezeBufferedSpecials` (18–19). Since 2026-10-16 `AttackFlags` 256 is `FighterBasicAttackRules.FlagActiveFacingRight` (the string, air-strike and construct-sweep box facing, latched at active start; decision 10.1, `USER-SIM-BOX-FACING-LATCH`) and 512 `FlagLedgeAttack`, and the universal-movement slot's values 8–10 are the ledge climbs `LedgeClimb`, `LedgeRoll`, `LedgeAttackClimb`. **Full at 128 B.** |
| 310 | `FighterVerbComponent` | The verb layer, incl. the packed `GrabPartnerPlayerID` + `ThrowDamageApplied`, and `MomentumRefundedFramesSlotOne` / `Two` (renamed 2026-10-11 from `MomentumRefundsSlot*`, same layout; they now count the frames per-hit Momentum refunded this cooldown cycle, 0–120). Since 2026-10-16 `Tumble` reads 0 none / 1 in hitstun / 2 `TumbleAfterHitstun` (`FighterVerbRules`). **Full at 128 B.** |
| ~~311~~ | ~~`FighterEchoRingComponent`~~ | **Retired** with the 5-sample ring (A1c). Never reuse the ordinal. |
| 312 | `FighterDefenseComponent` (MaxCount 2) | D04 Defy protection (`AwaitControl`, frames, proc identity, `DefyBarred`), Aegis flag, and the HP-barrier layer. |
| 313–317 | `FighterEchoStepRing0..4Component` (MaxCount 2 each) | The 31 consecutive per-tick position samples, 6/6/6/6/7. Slice 0 also carries `Head`, `LatestTick`, `ValidCount`, `Generation`, `LifeEpoch`, `Armed`, `ActivationTick`. |
| 318 | `FighterConductiveComponent` (MaxCount 2) | F07 Conductive marks: remaining frames, source player ID, chain-consumed execution ID. |
| 319 | `FighterSuddenDeathComponent` (singleton) | F22 phase generation, frozen regulation stocks-lost totals, `DefyDisabledByPhase`. |
| 320 | `FighterKnockdownComponent` (MaxCount 2) | Landing/aerial state. M05 knockdown/get-up sub-phase of `Stunned` (Package 12 W3b): `KnockdownFrames`, `GetUpKind`, `GetUpFrames`, `GetUpDirection`; Package 13 (plan D7) added W1's `AirDodgeUsed`, `SlamBouncePending`, `SlamBounceSpeed` (FP64) and W7b's `StaffRefundUsed` — 7 ints + 1 FP64, 36 B, all-zero inactive. |
| 321 | `FighterUltimateActivationComponent` (MaxCount 2) | A02 activation strike (Package 13 W6): caster `Phase` (Windup / Active / WhiffRecovery / Cinematic), `PhaseFrames`, `StartedAerial`, `FacingRight`, `LungeVelocityX`, `Origin`; victim `CaptorSlot`, `CapturedFrames`, `CaptureAnchor` — 64 B, all-zero inactive. Rules: `FighterUltimateActivationRules`. |
| 322 | `FighterCounterComponent` (MaxCount 2) | Tubman's Foresight (Package 13 W5): `Phase` (Startup / Window / WhiffRecovery / Sidestep), `PhaseFrames`, `AnswerPending`, `CountersLanded` — 16 B, all-zero inactive. Rules: `FighterForesightRules`. |
| 323 | `FighterActionStateComponent` (MaxCount 2) | The combat and movement update's action state (2026-10-16; `scripts/FighterSim/FighterSpecialCast.cs`) — nine ints, 36 B per fighter, 72 B per snapshot, all-zero idle: `SpecialSlot` (0 / 1 / 2), `SpecialPhase` (0 none, 1 Startup, 2 Active, 3 Recovery, 4 Delegated — Foresight runs on 322), `SpecialPhaseFrames`, `SpecialPhaseElapsed`, `SpecialFlags` (1 `FlagStartedAerial`, 2 `FlagEffectFired` — set for melee slots too, never read it as "landed" — 4 `FlagMeleeResolved`, 8 `FlagLandedDirectHit` (D3), 16 `FlagFallbackTiming`), `SpecialCancelBuffer` (D3: bits 0–2 the verb, 0 none / 1 roll / 2 jump / 3 basic / 4 movement, bits 3–6 its age, bit 7 Up and bit 8 Down held at the press), `BlockTiming` (bits 0–7 the perfect-block frames left, 8–15 the frames since the stance dropped, 0 = never raised this stock, saturating at 255, bit 16 the stance up last tick, 17 observed and 18 the escape tick (per-tick bookkeeping), 20–23 the perfect blocks resolved, cycling 1–15, read only by presentation and the drill), `DownTapFrames` (the 0–18 double-tap window) and `ActionFlags` (bit 0 `RespawnProtection`, 1 `JumpReleasedSincePress`, 2 `LedgeStickRearmed`, 3 `RiseFromOwnJump`; bits 4–31 reserved, 0). Added in `SpawnFighter`, held at zero while the respawn platform holds the fighter (the stock-loss reset), zeroed by the F22 Sudden Death setup. Rules: `FighterSpecialCastRules`, `FighterSpecialCancelRules`, `FighterBlockTimingRules`, `FighterMatchFlowRules` (respawn), `FighterMovementSystem.Movement.cs` (the latches); every accepted verb passes through `FighterActionRules.OnActionAccepted`. Seam: `FighterSimulation.TryGetFighterActionState`. |

`scripts/Networking/` now defines a versioned fixed-size input/hash protocol (**protocol v4** since Package 13 W5 — the roster enum serializes, so the Tubman swap bumped it and a v3 packet is refused; v3 dates from Package 11 A1c, carrying the logical combined verbs and a direct-vs-preset origin flag; `GameplayButtons` bits **12 = EchoStep, 13 = Grab, 14 = DirectOrigin**, bit 15 is the only free one, and the reserved Dash bit 11 is still never reused. `PlayerInputFrame.SerializedSize` 12 B and `RollbackInputPacket.SerializedSize` 47 B are **unchanged**; the 2026-10-16 combat and movement update kept v4 too, although every Fighter hash moved and builds from before and after it would desync on LAN, because LAN is de-scoped to Package 7 — its short-hop latch reads the existing `Released` field). `DirectOrigin` is the normalized instruction to a peer — *my chords are off, do not read my component bits as a chord* — which is what stops a peer re-recognizing a remote chord under its own shortcut settings. Also: acknowledgements, confirmed-frame hash exchange, direct-IP UDP LAN transport, deterministic in-memory test transport, remote prediction/correction, bounded rollback/resimulation, desync events, rollback-budget diagnostics, and cryptographically generated room codes. It does not yet have LAN discovery, handshake/rules negotiation, full-state resync, matchmaking UI, Steam Networking Sockets/relay, public queue, or production failure/forfeit flows.

Initial online/local Fighter Mode is 1v1. Even if code contains generalized player-count fields, do not build four-player behavior in this phase.

## UI, localization, audio, and accessibility

- UI is authored for a 1920×1080 reference canvas with Godot `Control` anchors and layout containers.
- **One shared theme.** `resources/UI/ftt_theme.tres` is the single `Theme`; adopt it by setting `Theme` on a screen's root `Control` and Godot propagates. `scripts/UI/UIPalette.cs` holds the matching C# colour constants for `ColorRect` fills and runtime tints that a Theme cannot express, and `UIThemeTests` fails if the two drift. The theme still needs no font file (`assets/fonts/` is empty): since Package 12 W6 (G05) its `default_font` is a `FontVariation` slot with no `base_font` (the engine face) whose empty `fallbacks` list is the reserved CJK slot, so dropping in a production face is setting `base_font`.
- **Type-scale roles are theme Label variations (V7.3, 2026-08-26):** `SmallLabel` 14 / body default 18 / `HeadingLabel` 22 / `EmphasisLabel` 26 / `TitleLabel` 28, authored in the theme and mirrored by `UIPalette.SmallFontSize`…`TitleFontSize` (+ the `*LabelVariation` name constants). Non-HUD screens set `ThemeTypeVariation` instead of `AddThemeFontSizeOverride` — `UIPalette.ApplyUiScale` rescales every per-type theme font-size entry in place, so variations follow the accessibility UI scale while an override freezes its label at 100%. The nine converted screens (ConfirmModal, HolodeckConsolePanel, CharacterSelectScreen, DialogueManager, PauseMenu, MatchResults, LocalFighterPause, CreditsController, TimelineRestartPanel) plus the new onboarding screens carry per-screen no-override pins; the HUDs (FighterHUD, StoryHUD, FighterPresentationOverlay) deliberately stay on `UiScaleBinder`, and world-space labels in `scripts/Environment`/factories keep their overrides. Exact-match scene-authored overrides (22/26/14) in StoryPause/DialogueBox/Credits/CharacterSelect were converted; non-role authored sizes (42/30/20 in CharacterSelect, MainMenu, HUD scenes) remain a recorded gap.
