- **Fighter Onboarding shipped (V7.3, 2026-08-26; design Section 7):** `scripts/UI/MoveListScreen.cs` + `SystemsCardScreen.cs` over script-less authored scenes (`scenes/ui/MoveList.tscn`, `SystemsCard.tscn`), both following the SettingsMenu overlay pattern (CanvasLayer, `ProcessMode.Always`, `Closed` event, never touch `SceneTree.Paused`). The Move List sources every number LIVE — `BasicComboRules.StringProfileFor` (per-character startups/damage shape/reach), grab/throw/roll/fast-fall constants, and the character's `{id}_data.tres` abilities via `AuthoredResources.Load` — never a second authored copy. Entry points: a Move List button on the Story pause (campaign character) and two on the Fighter pause (either active fighter), opening as overlays inside the pause CanvasLayer with focus return; Move List + Systems Card buttons on the Character Select footer (the Move List opens for P1's current tile; both joined the selection focus chain, now 5 entries). The Systems Card is one static localized page (eight `systems_card_*` section pairs in the .tscn; since 2026-10-11 its Echo Step body, `systems_card_echo_body`, states per-hit Momentum); `MoveListScreenTests` and `SystemsCardContentTests` pin the single-source rule, key resolution, and zero font-size overrides. **Package 11 added the six Holodeck Calibration Drills beside them** (F18) with two entry points — the standalone main-menu route and the hub console — and `MoveListScreen.Populate(characterID, MoveListMode)`, whose `Story` mode badges an unrestored Legacy slot with `movelist_dormant_until` and suppresses its stats.
- **The Story HUD is an authored scene against `docs/design-contracts/HUD_CONTRACT.md`** (Package 11 A8 rewrote both `scenes/ui/StoryHUD.tscn` and `scripts/UI/StoryHUD.cs`, rather than editing them). The tree is `SafeArea / TopLeft_Panel / TopRight_Panel`; widgets that used to be built at runtime are authored nodes. It carries **two status slots** (28 × 28, damage left / control right, fixed position, an empty slot hides its glyph and keeps its space, distinct glyph **plus localized name** per status — never colour alone), duration radials on both slots and readiness radials on the three cooldown icons and the Ultimate ring (`scripts/UI/RadialProgress.cs`; fill is written by the owner of the authoritative value and never self-ticked — since 2026-10-11 the three cooldown radials are synced every frame from the hero's own timers, `StoryHUD.SyncCooldownAuthority` → `HudAbilityIndicatorModel.SyncCooldownRemaining`, so per-hit Momentum, Time Freeze and `StoryCooldownMultiplier` reach them), **ability slot-lock overlays** showing Dormant / Suppressed / Clear (a locked slot is shown, never hidden, and is never "ready" however full its meter), the **Defy seal** (four states, shape-distinguished, no pulse), a **Time Freeze indicator that is its own node** — never attached to the rewind counter, which is the pattern the design now forbids — Act III **Beacon anchors** (M15, Package 12 W10: a 24 × 24 `BeaconAnchorIcon` immediately right of the rewind hourglass and ahead of the Time Freeze indicator, 8 × 8 gold diamond `AnchorPip`s that crack and fade on an Anchor Snap, a hollow last-stand icon at zero, and the localized accessible label `hud_anchor_charges_accessible`), the rewind counter, a persistent dust counter with a separately fading `+N` float that cannot move the committed balance, the **Integrity era-clock** (wedge, text, siphon streams bound to the live Extractor count, a frozen state with its own tint) and the Collapse Tremor overlay at levels 0/1/2. Only the block pips and the Act III anchor pips are still code-built, because their *count* is data-driven. The Fighter HUD gained two status slots per player, a Defy seal beside each meter, F21's mode-switched stock display and F22's "Sudden Death — next death loses" replacing the running clock. Both HUDs play the D10 "not yet" flash on a lockout-refused slot (`StoryHUD`, player 0 only, so the Mirror clone's refusals never flash; `FighterHUD.FlashLockoutRefusal`; the look is `FTT.UI.NotYetFlash`). Since 2026-10-12 the Story HUD also carries the authored `SafeArea/EncounterLockPanel` (`StoryHUD.EncounterLock.cs`, driven by `EventBus.OnEncounterLockChanged`): "Enemies left: N" (`hud_encounter_enemies_left`, `HeadingLabel`) while an encounter lock is engaged, hidden for a lock with nothing to count, and a 3 s GO prompt (`hud_encounter_go` plus an arrow glyph, `EmphasisLabel`) after a clear, none for `EncounterGoDirection.None`; theme roles only, no font-size overrides. `HUD_CONTRACT.md` does not name the panel yet (an owed amendment).
- **Author focus, do not rely on Godot's geometric fallback.** `FocusChainBuilder.Apply(container)` writes `focus_neighbor_*` links and takes initial focus; rebuild the chain whenever a surface shows or hides options. `Collect` skips hidden subtrees and `focus_skip` group members, and splices a `SpinBox`'s internal `LineEdit` in at the spin box's own position (internal children are invisible to `GetChild`, so a spin box is otherwise unreachable by keyboard and controller).
- **`PauseMenuBase` owns `SceneTree.Paused`** for both `PauseMenu` (Story) and `LocalFighterPause`; subclasses may layer presentation but cannot skip the tree write, and an overridden `_ExitTree` must call `base._ExitTree()`. Since Package 12 W6 it also opens the pause on window focus loss (`HandleFocusLost`, gated by `CanAutoPause()`, which may narrow but never widen `CanTogglePause()`); regaining focus never resumes. `LocalFighterPause.CanAutoPause` refuses while its wired `ResultsScreen` is showing (Package 12 Phase C), so a focus loss never opens the pause over the match results. `SettingsMenu` never writes the pause — closing it returns control to whoever opened it (since 2026-10-16 `ui_cancel` closes it, and pad B cancels a keyboard-slot listen while a pad-slot listen still binds B). **Since 2026-10-16** (`PROVISIONAL-PAD-MENU-RULES`) `ui_cancel` (pad B) resumes an open pause exactly as Resume does and is always consumed (swallowed while the controller-disconnect modal refuses the toggle); a child overlay answers its own cancel first, so one B closes one layer; and `PauseMenuBase.IsAnyOpen` counts open pause menus. **The tree pause is shared with a gameplay-pausing dialogue** (CM-FIXB-PADUI B16): `SetPaused` writes `Paused = paused || DialogueManager.HoldsGameplayPause` and `_ExitTree` hands the tree back to a dialogue that still holds it; a dialogue that ends under an open pause leaves the tree to the pause; and Restart Level and Quit to Main Menu close through `PauseMenuBase.ClosePauseForSceneExit()`, which releases the tree outright so the threaded scene load can run. `NearCaptureBeat` (Level 9) pauses the tree itself and is not an owner yet, so a pause opened over its struggle still unpauses the world on Resume (`VERIFY-NEAR-CAPTURE-PAUSE-OWNER`). The hub spends an Interact press that opened the Holodeck console, the Repository, a crew dialogue or the portal, because pad B is Interact and `ui_cancel` at once.
- **Loading is a themed scene, not a code-built overlay.** `GameManager.LoadScene` picks a Story portal or Fighter VS treatment from the destination path and keeps the 2 s minimum display time.
- Use `Tr("translation_key")` / `TranslationServer.Translate(...)` or the localization manager for all visible copy. Add English entries to `localization/en.csv`. Do not add localized JSON payloads or language selectors until localization production is scheduled.
- **In an authored `.tscn`, store the raw key as the control's `text` and let Godot's automatic control translation resolve it** — that is what lets a language change follow without rebuilding the surface, and scene tests assert the raw key rather than the English string. `SceneVisibleTextTests` rejects any visible `text` that is neither a real translation key nor an empty/glyph-only value; `ScriptTranslationKeyTests` sweeps every `Tr("...")` and `TranslationServer.Translate("...")` literal in `scripts/`; `UnusedTranslationKeyTests` reports keys nothing references and fails on a new one.
- `project.godot` registers `res://localization/en.en.translation` with English fallback. Keep that resource registration intact: Godot `Node.Tr()` bypasses the custom `LocalizationManager` and otherwise displays raw translation keys as placeholder text.
- Dialogue uses a typewriter reveal at the player's **Text Speed** (Package 12 W6, G10: Slow 20 / Normal 30 / Fast 60 characters per second / Instant) and punctuation holds (0.26 s after `. ! ?`, 0.12 s after `, ; :` at Normal, scaled by 30/rate; a run of marks holds once), a glass panel treatment, emotion-driven portrait framing, and a chirp every third non-whitespace character pitched per the locked character. Confirm completes the active line silently, then advances on the next confirm. **Hold-to-skip is universal (V7.6, Package 11 A5 — this supersedes the V7.3 "completed campaign AND previously seen" gate, which is deleted).** Holding the confirm/Interact action for `DialogueManager.HoldToSkipSeconds` (0.75 s) skips **any** sequence. If the sequence ID is not yet in the **global** `GlobalSaveData.SeenDialogueIDs`, the hold opens one confirmation on the shared `ConfirmModal` ("Skip this scene? It won't replay."); a sequence seen on **any** slot skips with no prompt. Per-slot `StorySaveData.ViewedDialogueIDs` is now the **effect ledger**, not the skip gate: `ResolveDialogueEffects(sequenceID)` is split out of `EndSequence()` and called from **both** the completion and the confirmed-skip paths, so a skipped scene still applies its gameplay effects exactly once, and `LastViewedDialogueID` is demoted to a presentation cursor. Hero-conditional sequences use the `<baseID>@<heroID>` ID form (see Narrative), and line text supports the `{CaptiveName1}` / `{CaptiveName2}` tokens, resolved at render time by `FTT.Core.CampaignCaptiveRoster` from the manifest roster minus the active hero (authored priority Leonardo → Cleopatra → Tesla) through the `captive_name_*` key family. Package 13 added `{HeroAcceptLine}` / `{HeroFarewellLine}` (W3, `DialogueTokens`, keys `hero_accept_line_<hero>` / `hero_farewell_line_<hero>` with a fallback) and `{MissingSoFar}` / `{MissingPlaces}` (W4, `MissingLegendTokens`); the unnamed-voice speaker (`speaker_unknown_voice`) shows no portrait. Package 12 W7 (M35) added `{HeroName}`, `{HeroAddressName}`, `{HeroPossessiveName}` and `{HomeEraName}` (`FTT.Core.DialogueTokens`, resolved in the same render pass from the saved hero ID through the `hero_name_*` / `hero_address_*` / `hero_possessive_*` / `hero_home_era_*` rows). New hero-variant keys use `__heroid` while shipped ones keep `_heroid` (`DEFER-VARIANT-KEY-SEPARATOR`). Every line shown, including a skipped sequence's, also enters the session-only 100-line `DialogueLog`. Since 2026-10-16 (CM-PADUI, CM-FIXB-PADUI B15; `PROVISIONAL-PAD-MENU-RULES`) the box stands down under an open pause menu and when a focused button outside it takes the confirm; a confirm already held when a sequence starts counts toward hold-to-skip only once it has been let go; pad A (also Jump) pages lines in a non-pausing sequence, as keyboard Space and attacking always did; and while gameplay runs under a non-pausing sequence a held confirm that is also Jump (pad A, Space) never counts toward the hold, so Interact (the button the hint names) and Enter still skip the send-off and the Homecoming. Whether jumping or attacking should page barks at all, and whether an Interact hold should arm the skip in one, are open (`VERIFY-BARK-INPUT-RULES`).
- **Audio buses are authored** in `resources/Audio/default_bus_layout.tres` — Master → Music, SFX → {Combat, Movement, Environmental}, UI, plus **`CriticalCues` and `Dialogue`** (added by Package 11 A8 / C01b) — registered under `audio/buses/default_bus_layout`. `AudioManager`'s `StemDirector` plays a `StageAudioSet`'s three stems synchronized and **additively** — ambient always audible, combat layering on, climax on top — with authored crossfades. **`AudioSnapshotMixer` is a priority selector, not an additive stack** (C01b, superseding "snapshots stack additively"): the highest-priority live snapshot wins, the background low-pass moved off the SFX parent onto its three World SFX children so UI and critical cues stay clear, the Pause `+2 dB` UI lift is gone, UI is out of the mixed buses, the dialogue chirp routes to `Dialogue`, and `AudioManager.PlayCriticalCue` is the path for a cue that must survive ducking. All 27 audio sets (17 Story + 10 Fighter stages) are authored on placeholder stems. **H04 core-mechanic cues (Package 12 W10):** `resources/Audio/core_mechanic_cues.tres` (`CoreMechanicCueCatalog`, IDs in `CoreMechanicCueIDs`) is the one catalog of the GDD's Section 8 core cues, each row naming its bus and whether it is Critical (Critical rows always route through `PlayCriticalCue`); `StageAudioSet.HazardWarningCue`/`HazardImpactCue` carry the ten stage-hazard pairs. `AudioManager.CoreCues` (`CoreMechanicCuePresenter`) plays Story cues from existing `EventBus` events, and `FighterSimulationDriver` raises Fighter cues from its read-only presentation pass (the knockdown thud reads component 320). The N01 seal charge/lock play from `EventBus.OnSealingAnchorChanged`, which `TemporalCoreAnchor` raises when it arms and when the seal is accepted (Package 12 Phase C; the shipped anchor is single-press, so the design's "charging hum while held" plays at the arm). Every stream is silent. **The entire placeholder audio kit is digital silence as of 2026-08-10 (user directive: the placeholder tones were annoying)**: the three stems, the five generated .ogg cues (the generator's documented RENDER_SILENT flag keeps durations byte-identical), and the six one-shot SFX .tres payloads — which also silences the dialogue chirp, footsteps, UI blips, and hit/hazard/pickup cues. Durations, loop metadata, paths, and all call sites are untouched, so every audio contract test still holds and Package 10's real assets drop in with no code changes — do not "fix" the silence by re-adding tones (resources/Audio/README.md carries the full record). Saved volumes apply at boot.
- Settings include audio buses, resolution/window mode/VSync, complete InputMap rebinding with conflict explanation and reset-to-default, damage-number visibility, HUD opacity, screen-shake scaling, haptics, and **Reduced Temporal Effects** (C01a — read it through `FTT.Core.ComfortSettings`, never directly, and never from `scripts/FighterSim/`: it is local presentation state and stays outside match snapshots and hashes, so the toggle produces identical gameplay hashes On and Off). It defaults **false**, is never inferred from Screen Shake, and is pushed at boot by `SaveManager._Ready` before the first loading/portal effect. All of it persists in global save data (schema v7).
- **Package 12 W6 front end (see `docs/handoffs/P12_W6.md`).** Rendering is capped at 60 FPS with no physics interpolation (G04). The main menu raises a boot overlay once per process as the current scene: photosensitivity notice (every launch, skippable after the first) → first-run cards (fresh install only) → a local crash-report prompt (G06/G09; `CrashReportService`, no upload). Text Speed 20/30/60/Instant and a session-only 100-line `DialogueLog` (G10, `ui_dialogue_log`). `PauseMenuBase` auto-pauses on window focus loss and on the no-op `PlatformOverlay` seam; the Story pause handles controller loss (G11). Toggle Block is an `InputManager` latch and the left stick gets per-device deadzone/down-threshold processing before quantization (G13). Per-category mutes never touch `CriticalCues`/`Dialogue` (M27). Difficulty can be lowered one step from the hub pause menu (G14, `StorySaveData.LowestDifficultyUsed`). Extras → Credits replay / Third-Party Licenses, and `application/config/version` is the one version string (G15).
- **C01c binding shape.** `InputBindingSet` carries `UnboundActions` and `ShortcutEnabled`, both keyed `action|deviceKind` and both surviving `Normalize()` — an explicit **Unbound** slot is a different thing from a missing override that inherits the default, and `Normalize` deletes empty rows, so the parallel set is the only representation of "cleared on purpose". `IsShortcutEnabled` returns **true for an absent key**, so an untouched payload reproduces today's chords. `InputShortcuts` owns the three fixed recipes (**there is no chord editor** — only their component actions are remappable) and the reachability rule. **`Actions.Ultimate` is remappable now**, so the old "the LB+RB chord and the derived Dash gesture are shown read-only" note is stale; the Controls tab lists every bindable action with keyboard / gamepad / Unbind / reset, per-device shortcut toggles, and a localized explanation when a route becomes unreachable. **Since 2026-10-16 the gamepad Ultimate is the C01c recipe at runtime** (decision 9.2, "Chord leniency … The Controls chord toggle gets wired up. No direct bind."; `USER-PAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD-LENIENCY`; `VERIFY-GAMEPAD-ULTIMATE-CHORD` closed, (a) and (c) by this change, (b) on 2026-10-11). Until then it was the Ultimate action's own two joypad events (9 + 10), read as a conjunction by `InputManager.ReadUltimatePressed`, so the toggle changed nothing, remapping a component only relabelled the chord, and the chord's component presses fired too. Now the gamepad direct slot is initially empty (`project.godot` keeps only keyboard U), the chord is read from the recipe's live component bindings through the leniency window (the `InputManager` bullet under Autoloads), so the toggle is live and remapping the Movement Ability or Special 2 moves the chord, and `ReadUltimatePressed` stays the direct route (keyboard U, or a captured pad bind, which C01c keeps legal). `ui_accept` and `ui_cancel` are fixed menu navigation, outside the Controls tab.
- Controller haptics and screen shake must respect their global accessibility intensity/enable settings. `HudOpacity` is polled live, not read once at construction.

## Coding and content conventions

- Match folder namespaces: `FTT.Core`, `FTT.Characters`, `FTT.Characters.Abilities`, `FTT.Combat`, `FTT.Enemies`, `FTT.Environment`, `FTT.UI`, `FTT.Networking`, and `FTT.FighterSim`.
- Godot C# node/resource classes are `partial`; reusable inspector data classes should use `[GlobalClass]` and `[Export]` fields/groups.
- Use `res://` paths for Godot resources. Prefer exported `PackedScene`, `Resource`, and typed node references over scattered magic paths.
- Keep static tuning in Resources and runtime mutable state in controllers/snapshots. Avoid creating a second canonical value in a factory or UI script. Ability-scoped Resonance lanes are read through `PlayerController.StoryScoped(key, abilityScope)`; the scoped bucket is copied in **one** assignment by `CharacterFactory` — never as a second per-ability field. **Every Story grid lane reaches a hero only through `CharacterFactory.ApplyActiveStoryProgression`** (2026-10-11), which both the build and the live re-resolve call; never hand-copy `StoryStatProfile` fields anywhere else, or the two paths drift. Story kit damage reads the grid's damage lanes and the drop buff only through `PlayerController.StoryAbilityDamage*` (Ability implementation), and the Influence meter's cap and rates only through `InfluenceMeterRules` (`InfluenceMeterSourceScanTests`).
- **Save payload classes are plain data with no engine dependency, and must stay that way.** Never read a resource, a manifest or any `res://` path from a `GlobalSaveData` / `StorySaveData` field initializer or from `Normalize()`. Pure-C# GdUnit suites construct and normalize those objects with no Godot runtime, where a `FileAccess` call is an **uncatchable** access violation that kills the whole test host (CLAUDE.md failure signature A6b). Engine-side defaults belong on the autoload — `SaveManager.EnsureRosterUnlocked` is the pattern. The same caution applies to `CharacterRoster`, `CampaignCaptiveRoster` and `ContentManifest.LoadDefault()`: they need the engine, so only autoloads and `[RequireGodotRuntime]` suites may reach them.
- **A retirement source gate makes the retired noun unusable in identifiers anywhere in the repo, test names included.** `TimeFreezeTests.TheRetiredTimeVerbLeavesNoTraceInTheSource` scans every `.cs` under `scripts/` and `tests/` for the assembled manual-rewind token after stripping comments — so a pin that has to *name* the thing it retires must name it in prose, not in a method name.
- **Any GdUnit case that adds a grouped node to the `SceneTree` root must free it in a `finally`.** A leaked `StoryPlayer` is a session-wide failure amplifier: every Story suite that resolves the player by group then picks up a foreign dead fighter, and the resulting cascade points at seams that do not exist.
- **Any GdUnit case that writes `GameManager.CurrentSession` must restore it, and a suite should not write the real `user://saves`.** `SessionData` is a struct, so snapshot it before and assign it back in a `finally`. A leaked `ActiveSaveSlot` makes every later `CharacterFactory.CreateCharacter(id)` (default `applyStoryProgression: true`) install the Legacy Unlock gate from whatever save the developer's machine holds in that slot, and points later suites' autosaves at that real file. `MainMenuSceneTests` leaked slot 0 from three cases until 2026-10-11 (an empty slot row writes it); its `Open` / `Teardown` now snapshot and restore the session, and its one disk-reaching deletion runs only against a slot with no file on disk (`SaveManager.HasAnyStorySlotFileOnDisk`). The symptom is CLAUDE.md's "leaked save slot" signature. Prefer the scratch-directory idiom (`user://test-saves/<guid>`) for new save-writing tests; several older suites still borrow a real slot (2) and the real `global.sav` and restore them.
- **A test that walks a fighter across an authored stage has to respect `FighterStageGeometry.FloorSegments`.** `IsOpenStage` is the cheap guard; a chase loop that ignores it ends in a ledge hang, a 300-frame auto-release and a lost stock instead of the setup it meant to build.
- Poll named InputMap actions through `InputManager.Actions`. Preserve device/player separation for local 1v1; direct global input polling can make both fighters react to one device.
- Gameplay state changes belong in `_PhysicsProcess()` at 60 Hz. `_Process()` is appropriate for presentation/loading/UI, not authoritative combat simulation.
- Use `EventBus` for cross-system events, but keep payloads sufficient for consumers and avoid hidden global coupling.
- Pool frequently spawned or destroyed gameplay objects. Reset every mutable field in `OnSpawn()`/`OnDespawn()`.
- Dispose engine-returned Godot collection wrappers (`GetNodesInGroup`, `GetChildren`, `GetOverlappingBodies`/`Areas`) deterministically at recurring gameplay call sites: bind the result, then `using var lifetime = result.AsDisposable();` (`FTT.Core.GodotCollectionExtensions`). Wrappers left to the GC land on the finalizer queue, and a collection finalizer that runs during engine teardown crashes the process (`0xC0000005` in `godotsharp_array_destroy`); `GameManager._ExitTree` drains the queue at quit as the backstop, but deterministic disposal is the first line of defense and also avoids gameplay-time GC pressure.
- **Never call `Dispose()` on a Godot `Resource`** (or any `RefCounted`), in production or test code. Reference counting owns their lifetime. Disposing one that another Godot object still references — or that a parent resource will release — double-disposes its C# script instance and trips `CRASH_COND(gchandle.is_released())` in `mono_object_disposed_baseref`, which kills the process with `0xC000001D` at some unrelated later point. `Free()` the *node* and leave its resources alone.
- **Load authored `.tres` data resources through `FTT.Core.AuthoredResources.Load<T>()`, not `ResourceLoader.Load`/`GD.Load`.** `CharacterData`, `AbilityData`, `EnemyData`, `BossData`, `EnemyAbilityData`, `ResonanceGridData`, `DialogueSetData`, `ScenePoolConfig`, `StoryDropTable` and friends are immutable tuning data; the cache pins each one for the process lifetime. Loading one, reading it, and dropping the reference destroys the whole scripted graph — including every sub-resource behind an `Array[ExtResource(script)]` export — and rebuilds it on the next call, and that churn races the .NET finalizer thread into the same crash. Scenes, textures, audio, and anything pooled or streamed must *not* go through the cache; they are meant to be released. **A scripted `.tres` that a scene file assigns to an export bypasses `Load`**, so that export's setter must call `AuthoredResources.Pin(value)` (`ContentTemplateMarker.Contract`/`PoolConfig`, `ChronalDustPickup.VisualTiers`). The unpinned contracts crashed about one full suite in three before the 2026-10-04 fix, and `SceneAssignedResourcePinTests` guards the rule.
- **Physics in/out signal callbacks cannot mutate physics-space state.** While an `AreaEntered`/`BodyEntered` handler (and everything it cascades into — hit resolution, kills, drop spawns, lethal-hit rewinds) is running, the engine rejects reparenting collision objects, toggling `Area2D.Monitoring`/`Monitorable`, disabling collision shapes, and `ProcessMode` writes on collision-bearing subtrees ("Function blocked during in/out signal" / "Can't change this state while flushing queries" / "Disabling a CollisionObject node during a physics callback"; a body added mid-flush is left with a null space and later crashes `MoveAndCollide`). The engine exposes no query for this state, so the codebase marks it itself: signal entry points that can cascade wrap their body in `using var scope = FTT.Core.PhysicsCallbackGuard.Enter();` (see `Hitbox.OnAreaEntered`), `PoolManager.Release` defers itself while the guard is active, monitoring/shape/process-mode writes go through the `PhysicsSafeSetters` extensions (`SetMonitoringSafe`/`SetMonitorableSafe`/`SetShapeDisabledSafe`/`SetProcessModeSafe`), and trigger callbacks that spawn collision bodies (wave triggers, `RoomTransitionTrigger`) hop off the flush with `Callable.From(...).CallDeferred()`. Direct calls — which is how tests drive these paths — stay fully synchronous; never "fix" one of these sites with an unconditional `CallDeferred` that would break that.
- **Encounter locks (2026-10-12) are built in `BuildLevel`** (built later, a resume baseline cannot restore them Cleared and their members spawn behind a resumed hero), and lock membership is registered at the spawn call, never inferred from a node's parent. Keep checkpoints, fonts and sealing anchors out of lock areas, Middle and PreBoss anchors reachable before the next lock's engage line, and a font's channel circle clear of every barrier; never make a lock require a puzzle that legitimately resets on a rewind (a lock-required objective is one-way), and derive a puzzle objective from `EncounterLock.IsRequiredPuzzleMet`. A real-scene suite that walks a hero through a level without testing locks may set `EncounterLock.SuppressForTesting` in `try`/`finally`; a test that activates a checkpoint past a lock must clear that lock first; a test that stops a pooled member's physics restarts it before the body goes back to the pool. The full authoring guide is `docs/handoffs/GU_E.md`, with the fix-round additions in `GU_FIX_ENEMY.md`, `GU_FIX_OBJECTIVE.md` and `GU_FIX_LEVEL.md`; `EncounterLockContentTests`, `EncounterLockObjectiveContentTests` and `AnchorApproachContentTests` enforce it on every campaign scene.
- **Whoever sets `SceneTree.Paused` hands it back, including on teardown.** Release it in `_ExitTree` as well as on the normal path (`DialogueManager`, `PauseMenu`), and restore it in a test's `finally`. A leaked pause freezes the next scene in game, and under GdUnit4 it stops the runner's transport node and hangs the whole test session. Since 2026-10-16 the pause menu and a gameplay-pausing dialogue share the tree pause: each hands it back only once the other no longer holds it, and a flow that leaves the scene from the pause releases it outright (`PauseMenuBase.ClosePauseForSceneExit`), because the threaded scene load needs an unpaused tree. A third owner is not covered yet (`VERIFY-NEAR-CAPTURE-PAUSE-OWNER`).
- Keep Story-only progression modifiers out of shared Fighter stats and ability resources. **Nothing in `scripts/FighterSim/` may consume Story progression** — no Resonance grid values, no `StoryAbilityPerks`, no `StoryDifficultyTuning`, no `EnemyStaggerRules`, no `TimeFreezeController`, no `Suppression`, no `LegacyUnlockSchedule`, and since 2026-10-12 no `StoryEngagementRules`, `StoryEngagementDirector` or `EncounterLockRules`. `ResonanceReaderCoverageTests.NothingUnderTheFighterSimNamesAResonanceOrStoryPerkType`, `InfluenceMeterSourceScanTests` and `StoryEngagementDirectorTests.NothingInTheFighterSimNamesTheEngagementDirector` now guard the Resonance, meter and turn-taking halves of this rule structurally. `CharacterFactory.CreateCharacter(..., applyStoryProgression: false)` remains the isolation seam, and A7b added a **separate** `applyLegacyUnlockLocks` parameter so F20's Hard-only perk mirroring can coexist with "core-kit access independent of Story ability locks" — do not collapse the two back into one switch. Conversely the Legacy Unlock Schedule and Suppression's ability lock are **Story-only gates** (the Nexus Ultimate authorization was deleted with Level 4A): Fighter Mode, CPU matches, the hub Holodeck and the Calibration Drills always run full normalized kits.
- **No level may require a specific character's abilities.** V01c's one exception was Level 4A, retired with S27, so the rule now has no exception. *(Historical:* a `LegacyKitGate` grants that ability no general puzzle eligibility anywhere else, may require only a **canonical kit slot** and never a purchased Resonance node, and a Traversal gate's landing box must sit where the hero is **during** the movement-ability cast rather than at the far landing, because `LegacyKitGate.TraversalGraceFrames` was only 30.*)*
- Preserve historical framing and the selected character's ability identity when adding mechanics; mechanics should not become generic solely for implementation convenience.
- Update translation keys, save schemas/migrations, rollback snapshots, pools, and tests whenever a changed data field affects them.
- Preserve unrelated user changes in this working tree. Do not clean, reset, or rewrite generated/user-modified files outside the task.

## Validation expectations

For ordinary C# changes:

1. Run `dotnet build FightersThroughTime.csproj` from the repository root.
2. Open/run the affected Godot scene when a Godot executable is available. The main flow starts at `scenes/menus/MainMenu.tscn`; `scenes/arenas/TestArena.tscn` is the current focused combat sandbox.
3. Check the Godot output for missing scripts, invalid `res://` paths, node-name mismatches, invalid exported resources, and signal/event lifecycle errors.
4. For gameplay changes, test state interruption, death/respawn, pause, and scene transitions in addition to the happy path.
5. For combat/system changes, consider both Story Mode and Fighter Mode and explicitly confirm that Story progression cannot leak into competitive values.
6. For input changes, test keyboard and two-controller device assignment.
7. For pooled objects, exercise capacity/overflow and repeated spawn-release cycles.
8. For save changes, test empty slots, round-trip serialization, corrupt/old data handling, and migration.
9. For future deterministic networking work, add headless repeatability/desync tests before treating the system as usable online.

GdUnit4 v6.2.0 is installed. Run the headless suite with a valid console Godot path. On the current development machine both Godot 4.7.1 .NET executables live in `D:\Projects\`, one directory above the repository:

```powershell
$env:GODOT_BIN = "D:\Projects\Godot_v4.7.1-stable_mono_win64_console.exe"
dotnet test FightersThroughTime.csproj --settings .runsettings
```

Verify the reported `Total:` count against the baseline below. If GdUnit4 cannot launch Godot it logs `Rebuilding Godot Project ends with exit code: -1073741819`, runs only the ~21 pure-C# tests, and still exits 0 — a green exit code alone does not mean the suite passed. That happens when `D:\Projects\GodotSharp\` is missing or the Godot binary is separated from it; Godot then aborts with `.NET: Assemblies not found`. A **green `Passed!` line followed by `The active test run was aborted … Test host process crashed`** is a different thing again — CLAUDE.md failure signature 7, a pure-C# `[TestSuite]` that touched a Godot API. If a run reports a large partial `Total:` with a negative exit code, read `docs/PACKAGE5_CAMPAIGN_PLAN.md` §9 and CLAUDE.md's failure signatures before assuming a real regression. A partial total with **0 failures and a positive exit code 100** — or a plausible-looking green `Total: 73` in about twelve seconds — is CLAUDE.md failure signature 5 (cross-worktree GdUnit pipe contention), not a regression: never run GdUnit concurrently across checkouts, and always compare `Total:` against the exact expected number. A **full** `Total:` with about ten Story cast or movement-ability cases stuck in `Idle` / `Running` / `Airborne` is CLAUDE.md failure signature 8: a suite leaked `GameManager.CurrentSession.ActiveSaveSlot` onto a real `user://saves` slot.

The current baseline is **3529** passing tests, after the 2026-10-18 retro v2 sprite and VFX pass (the bullet of that name under Known prototype gaps; the ledger section "Confirmed open deviations — retro v2 sprite and VFX pass, 2026-10-18"), verified across three consecutive full runs on 2026-10-19 on the uncommitted pass over `d12e8eb` (`Failed: 0, Passed: 3529, Total: 3529`; 1 m 37 s, 1 m 37 s and 1 m 38 s) with orphan processes drained and the lock directory held for each, and again three times on 2026-10-20 after its locomotion-loop follow-up (1 m 39 s, 1 m 40 s and 1 m 41 s). The arithmetic is exact: 3518 **+11** — the new `AbilityVisualPresentationTests` 5 (a projectile holds the travel frame, a synced Fighter proxy keeps animating, a released proxy hides), `RetroSpriteScaleNormalizerTests` 4 → 8 (the metadata helper, a baked sheet never boosted, a per-animation feet gap, mipmaps only above 1×), `OwnershipOutlineTests` 4 → 5 (outline thickness scales with the texel scale) and `CharacterPresentationTests` 7 → 8 (`EveryRosterSpriteSetIsRetroV2AtTwoTimesTexels`). Changed in place with no count change: `CharacterPresentationTests`' frame-count case (idle 4, run 6, else 3; run 8 since 2026-10-20, when the case also began failing on a looping fall or a non-looping idle or run) and VFX-cell case (192 × the texel scale), and `OutlineGlowShaderTests`' thickness range (0–10). The flag also made the suite faster (~70 s, down from ~100 s). GdUnit's `Detected <N> orphan nodes` warning varies between runs on an identical build and is not a failure.
