## Known prototype gaps to remember

- **Cross-mode basic-combat parity landed 2026-08-09, revised the same day for attack-on-the-move** (swings steer freely and movement never breaks the chain — see the Combat section bullet; post-Package-8 pass; audit `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT_2026-08-08.md` themes H-6/M-12/M-17 closed, H-5/M-13/M-14 narrowed): the shared `BasicComboRules` rulebook, the sim's phased basic string, Story's recovery/hold cancels and special-cancel, the grounded block stance plus interval regen in both modes, unified basics hitstun, replace-velocity knockback, and Story's intended 4-frame air-accel constant. **Still divergent, deliberately deferred** (each risks stage-geometry or campaign retunes and needs its own pass): gravity/jump physics (H-7 — **narrowed 2026-10-08** by `USER-MOVEMENT-FEEL`: both modes now share the 6 / 4-frame ramps and the jump-arc shape, meaning the apex hang, the ×2.2 fall, the 12 u/s normal-control terminal and the ramped 15 u/s fast-fall; **narrowed again 2026-10-09** by the user's "Document both builds" ruling: the per-mode base gravities (Story weight-coupled 18 × (0.8 + 0.4 × Weight), 21.6 u/s² at Weight 1.0; the sim a flat 30 with no weight term) are now **intended** and the GDD states both, so they are no longer a divergence; **narrowed a third time 2026-10-16** by the combat and movement update: the sim gained the ×2.5 short hop, which in both modes cuts only a rise from the fighter's own Jump press (decision 5.1 with Q5, `USER-SIM-SHORT-HOP`, `USER-SHORT-HOP-OWN-JUMP-ONLY`), and the hang needs Jump held in both (6.1), so the sim's jumps are no longer fixed-height; still divergent, as `DEFER-H7-STORY-TERMINAL`, are the launch band (Story ×1.8 with a 10 u/s clamp, the sim flat with a 20 u/s clamp), its launch gravity and Story's launch-band short-hop clause), the Fighter-side universal movement set (M-16 — **ledge grab landed 2026-08-10**, and its get-ups and first-grab intangibility on 2026-10-16; crouch, skid, coyote time, and jump buffer remain sim-absent), and per-ability authored hitstun for specials/ultimates in the sim (M-13 remainder — **the generic half closed with Package 12 W3b**: melee Special/Ultimate intents and Special projectiles read the projected `FighterAbilityHitData` through `FighterHitContractTable`; the bespoke per-kit pulse constants are still open under `DEFER-SIM-ABILITY-HITSTUN`). **M-18 (drop-through input) closed on 2026-10-16** (decision 6.3, `USER-DROP-THROUGH-BOTH-INPUTS`): the double tap of Down and Down+Jump drop through in both modes, the sim's tap window living on the new component 323; it had stayed open by deliberate decision only because 305 and 310 were full at 128 bytes and any new sim state needed a new Klotho component. **Floor-segment ledges landed with Package 11 A9**, extending M-16's ledge-grab half to Open-stage main floors. The 2026-08-10 gameplay-feel batch also superseded two 2026-08-09 decisions by user directive: the whole-string facing commit (facing now follows held movement mid-swing) and the chain-holding hitstun (a grounded blocking victim escapes the string after hit one). **The 2026-10-11 string-hitbox pass** moved the grounded string boxes into one table (`FIX-STRING-BOX-ONE-TABLE`) and recorded the hit-geometry divergences it found, each open for a user ruling: the reach factor scales the X offset where the design keeps offsets fixed (`VERIFY-STRING-REACH-OFFSET-SCALING`), Story's hit 2 sits below the hurtbox centre (`VERIFY-STORY-STRING-HIT2-LIFT`), Story's own aerial string table (`VERIFY-STORY-AERIAL-STRING-BOXES`, **closed 2026-10-11 by D6b**: the Story aerial string boxes share the grounded x-span, front-only, and only their vertical span is Story-authored; the aerial string itself was retired on 2026-10-12 by the single air strike, which keeps that rule), the sim re-reading facing on every active tick (`VERIFY-SIM-STRING-FACING-PER-TICK`, **closed 2026-10-16** by decision 10.1: the sim latches the box facing at active start, as Story does, `USER-SIM-BOX-FACING-LATCH`), every Story hero's 40 × 64 px hurtbox (`VERIFY-STORY-HURTBOX-UNIVERSAL`; the sim half is `VERIFY-SIM-HURTBOX-WIDTH`; since 2026-10-16 each mode states its value once, decision 8.4, and the target size is still open) and the directional strikes' pivot-versus-box hit tests (`VERIFY-DIRECTIONAL-HIT-MODEL`). **Deliberate mode differences since 2026-10-12:** Story knockback is static while the sim keeps the missing-HP ramp (`USER-STORY-STATIC-KNOCKBACK`), and the closer camera, encounter locks and enemy turn-taking are Story-only; the single air strike and the −20 % speed cut apply to both modes alike. **The 2026-10-16 combat and movement update** made more one rule in both modes — Fighter Specials keep their authored frames as Story's do (decision 1.2; Story still fires a Special's effect one tick earlier, `VERIFY-SAME-TICK-VERB-PARITY` (8)), and the cast brake, D3, the perfect block, scaled shieldstun, the post-hitstun tumble, Defy's early end, the air-dodge refresh, the held-Jump hang, the own-jump short hop and both drop-through inputs are shared — and added deliberate differences: respawn protection (Q8) and the ledge get-ups (5.2) are Fighter-only, the Ultimate startup armour, the ability shake, the 10.5 Echo Step cost, hazard blocking, the basic floor and the dimmed numbers are Story-only, and corner forgiveness differs by geometry (Story's head-clip nudge and wall-top lip pop; the sim's one-way platform lip pop, because no sim ceiling is partial).

- **The 2026-08-08 implementation audit's actionable findings were fixed on 2026-08-10** (`docs/IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT_2026-08-08.md` §"Resolution record" has the full list). Standing-behavior changes to know: pooled `Hitbox`es and construct hurtboxes rebind on `_EnterTree` (pooled damage delivery had silently died after one pool cycle — the audit's one Critical); kill dust is awarded exactly once through the physical pickup and `DustEarnedThisLevel` is wallet receipts with mob/extractor/boss itemization — extended 2026-08-26 by the **V7.3 Single Icon Rule**: boss and extractor awards are physical pickups too (`StoryDropSystem.SpawnDustAward(amount, position, parent, source)` — pooled `chronal_dust`, never expires, forced Large tier, deferred off a physics flush; headless/no-pool fallback pays the wallet directly), the wallet event fires once at COLLECTION (`ChronalDustPickup.Collect()`), and the boss/extractor results-line attribution rides the new `OnDustAwardCollected` payload at that same moment (Level 13's Mirror Paradox deliberately stays wallet-direct with explicit attribution); the Story pause is Resume/Settings/Save/Restart/Exit, and its Exit now charges the voluntary 20 % fee on undeposited dust — the player keeps 80 %, rounded down (`SessionExitGuard.CalculateExitRetainedDust`; the 50 % retention this fix round shipped is superseded); the tutorial calibration was **rebuilt for V7.6** (see the Level 0 bullet — the special/ultimate/movement lessons are deleted); Story specials/ultimates are interrupted by stun or death (hyper-armor exempted at the hit-resolution site) and start cooldowns at cast; Fighter matches roll a per-match seed (rematches reroll; the Package 7 handshake must later agree on it); the Fighter select is the designed two-token flow (device-separated cursors, READY, 3 s cancelable countdown, stage phase; the original duplicate prevention was removed 2026-08-26 by V7.3 ruling #17 — mirror matches are allowed); the CPU observes projectiles per its difficulty bands; the Mirror Paradox freezes and clears during rewinds; pause/low-health/ultimate audio snapshots and the full haptic table are wired; Settings is controller-navigable; legacy save migration is debug-builds-only and binding restore is filtered to remappable actions. Still open by design decision (do not close silently): the five authored-but-never-spawned elites vs. the locked E=0 economy rows (M-5), hazard/orb spawn-timing randomization, cross-mode pushbox unification, and the eight widened-scope talent minors. **H-11 (the bottom-blast-zone pillar vs. the solid-floor stage dossiers, including Paris's flattened pit) closed with Package 11 A9** — three Open stages author real pits and the blast zone is reachable through ordinary play. The **Single Icon Rule** note above is also amended by F05: the boss still forces a Large plate, but an ordinary Extractor now shows the icon its actual award earns. Checkpoint strike-to-activate closed 2026-08-26 with the V7.3 story/core batch (see the Story progression section); extractor idle discharges had already shipped with V7.2. H-5 (the 5 s shatter lockout and shieldstun) closed 2026-08-26 with the V7.3 block-model pass — see the Combat section bullet; since 2026-10-16 a perfect block spends no charge and sets no shieldstun, and an ordinary block's shieldstun scales with damage (decisions 3.4 and 4.1).
- The menu, character select, test arena, hub, and **all sixteen campaign levels (0–15)** exist as authored scenes (Package 5 closed 2026-08-08), and **every production UI surface, the audio framework, the visual framework and complete controls/settings landed with Package 8** (closed 2026-08-08). Presentation content throughout is still placeholder — the frameworks are real, the assets are not; see the bullets below.
- **Nobody has visually judged any of the Package 8 output — and nobody has judged Package 11's, Package 12's or Package 13's either.** Headless gates prove scenes load, node types are right, shaders compile, ambient tones clear a readability floor and every string resolves; no automated gate can say whether the outline reads at gameplay scale, whether the parallax works at the camera's 1.4× zoom, whether an era's lighting lands, whether the dialogue glass panel looks like glass, whether the Integrity era-clock is legible mid-fight, whether the Collapse Tremor reads as pressure or as noise, whether the two-colour grammar lands, whether an Open stage's pit is readable at the camera's framing, whether Tubman's placeholder art reads, whether the 2026-10-18 retro v2 sprites, effects and construct props read at gameplay scale in either mode, whether the Level 0 hold, the triage readout, the send-off or the Homecoming land, or whether any reworked kit is fun. **Treat the whole visual layer as unreviewed until a human opens the editor.**
- What is still missing from the campaign is **production assets**, not structure and no longer hooks: Package 8 (closed 2026-08-08) built every presentation framework and filled it with contract-conformant placeholders, so art, animation libraries, real music stems, real VFX art and cinematic boss presentation are now asset-replacement work in **Package 10**. **Florence's three Chronal Extractors were authored by Package 11 A3** (`florence_extractor_0/1/2`, one per traversable room, none in the boss arena), closing that content gap.
- **Package 10 pass 1 landed 2026-08-10** (generated vector art, one surface each, from the `D:\Projects\fighters-through-time-docs-2\mockups\` reference set): `tools/generate_assets.gd` is a headless SVG→PNG pipeline (jobs in `tools/asset_src/jobs.json`; sources under the `.gdignore`d `tools/asset_src/`, previews in its `preview/`) that rasterizes through the same ThorVG Godot imports with, plus deterministic procedural atmosphere textures. Wired so far: the main menu's Temporal Glass treatment (starfield + era-shard hero art under the pinned `Background` node, per-scene glass `StyleBoxTexture` overrides on the root buttons, the `DialogueGlassPanel` variation on the sub-screen panels — MainMenu's authored node paths, keys and focus chains unchanged); Einstein's `einstein_frames.tres` rebuilt as a 62-frame paper-doll set over the same 27 animation names (96×128, feet-on-origin, `tools/asset_src/einstein/build_einstein.py` emits frames and the .tres) plus a real vector portrait in `einstein_portrait.svg` (same path, so the Fighter HUD/loading/select pick it up); and Level 6 Pompeii's backdrop (`Parallax2D` sky/Vesuvius/skyline/ruins layers in the scene) with textured surfaces via the new opt-in `StoryLevelControllerBase.SurfaceTextures` hook (`LevelSurfaceTextures`; null default keeps every other level's ColorRect graybox, and an explicit builder fill still wins). The menu portion was expanded in a visually verified second pass: three text-free raster plates under `assets/backgrounds/menu/` now distinguish the root menu, shared submenus, and the cyan/violet fighter-select timeline; the opt-in `TemporalGlassButton` theme variation is used by MainMenu, CharacterSelect, Settings, StoryPause, LocalFighterPause, and confirmation/fallback pause controls while preserving localization keys, node contracts, and focus chains. Manifest statuses deliberately stay `Placeholder` (`CharacterPresentationTests` pins that for character visual sets), the fighter-select card frames (`assets/sprites/ui/card_frame*.png`) are generated but not yet wired into the select screens, Einstein's set still carries now-unused `dash` frames from before the dash removal, and non-Einstein character portraits remain placeholders.
- **Package 10 retro raster pass landed 2026-08-10 and supersedes the pass-1 Einstein paper doll** (its character and VFX atlases are superseded in turn by the 2026-10-18 retro v2 pass below; the hub, Florence and menu art still stand): `einstein_frames.tres` now contains 78 atlas regions (three frames for each of the 26 required/runtime animations; no retired `dash`) over five 96×128-cell retro-pulp PNG atlases under `assets/sprites/characters/einstein/retro/`. `tools/build_retro_einstein_spriteframes.py` deterministically rebuilds the character and ability-effect resources. Einstein's three-hit standard combo now uses two cyan temporal-energy slashes followed by a tall upward launcher, with three hand-inked retro-pulp frames per attack in the existing combat atlas/runtime names. Story explicitly starts each `basic_attack_N` sprite animation alongside its callback timeline; Fighter presentation gives the sprite a pausable clock while the simulation-owned body remains disabled. The obsolete yellow `MeleeHitVisual` rectangle is no longer created; the real collision hitbox is unchanged. All nine `CharacterData` resources now point to distinct 512×512 transparent retro-pulp PNG portraits under `assets/sprites/characters/portraits/`, feeding character select, loading, Story HUD, and Fighter HUD from the existing shared field. A four-row ability VFX atlas and four pooled Einstein scenes present Mass-Energy Conversion, Relativity Rift, Relativity Warp, and The Cosmological Constant; Story projectiles/zones opt into the raster art by `AbilityID` while the four AbilityData resources remain on the six-scene shared taxonomy required by `AbilityVfxAssignmentTests`. The Time-Ship hub now layers horizontally repeating far/mid ship art plus a holodeck overlay, and Florence uses repeating far/mid `Parallax2D` skyline art instead of the old copied single plate. All were visually captured in live scenes; the full suite passed 1417/1417.
- **The 2026-08-11 sprite-size pass normalizes figure scale at runtime** (since the 2026-10-18 retro v2 pass it still serves enemies and bosses, while a roster sheet whose `SpriteFrames` carries `retro_baked_scale` gets factor 1 and the sheet's own per-animation feet lines; the 0.845 factory scale is now divided by the sheet's texel scale). The generated retro sheets share their cell grids and 3 px feet baseline, but the drawn figure's scale drifts wildly between animations of one sheet (enemy attack rows at roughly half the idle figure; character ability atlases at ~60%). Correcting the PNGs is impossible without clipping cell width, so `FTT.Combat.RetroSpriteScaleNormalizer` (attached under every body `AnimatedSprite2D` by `CharacterFactory`, `EnemyController`, and `BossController`) measures each animation's opaque art height once per SpriteFrames via `Image.GetUsedRect` (cached process-wide), boosts undersized animations toward a per-pose target fraction of the sheet's idle height (never shrinking, clamped at 2.0×), and re-anchors the art's bottom row at parent y = 0 (the feet-on-origin convention) on every animation change. Base scales were rebased in the same pass and raised again by a second +30% user request the same day: the character factory sprite is **0.845** (0.5 → 0.65 → 0.845), `StandardEnemy.tscn` **0.845** (was 1.0 — enemies used to render at twice the player's pixel density before the pass), `EliteEnemy.tscn` **1.014**, `Boss.tscn` **0.845** (the pass also fixed boss feet sitting 40 px below the floor with the 256 px authored cells). Facing must remain `FlipH` — the normalizer owns the body sprite's `Scale` and `Position`; collision, pushboxes, and all gameplay tuning were deliberately untouched. `RetroSpriteScaleNormalizerTests` pins the factor math, clamps, and feet anchoring. Nobody has visually reviewed the result in the editor yet.
- **Package 10 roster sprite expansion landed 2026-08-10** (every character and VFX atlas it describes is superseded by the 2026-10-18 retro v2 pass below; its raw sources stay under `retro_roster_sprites/`): Joan, Leonardo, Lincoln, Cleopatra, Tesla, Shakespeare, Mozart, and Pocahontas (since replaced — **Tubman's** set was generated by Package 13 W5 to the same contract from original SVG sources, `tools/asset_src/tubman/`, through a new `svg_atlas` job type in `generate_assets.gd`) now match Einstein's retro-pulp runtime contract: five transparent 96×128-cell character atlases, 26 animation names with exactly three frames each (**30 since Package 12 W10 / H03**: `up_attack`, `down_air` — renamed from the retro set's `down_attack` — and placeholder `grab`/`throw` rows reusing the `basic_attack_1`/`basic_attack_3` combat regions; `resources/Contracts/player_presentation_contract.tres` lists all 30, and `build_retro_einstein_spriteframes.py` now delegates to the roster builder), and one four-row 192×192-cell ability-VFX atlas per character. `tools/build_retro_character_spriteframes.py` rebuilds all nine character/VFX resources. `AbilityVisualLibrary` resolves every canonical ability ID; Story cast, projectile, zone, and shared VFX paths use those atlases, while Fighter proxies and accepted ability inputs present the correct character pose/effect without writing into deterministic state. Raw/keyed sources, prompt records, packing helpers, and visual-QA contact sheets live under `D:\Projects\fighters-through-time-docs-2\mockups\implementation_assets_v3\retro_roster_sprites\`. The focused presentation/content suites pass 12/12. A contemporaneous full run executed 1441 tests (1434 passing); its seven failures are the in-flight gameplay-feel branch's level-reachability assertions after movement-stat changes, not roster-art failures.
- **The retro v2 sprite and VFX pass landed 2026-10-18/19** (user request: improve every roster character's spritesheets, animations and ability effects in the docs mockups' retro-pulp style; ledger section "Confirmed open deviations — retro v2 sprite and VFX pass, 2026-10-18"). All nine characters were redrawn through Codex's `image_gen` (a metered plan, so generation was paced across usage windows) from a model sheet per character and dense planned pose sheets, then processed by `tools/retro_sprites/`: `segment.py` cuts each sheet into figures (sheet overrides restate a layout the model drew differently and cut figures an effect joins), `pack.py` scales each sheet by its plain standing reference figure to the character's standing height, finds each frame's pivot and feet, derives the code-built poses, registers the looping frames to each other and writes six atlases per character under `assets/sprites/characters/<id>/retro/` plus `tools/retro_sprites/manifests/<id>.json`, and `tools/build_retro_character_spriteframes.py` writes the `SpriteFrames` from the manifest. By user ruling the art is at **2× texels** (figures never shrunk to fit a cell; cells are sized per animation) and **idle has 4 frames and run 8** (6 until 2026-10-20), every other animation keeping three key poses (`design-godot.md` :3810, :3819–3820, :3822, :3928 and :4877 amended in the master and the mirror). Since a 2026-10-20 user request the idle is a fighting stance with the hands or weapon up, bobbing a little in place; the run is an 8-frame contact / down / passing / flight cycle at a per-character rate (`MaxMoveSpeed` × 2.6 fps, 10–16); and the fall plays once and holds its pose until landing (ledger `USER-IDLE-FIGHTING-STANCE`, `USER-RUN-EIGHT-FRAME-CYCLE`, `USER-FALL-HOLDS-POSE`, `PROVISIONAL-RETRO-V2-LOOP-RULES`). Runtime: `FTT.Combat.RetroSpriteMetadata` reads the builder's `retro_texel_scale` (2.0), `retro_baked_scale` and per-animation `retro_feet_gaps`; `CharacterFactory.CharacterSpriteWorldScale` (0.845) is divided by the texel scale with `LinearWithMipmaps` filtering, `GlowPresentationController` doubles both outline thicknesses on a 2× sheet, `VfxEffect` and `AbilityVisualLibrary` divide their call-site scales by it, and every v2 atlas imports with mipmaps, so a standing figure is still about 103 world px; texture memory per character is 41–51 MiB uncompressed with mipmaps (about 4 MiB before), with each atlas shelf-packed by `pack.py` (no side over 4096) and every cell keeping a 12-texel margin on every side (under the feet too, so a status outline is never clipped by the quad). The ability VFX were redrawn the same way at 384 px cells (`vfx_pack.py`, fitted to each old row's footprint), the four Story constructs (Tesla coil, clockwork turret, serpent nest, sonata platform) got props in place of tinted graybox tiles (`assets/sprites/constructs/`, `construct_pack.py`), and Tubman got a new portrait. Fixed on the way: a Story projectile held its burst frame through its flight and now holds the row's travel frame; Fighter projectile, construct and zone proxies restarted at frame 0 every sync; `ledge_hang` is anchored by its grip 52 world px above the origin, so the hands meet the edge; a Down-Air strike's body is pinned on its hurtbox with the weapon and impact hanging below it (`feet_at` overrides). Derived poses (breathing crouch, block and defeat, a swaying hang, a wobbling daze, a hologram respawn) stand in for drawn frames, and a skid whose middle frame the model drew as a full slide reuses its braking pose. Raw sheets, prompts, Codex session logs, review sheets and the snapshots of the replaced atlases are under `D:\Projects\fighters-through-time-docs-2\mockups\implementation_assets_v3\retro_roster_v2\` (its `README.md` is the record); `tools/capture_character_sprites.gd` renders contact pages of any roster's frames through the real normalizer (it needs a window). Suites: `AbilityVisualPresentationTests` (5), `RetroSpriteScaleNormalizerTests` (+4), `OwnershipOutlineTests` (+1), `CharacterPresentationTests` (+1, `EveryRosterSpriteSetIsRetroV2AtTwoTimesTexels`). Manifest statuses stay `Placeholder`. **Nobody has looked at it in play** (`VERIFY-RETRO-V2-HUMAN-REVIEW`).
- Much of the environment and ability presentation is code-generated placeholder geometry, but it is now **driven** rather than dead. Player characters render from per-character placeholder `SpriteFrames`/portraits wired through `CharacterData`; enemies and bosses render from the authored `scenes/enemies/` scenes with `EnemyData.PlaceholderTint`/`SpriteFramesResource`. On top of that Package 8 added the shared `outline_glow.gdshader` with `GlowPresentationController` as the **single owner** of a sprite's material/tint state (three independent channels: base tint, tint override, arbitrated outline stack — so a fighter tint survives a status ending), a pooled `VfxEmitter` that finally consumes `AbilityData.CastVFXScene`/`ImpactVFXScene` on all 36 abilities and binds all 78 roster `PresentationEventID` hooks through `RosterVfxMap`, a shared 500-particle budget registry with steal-oldest eviction, `VisibleOnScreenNotifier2D` off-screen suspension, and `StageLightingRig` (`CanvasModulate` + textured `PointLight2D`s) on all ten Fighter stages plus the hub and levels 2 and 6. Grounding/glow corrections from the first human look (2026-08-09): the shared radial light gradient (`assets/placeholders/glow_light_gradient.tres`) interpolates linearly — the stepped gradient drew every light and the per-fighter slot glow as hard-edged bullseye rings — and the placeholder sprites sit with their feet on the body origin (the factory carried a scale-1.0 offset at 0.5 scale, floating every character 32 px in both modes; Boss/Elite scenes and the TestArena floor carried sibling offsets). Fighter stage backgrounds are `Parallax2D` (world-space, so they now zoom with the camera).
- Enemy and boss runtime is data-driven as of Package 4 A1: `EnemyAbilityData` (`MeleeStrike, Projectile, Shockwave, ChargeDash, ShieldBubble, AreaPulse, SummonMinions, Teleport`, plus V7.3's append-only `PersistentFieldAtTarget`) executed by the shared `EnemyAbilityExecutor` for both elite secondary abilities and every boss attack, with telegraph/active/recovery frames at 60 Hz, pooled `enemy_projectile` shots, difficulty-scaled reaction delays (`StoryDifficultyTuning.ScaleReactionDelayFrames`), weighted boss selection with distance filtering and an explicit empty-filter fallback, phase gating/speed multipliers, telegraph interruption, and `BossEncounterController` for HUD/dust/rewind wiring. Since 2026-10-09 a Teleport's landing and a SummonMinions spawn point are validated by `FTT.Combat.RelocationRules` (never through a wall, never over nothing; the boss-arena bullet under Scene architecture). Chase holds a stand-off band as of 2026-08-10: enemies (and bosses' rest-window tracking) stop advancing at 85% of their own authored attack range and only resume beyond 110% (`StandOffEngageFraction`/`StandOffResumeFraction`), attacking from the band instead of pressing into the target's pushbox. **Since D6c (2026-10-11)** a mob whose next attack is its MeleeStrike / ChargeDash primary instead commits inside its own box's reach against the hero minus 10 px and holds just outside pushbox contact (Combat, the Story enemy combat rules bullet). The complete roster landed 2026-08-07: 27 `EnemyData` (era standards/elites plus the cultist mobs) and 15 `BossData` resources with 76 `EnemyAbilityData` kits, all registered `Implemented/ReadyForReplacement/Valid` in `resources/Content/content_manifest.csv`, all localized, and all covered by content tests. **Package 11 A7a added the 28th enemy, the Eraser** (`resources/Enemies/unbound_eraser.tres`, Elite tier) with three kits: `null_lance` (a `MeleeStrike` that is explicitly **Basic**-class via `ForcesBasicBlockClass` — the one authored opt-out from the elite Guard-Crush implicit), `siphon_snare` (the new `EnemyAbilityArchetype.SiphonTether = 9`: a 180-frame non-damaging channel with no hitbox that drains Influence, through `SiphonTetherChannel` and its static one-tether registry), and `lance_jab`. `EnemyController.ResolveGuardCrush(ability)` is now the single Guard-Crush decision point, and `PlayerController.TryAbsorbNonDamagingCast` routes a zero-damage payload through `BlockSystem.ResolveHit` rather than reimplementing block rules. **The Eraser and the Chrono-Warden are salted, not saturated:** the Eraser debuts in **Level 5** (moved from the retired Level 4A by Package 13 W2; the Level 0 watcher silhouette foreshadows it) and appears in 7 (a first salted bark, `dlg_l07_eraser_bark`), 9 (the near-capture silhouette only), 12, 13 (a pair, with `level_13.eraser_pair`), 14 and 15; the Chrono-Warden finally has spawn sites at all, debuting in Level 6 and appearing in 9 and 14 (closing recon G8 — it had a resource, three kits, a manifest row, localization and two behaviour suites, and zero spawns anywhere). Levels 8, 10 and 11 are deliberately untouched. **Since 2026-10-12 the hunters the encounter locks adopted are ledgered lock members** (a lock member must come from a manifest row): Level 6's and Level 9's Chrono-Wardens, Level 7's Eraser (moved off the open boarding channel onto the ramp deck), the Erasers of Levels 12–15 (all moved onto their routes and out of the arenas) and Level 14's Chrono-Warden now pay an elite share of their level's unchanged pool, and Easy's per-row prefix still drops each (`VERIFY-ERASER-DUST`).
- **Boss changes (Package 11 A7b).** Exactly the four V7.6 2.E HP rows were applied — `borgia_inquisitor` 500 → **350**, `siegemaster_duke` 540 → **520**, `chronal_inventor` 560 → **700**, `revolutionary_tribunal` 590 → **850** — and **the other ten Act II/III pools are untouched and in open conflict with the design table**, recorded as `VERIFY-BOSS-HP` (owner Unassigned, acceptance = one explicit user ruling applied to all fifteen rows at once). Do not "reconcile" that curve. `BossRosterActITests` retired its 500-anchor / 500–700 band and now pins the four authored values literally, asserting ascension only across the unchanged tail. Two new authored bool flags, both on `apex_eraser` alone: **`HasHistoricalRecovery`** (T01b — a 181-sample position/HP ring recorded once per authoritative tick, an exact 180-frame lookback, `heal = max(0, min(P − H, floor(0.20·M), M − H))`, a historical → current → authored-anchor destination chain, and a new `BossState.HistoricalRecovery` whose 90-frame beat runs before every other clock on the controller so statuses, Conductive marks, cooldowns and an in-flight hitstop all hold) and **`BorrowsRosterLegacies`** (V7.5 — at the final phase the boss's kit is augmented with one projected `EnemyAbilityData` per borrowed character, built from each one's **Special 1** `AbilityData` by shape, from `CampaignCaptiveRoster` minus the active hero, **never a literal nine-element list**; the projections are runtime-only and never written to disk or entered in the manifest). `archive_remnants.tres` is retained unchanged — augment, not replace.
- **Boss phase rules and squads (Package 12 W9; handoff `docs/handoffs/P12_W9.md`).** Design §6 Rule 1 — every phase changes a *rule*, never only a speed multiplier — is pinned over all fifteen campaign bosses by `BossPhaseRuleMatrixTests`. `BossData` gained `BossPhaseTrigger { HpThreshold (default), MemberDefeat }` and the squad fields `SquadMemberCount` / `AbilityMember` (derived `MemberMaxHP` = `MaxHP / SquadMemberCount`), plus data-driven phase mechanics: `TeleportDecoyMinPhase` (Jackal Priest sand decoys, `BossDecoy`), `GuardedSummonMinPhase` (the Tragedy King is invulnerable mid-soliloquy until his summoned actors fall or bow), and `ArenaGuardianMinPhase` / `ArenaGuardianHP` (level-placed `ArenaGuardian`s — the Chronal Inventor's corner coils — shield the boss via `BossController.RegisterGuardian`). `BossController` raises `PhaseEntered` and supports `EnterPhase`, `AbsorbSquadMember` and `RaisesDefeatPayload`; `BossEncounterController` spawns one body per squad member, shows one summed HUD bar, re-raises `PhaseEntered` once per phase for level arenas, hands a fallen member's abilities to the survivor and raises **one** defeat payload (one 25-dust award). The Revolutionary Tribunal is a 2 × 425 `MemberDefeat` squad (850 total unchanged; knockback-immune false, rest 1.0) whose Phase 2 collapses Level 4's courtyard centre into a **non-lethal** ramped pit (its floor, y 1120 — `Level04Controller.TribunalPitFloorY` — lies 40 px below the arena's 1080 bottom edge, which is why the 2026-10-09 arena fall net fires only 240 px below an arena's edge; `VERIFY-L04-PIT-FALL-NET`, closed). The Borgia Inquisitor's Phase 2 has no speed multiplier: it adds `after_image_dash` (`EnemyAbilityData.LeavesAfterImages`, suppressed by Reduced Temporal Effects) and Level 1 burns the scaffolding and closes the arena. `EnemyData.SecondaryAbilities` is an opt-in **standard-tier** secondary path (the elite Guard-Crush implicit is tier-gated); the Rift Phantom's `rift_step` Teleport is an engage blink (a flying wall-phaser, so the 2026-10-09 relocation rule leaves its landing as requested). Six bosses whose Phase 2 was speed-only got a stand-in phase-gated ability; their arena halves are `VERIFY-BOSS-PHASE-ARENAS`. (The 4A Tesla and Shakespeare bosses that were still speed-only were deleted with Level 4A; `VERIFY-4A-BOSS-RULE1` is closed as invalidated.)
- **The Mirror Paradox now reads the Story difficulty (F20, Package 11 A7b).** The clone's CPU tier is `StoryDifficultyTuning.CurrentStoryDifficulty`, latched once at spawn — not the hardcoded Hard engine and never the saved Holodeck difficulty — and its band is `CpuBandTuning.BossOverride(difficulty)`, with a new `EasyBoss` restoring the full core kit on Easy. HP is 700 / 1000 / 1500 from the authored 1000 base. **On Hard only** the clone mirrors purchased grid perks (`applyStoryProgression: true`), while `applyLegacyUnlockLocks: false` keeps core-kit access independent of Story ability locks at every tier. The outgoing campaign damage multiplier (0.5× / 1.0× / 1.5×) is now actually applied — the clone is a `PlayerController` and so never passed through `EnemyAbilityExecutor.DamageMultiplier`, which was a real shipped gap. Since 2026-10-11 that multiplier (it rides `StorySpecialDamageMultiplier`) also reaches the clone's pooled-projectile contacts and its constructs through the one Story damage composition (`PROVISIONAL-STORY-ABILITY-DAMAGE-ROUTING` (d)); on Hard the clone also mirrors Mozart's build-rate lane (`PROVISIONAL-STORY-METER-GAIN-SCOPE` (5)) and the repaired grid lanes. It mirrors purchased perks **once, at spawn** — the clone never listens to the live grid re-resolve, so a Beacon purchase made during the encounter does not reach it (`PROVISIONAL-GRID-LIVE-APPLY`). Its CPU reads a reach-aware melee band against the hero's real hurtbox (the CPU melee band bullet under Combat). Since 2026-10-12 no mob or boss targets the clone (`EnemyController` and `BossController.FindNearestPlayer` skip `IsStoryHostile`), the Mirror's reveal frames its arena on the hero's camera, and the clone's airborne press is the single air strike. Since 2026-10-16 the clone holds Jump for a full hop, its adapter projects its own executing Special so the CPU waits out its cast (`MirrorParadoxDecisionAdapter.ProjectSpecialCastPhase`), its activation wind-up is telegraphed, its post-contact Story Ultimate is startup-armoured, and it inherits the tumble, D3 and the Story Down+Jump and movement-ability fixes as a `PlayerController`; the Hard clone still wears the gold hero aura, a presentation question (`VERIFY-MIRROR-CLONE-HERO-AURA`). Per-level pool warm-up configs for levels 2-15 live in `resources/Pools/level_pool_configs/` and are all wired into `scene_pool_catalog.tres` as of the Package 5 closeout; `ScenePoolConfigTests` asserts each campaign scene resolves to its own `ConfigID`, because a mis-pointed row silently warms another level's budget.
- **The V7.3 remaining-story batch landed 2026-08-26** (Phase 5): the **Chrono-Warden rework** — `dilation_field.tres` moved to the new `PersistentFieldAtTarget` archetype (`FieldDurationSeconds` 4 s export, additive): the executor drops a `DilationFieldZone` (Area2D, `persistent_construct` group so the rewind freeze sweep reaches it, `IStoryRewindSimulation`) at the target position CAPTURED at cast, radius `PulseRadius`, refreshing the TimeDilation slow on every player inside per step (the status sweep reads the `"Players"` group by distance — the extractor's siphon-engage pattern — so it stays deterministic under headless direct calls); and Phase Skip is now **reactive** — a Teleport-archetype elite fires immediately via `EnemyController.TryReactivePhaseSkip()` when the target closes within `ReactiveTeleportRangePixels` (125) with the elite cooldown up (marking it), and Teleport elites are excluded from the sequential `SelectNextAttack` cycle (since 2026-10-09 the landing, like every Teleport's, stops short of a wall and needs floor under it). The **telegraph is dual-channel** (ruling #11): the executor lazily creates a `TelegraphGlyph` Node2D (since 2026-10-16 `FTT.Combat.TelegraphGlyph` under `scripts/Combat/`, moved from `FTT.Enemies` for the Ultimate telegraph and now the one statement of the three class colours, `BasicColor` / `GuardCrushColor` / `UnblockableColor`; open circle = Basic, diamond = Guard-Crush, X = Unblockable, `_Draw()` primitives) shown only during the telegraph and resolved from the SAME class flags as the tint, and the legacy scalar-melee fallback's tint moved from orange into the Basic white/yellow family (`EnemyAbilityExecutor.BasicTelegraph`). The **Restoration Font channel is range-anchored**: moving beyond `ChannelRangePixels` (90, export) interrupts and refunds the use.
- The Level 13 Mirror Paradox (Package 4 B6) deliberately bypasses `BossController`. Since Package 13 W6 its clone uses the Fighter activation-strike version of its Ultimate against the player (`VERIFY-STORY-MIRROR-HOLD`: the Story cinematic does not hold the player). `MirrorParadoxController` builds a clone of the session's locked character through `CharacterFactory.CreateCharacter(..., applyStoryProgression: false)` — normalized base resources only, no Resonance stats and no `StoryAbilityPerks` — gives it the authored 1000-HP pool through the new `PlayerController.EncounterMaxHPOverride`, and drives it with the real deterministic CPU engine. **Package 11 A7b changed both halves of that sentence** — see the F20 bullet above: the tier is the Story difficulty, the band is `CpuBandTuning.BossOverride`, Hard mirrors purchased grid perks, the Legacy ability gate is never installed, and the campaign damage multiplier is applied. `FighterCpuController` now reads a mode-neutral `CpuDecisionObservation` (raw `FP64` world-unit positions, still no Godot dependency), so `MirrorParadoxDecisionAdapter` can project Story state into the same decision table and hand the resulting `PlayerInputFrame` to the clone through `InputManager.SetInputSource`; nothing flows back into `scripts/FighterSim/`. `MirrorParadoxEncounterController` parallels `BossEncounterController` for the HUD bar, the **25**-dust award (F05 moved every boss from 50 to 25, and the Mirror has no wallet-direct path left), and rewind freeze. The `mirror_paradox` manifest row is registered (owner `FighterCpuController`); Level 13 authoring remains Package 5.
- **Never author an empty Script-typed array in a `.tres` or `.tscn`.** `Prop = Array[ExtResource("N")]([])`, where resource `N` is a `.cs` script, corrupts the Godot 4.7.1 .NET heap when the resource is marshalled to its C# array export. The process then dies at an unrelated site — a silent GdUnit child death (exit `-1073741819` / `-1073741795`), a hang, or `ResourceLoader.Load` returning null for an unrelated resource — so the crash never points at the cause. To express "no entries", omit the property and let the C# field default apply. Non-empty Script-typed arrays are fine, and so are empty arrays of builtin types (`Array[float]([])`, `Array[int]([])`). `tests/ContentValidation/ScriptTypedEmptyArrayTests.cs` scans the content directories and fails with the offending file and line. This cost a full debugging cycle in Package 4 B6; see that plan's Deviations section.
- The deterministic Fighter and local match foundations work, and **all ten stages are independently authored production-contract scenes** with distinct geometry, era presentation, pool configs, audio sets, preview plates, and deterministic hazard identities (Package 6, closed 2026-08-08); stage select routes each catalog entry to its own scene and shows its preview. Match flow is complete end to end: a deterministic 180-frame pre-match countdown, the five-second respawn-platform dissolve with input lock and a post-drop invulnerability window (three seconds until 2026-10-16; the respawn-protection sentence below), driver-side KO presentation (hit-freeze, slow motion, camera focus, stamp, winner pose, fanfare hook) that provably does not touch simulation state, an authored pause menu and results screen, controller-disconnect forced pause with reassignment, and all five end conditions. The CPU covers off-stage recovery, orb pursuit, and per-band hazard avoidance at its design difficulty matrices. The rollback-readiness gate passes across all ten stages (worst case 0.058 ms median / 0.104 ms p95 against an 8 ms budget, largest snapshot 2,699 bytes, zero breaches; re-measured on 2026-10-16 after this update's component 323 at 0.084 ms median / 0.168 ms p95, largest snapshot 4,473 bytes, zero breaches) — that is Package 7's entry criterion, and `docs/PERFORMANCE_BASELINE.md` carries the per-stage tables. **Respawn protection since 2026-10-16** (decision 4.2 with Q8 "Fighter only, both levers"; `USER-RESPAWN-PROTECTION`, `USER-RESPAWN-PROTECTION-FIGHTER-ONLY`, `PROVISIONAL-RESPAWN-PROTECTION-CURVE`): the flat `RespawnInvulnerabilityFrames` (180) is deleted; `FighterMatchFlowRules.RespawnInvulnerabilityFor(waited)` = max(60, 120 − 60 × clamp(waited − 30, 0, 270) / 270) grants 120 frames at the first legal drop, shrinking to 60 at the auto-drop, recorded by component 323's `ActionFlags` bit 0 `RespawnProtection`; the first swing, Special, Ultimate or grab ends it, while jumps, the roll, the air dodge, the movement ability, Echo Step, the ledge grab, the neutral climb and the ledge roll keep it (the ledge attack is a swing and ends it). The "Respawning" label and the spawn glow read the record (`FighterSimulationDriver.ShowsSpawnProtection`), so a roll, tech or Foresight grant no longer reads as respawning; two presentation-only limits remain (a late roll or air dodge, and a respawn grant shorter than 30 frames merged into a first ledge grab, keep the glow up a little longer). Story's post-rewind protection is untouched. The `Parallax2D` migration and per-stage era lighting landed with Package 8 (B7), along with the production `FighterHUD` scene that replaced the debug labels. Fighter matches now present combat, not just movement (2026-08-09): `FighterSimulationDriver.SyncPlayer` maps sim state onto the full placeholder animation set (attack poses per chain step, hitstun, dazed, block, jump/fall, run/idle) with sprite facing sync, keeps authored sprite frames advancing on a pausable presentation clock without the retired yellow melee debug rectangle, spawns floating damage numbers, shakes on blocked hits, and the glow arbiter runs `ProcessMode.Pausable` so hit flashes decay under the driver's disabled proxy subtrees (previously the first hit's red tint stuck for the whole match). What remains for these stages is production art, music and VFX assets (Package 10), cinematic KO presentation, Fighter-mode footsteps (the driver disables the presentation bodies' `ProcessMode`, so `PlayerController`'s footstep emission never runs in a match), and a human playthrough of all ten. All nine character kits (specials and movement) are converted to canonical mechanics in both modes with five authored construct scenes under `scenes/constructs/` and all 27 Resonance major perks wired Story-only; the 36-slot audit with per-slot approximations lives in `docs/PACKAGE3_KIT_AUDIT.md`. All nine ultimates are canonical multi-hit structures via the `FighterUltimateRules` dispatch (X7 closed; cinematic presentation is Package 8); rollback performance validation and presentation polish remain incomplete. `BaseSpecial` abilities should use `UseAuthoredPhaseFrames()` so `.tres` startup/active/recovery frames are authoritative rather than hardcoded `PhaseTimer` constants.
- Direct-IP LAN packets/rollback are implemented as a foundation, but **LAN is de-scoped to Package 7 as of 2026-08-26 (V7.3)**: the main menu's code-built LanButton was removed (`MainMenuSceneTests` pins its absence; `menu_lan_match` is a recorded translation orphan), while `NetworkSelectScreen`, `scenes/menus/NetworkSelect.tscn`, `scripts/Networking/`, and their tests all remain for the later package. Steam Networking Sockets, relay, public matchmaking, connection negotiation, state resync, and production online UI are absent.
- Secure **schema-v8** saves, confirmed slot deletion, localized slot summaries, puzzle completion persistence, and all nine V7.6 Resonance Grid resources are implemented. **All nine traversal flags are wired across all nine kits** (Package 11 A4), so the old "only Einstein's perks are wired" note is stale for that layer; bespoke Major execution beyond the eight A4 reworked is still per-kit work, as are complete hub stations/gates.
- The shared puzzle/environment toolkit, Story drop tables/pickups, world-aware rewind freeze/projectile clearing, the R03 Post-Landing Hold with 120-frame protection from the thaw, and Timeline Collapse restart choice are implemented with replaceable placeholders. Full level authoring and final rewind/drop presentation remain later-package work. The item-drop damage buff (Easy ×1.5 for 15 s, Normal ×1.25 for 10 s, none on Hard) reaches every Special, Ultimate, construct and zone tick exactly once since 2026-10-11 (the Story damage composition under Ability implementation, `FIX-STORY-DAMAGE-BUFF-KIT-ROUTING`), and `ChronalOrbItem`'s `SpeedBuff` / `DamageBoost` effects now grant that drop buff at the current Story difficulty through `ApplyStorySpeedBuff` / `ApplyStoryDamageBuff`, ignoring the orb's own value and duration; on Hard the orb is consumed with no effect and never cancels a running buff (`FIX-STORY-ORB-BUFF-CASES`). Nothing spawns a Story orb yet, and whether one belongs in Story at all is open (`PROVISIONAL-STORY-ABILITY-DAMAGE-ROUTING` (f)).
- **Localization is now gated in both directions** (Package 8 C1). Forward: `ScriptTranslationKeyTests` sweeps every `Tr("...")`/`TranslationServer.Translate("...")` literal under `scripts/`, and `SceneVisibleTextTests` refuses any visible `text` in an authored `.tscn` that is neither a real key nor an empty/glyph-only value. Reverse: `UnusedTranslationKeyTests` reports keys nothing under `scripts/`, `scenes/` or `resources/` references, failing on a new orphan while letting a recorded one be retired freely — twelve are on that roster today (ten pre-Package-8 keys, `menu_lan_match` for Package 7 and `speaker_cleopatra`; the Package 12 Phase C closeout deleted W5's four). All three resolve through the compiled `en.en.translation`, not the CSV.
- Stable-ID Story pools now cover placeholder projectiles/zones, the shared `standard_enemy`/`elite_enemy` tier pools, `enemy_projectile`, Dust/helper loot, placeholder VFX, and persistent constructs; production kit/boss/hazard scenes added later must enter these pools and preserve reset contracts. The P0 combat path runs on the physics frame clock with posed placeholder sheets — the placeholder combat animation library and its method-track callbacks were deleted by the 2026-10-04 feel pass (F2) — while production animation libraries and broader FSM/content coverage remain incomplete; a production animation library must be driven from that clock, never drive it.
- **Settings and input remapping are complete** (Package 8 A4, 2026-08-08): an authored `scenes/ui/Settings.tscn` with Audio/Display/Gameplay/Controls tabs, full InputMap rebinding (listen-for-input capture, one slot per device kind so a key remap never clears the joypad binding, conflict detection that blocks rather than swaps and names the owning action, per-action and global reset-to-default), and resolution/window-mode/VSync persistence applied at boot by `ViewportEnforcer`. Bindings live in the global payload as `InputBindingSet` (typed multi-event, physical keycodes), now at **schema v7** (the Package 12 bump added Block Mode and per-device stick profiles) and extended by Package 11's C01c work with `UnboundActions` and `ShortcutEnabled`; only actions that differ from `project.godot` are stored. `Actions.Ultimate` joined `RemappableActions` with Package 11 A8, and the Dash gesture no longer exists. Package 12 W6 added Block Mode Hold/Toggle, per-device left-stick deadzone and down-threshold sliders (`GlobalSaveData.StickProfiles`) and moved the Vibration rows onto the Controls tab.
- **The 2026-08-11 construct rebalance reworked the four attack-capable deployables** (Tesla coil, Leonardo turret, Cleopatra serpent nest, Pocahontas vine snare — the snare left the game with her in Package 13, leaving three) in both modes: damage halved and attack cadence halved in the four ability `.tres` (coil/turret 5 HP per 4 s, snare 8 per 1 s — the serpent nest is authored at the design's 6 HP per 1 s, `cleopatra/special_1.tres`; the Story coil fence went 4 HP per 1 s and the sim fence matched), `KnockbackForce` zeroed — construct hits are now impulse-free, and a zero knockback deliberately no longer replaces the victim's velocity anywhere (guards in `PlayerController`/`EnemyController`/`BossController` and a `knockback > 0` gate inside `FighterDamageRules.ApplyFighterHit`; hitstun still applies). Sim persistents spawn **bottom-anchored** (`Position.y = owner feet + HalfExtents.y`) — they used to sit half-buried at the floor line — and **basic swings now damage opposing constructs** via `ApplyConstructSwing`/`FlagConstructHitResolved` (previously nothing in the sim could reduce a construct's HP, so they were indestructible in Fighter matches). All four construct scenes carry an overhead `HPBar` ProgressBar driven by their nodes, and the driver's persistent proxies draw an HP mini-bar. `FighterConstructAttackTests` pins the anchor, attackability, and impulse-free stun; the kit/content suites' cadence windows and pins were retuned in the same pass.
- The Tutorial three-part flow, Florence four-room progression with gear puzzle and boss, hub Holodeck/NPC/Repository loop, resource-driven dialogue system, Story HUD, results overlay, and Story difficulty scaling are functionally complete with placeholder presentation. The tutorial's rewind calibration step is a **scripted demonstration** through `ChronalRewindManager.TriggerScriptedRewind()` (2026-08-09; the old gate waited on an event the calibration phase could never raise — a permanent soft-lock every playthrough hit). `ScriptedRewindTests` pins the demonstration path. The manual rewind, its scrub depth, the Stasis Echo and `ScriptedFreeRewind` are **all deleted** (Package 11 A2): the escape verb is Time Freeze, and Level 0's drill gets a ready freeze on every retry through `TimeFreezeController.DrillFreeFreeze` — the only recharge bypass in the game. The scripted death-rewind demonstration keeps `RefundRewind()`.
- **Levels 2–15 are done** (Package 5, 2026-08-08): every level is authored with room graph, checkpoints, the locked encounter economy, a mechanically-implemented era identity, a boss arena wired to the authored boss resource, extractors, and entrance/boss/exit dialogue. The enemy and boss roster is now instantiated across the campaign rather than only in Florence. All seventeen scenes carry a `StageAudioSet` driven by `StoryAudioDirector` (ambient ↔ combat on engaged enemies, climax on boss reveal). Still remaining: production character/environment/enemy/boss art, real music stems and VFX art, cinematic boss presentation (Package 10), per-room lighting beyond the two exemplar levels (2 and 6 — the other thirteen campaign scenes are unlit; the rig is the pattern), performance profiling (Package 9), exports, and platform validation. A human end-to-end playthrough of levels 0–15 at each difficulty (a Package 5 exit criterion the automated gate cannot discharge) has not been performed. Since 2026-10-12 every level is also laid out as encounter locks (Scene architecture), which moved many anchors, fonts and spawns; the per-level record is the ledger's `PROVISIONAL-LOCK-LEVEL-AUTHORING`.
- `.gitignore` excludes generated output, but legacy `.godot/` files are already tracked and still appear in Git status. Do not remove them from the index without explicit repository-cleanup authorization.
